# StoreQL — API Guide

> A business-capability tour of the entire API surface: what you can *do* with each part of it, not how it's implemented. Pair this with [README.md](../README.md) (the product tour these capabilities serve) and [docs/UI-GUIDE.md](UI-GUIDE.md) (the screens that call this API). For request/response schemas, data model, and event payload internals, read the actual code under `services/*/src/main/java/com/storeql/*/api/` and `dto/` — this guide intentionally stays at the capability level so it doesn't drift out of sync with the code.

Every call goes through one public entry point and lands on one of 12 independent business capabilities behind it. Nothing else is reachable from outside the platform.

## Health, on every service

Four endpoints, and which one a caller wants depends on what it would do with the answer.

- `GET /health/live` — is this process wedged? One check, no dependency, ever. **This is what a Kubernetes liveness probe calls**, because the only remedy for a failing liveness probe is to kill the container, and killing a healthy process because its database is slow turns a database problem into a restart storm.
- `GET /health/ready` — can it serve right now? The database and the Kafka consumers. Failing it takes the replica out of the load balancer and nothing else. The database answer comes from a background prober, so the probe never queues behind real traffic for a connection, and a pool that is merely **busy** keeps the replica in rotation — pulling a saturated replica moves its traffic to its neighbours and empties their pools next.
- `GET /health/started` — has it finished booting? Holds liveness off while the JVM and the inline Flyway migration start.
- `GET /health` — every check at once, the database included. For a dashboard or a person. **Never for a liveness probe.**

Probes are not published: through the gateway these need a token like any other path.

- `GET /admin/health` (PLATFORM_ADMIN, OWNER, MANAGER) — the deep check, and **no probe calls it**. Makes a real round trip to the database and times it, reports the Kafka consumers, prints the connection pool's own figures (`active`, `idle`, `total`, `max`, `waiting`) and what readiness is currently saying. The two failures that look identical from a probe and want opposite remedies are told apart here: a database that is down fails the round trip, while a pool that is empty shows a fast round trip with `active` at `max` and `waiting` above zero — the fix for which is a bigger pool or fewer callers, never a restart.

## The gateway (the one public door)

The gateway is the single public door to the whole platform — nothing else is internet-reachable. Every call comes in as `/api/v1/{service}/{path...}`. The unversioned `/api/{service}/{path...}` form is the deprecated alias of the current version (22.8): the gateway routes the two identically, and every answer on the alias — a refusal included — carries `Deprecation: @1790121600` (RFC 9745), `Sunset: Thu, 30 Sep 2027 00:00:00 GMT` (RFC 8594) and `Link: </api/v1>; rel="successor-version"`; from the sunset day it answers `410 API_VERSION_RETIRED`. A version nobody published is `404 API_VERSION_UNKNOWN` with a `Link` to the latest. `GET /api/versions` describes all of it with no credential — the current version, every version with its status and dates, the policy in a paragraph (breaking changes only as a new version; a version supported at least twelve months after its successor; additive changes within a version), and where a version's OpenAPI description is ([docs/API-VERSIONING.md](API-VERSIONING.md)). The gateway only proxies to the 12 declared business services (an explicit allowlist — `iam-svc, tenant-svc, product-svc, inventory-svc, pricing-svc, cart-svc, order-svc, payment-svc, purchase-svc, customer-svc, notification-svc, reporting-svc`); anything else, even if it happens to be registered in service discovery, is unreachable. It terminates and verifies the JWT, strips any client-supplied identity headers, and re-stamps `X-Tenant-Id` / `X-User-Id` / `X-User-Email` / `X-Roles` / `X-Store-Ids` / `X-Permissions` from the verified token so every business service can trust those headers unconditionally (`ProxyResource.FORWARDED_HEADERS` is the one list of what reaches a service — a header stamped but not listed is dropped, which is how `X-Store-Ids` went missing for as long as it did, SJ-D46); it also forwards `Idempotency-Key` and the real `Content-Type` (so binary uploads like product images pass through intact) untouched.

**Vulnerability reporting (RFC 9116):** `GET /.well-known/security.txt` is public on the gateway, and the web app's nginx passes the same path through, so a researcher finds it on whichever host they reach. It is written from configuration, never from defaults — `SECURITY_TXT_CONTACT` (comma-separated `mailto:`, `https:` or `tel:` URIs), `SECURITY_TXT_EXPIRES` (an ISO instant in the future and no more than 366 days out, as RFC 9116 §2.5.5 recommends), and optionally `SECURITY_TXT_POLICY`, `SECURITY_TXT_CANONICAL` (both `https:`) and `SECURITY_TXT_PREFERRED_LANGUAGES`. Missing, expired or malformed settings answer `404` and log why, rather than publishing an invented address or a stale file; a value containing a line break is refused, so settings cannot add fields. `Cache-Control: max-age=3600`. What happens to a report is in [SECURITY.md](../SECURITY.md).

Guest/customer storefront access is carved out by an explicit path whitelist keyed on an `X-Storefront-Tenant` header (catalog browse, price resolve, active promotions, per-store storefront config, stock availability, a store's delivery and collection windows (`GET /api/v1/order-svc/storefront/fulfilment-slots`), and — for a signed-in customer only — placing an order and paying online); everything else requires a verified staff or customer Bearer token. The routes that exist before anyone is signed in are matched exactly: sign-up, sign-in, refresh, the second-factor and single-sign-on steps, the token signing keys, and a forgotten password — `POST /api/v1/iam-svc/auth/password/forgot`, `POST …/auth/password/reset` and `GET …/auth/password-policy` (answers under `/auth/password/` are `Cache-Control: no-store`).

Protective features: Redis-backed rate limiting (default 100 req/min per caller, so it holds across gateway replicas), brute-force login lockout (5 failed attempts → 15-minute block on `/auth/login`), a **card-data guard** (every body and query string is scanned for a payment card number — 15/16/19 digits, Luhn-valid, an issuer range issued at that length — and one found is answered `400 CARD_DATA_NOT_ACCEPTED` without being forwarded, echoed or logged; EAN-13/GTIN-14 barcodes, IMEIs and all-digit ids are deliberately not cards; with the log scrubber in every service, this is what makes the PCI DSS SAQ-A scope a fact rather than a design intention), a **body-size cap** (1 MB on every route; only a route that takes a document is allowed more — `storeql.gateway.upload-routes`, today `/api/purchase-svc/e-invoices=21000000` and each network's delivery route beside it, `/api/purchase-svc/e-invoices/inbound/peppol`, `…/fr_pdp` and `…/simulated`, at the same, and `/api/payment-svc/admin/settlements=6000000` for an acquirer's settlement file (11.10); the list is `GatewayConfig`'s default and lives nowhere else, since a one-route copy in the packaged properties once shadowed it and held every network's delivery to 1 MB (SJ-D64) — matched on the normalised path exactly, so neither its sub-routes nor a `..` walk out of it take its cap; a body over its cap is refused `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` on its declared length before a byte is read, or counted up to the cap when it declares none), a deny-by-default CORS policy (only explicitly configured browser origins get CORS headers), a per-upstream circuit breaker (opens after 5 consecutive connect/read failures for 10s and fails fast with a clean 503 instead of piling up timeouts), connect/read timeouts (2s/10s, surfaced as 502/504 rather than hangs), and a tenant-status gate that polls tenant-svc's `storefront/active` flag (cached 15s, fails open) so a suspended tenant's storefront stops serving without the gateway holding its own tenant data.

**Errors are RFC 9457 problem details:** every error, from any service or the gateway itself, is `application/problem+json` — `type` (`urn:storeql:problem:CODE`), `title`, `status`, `detail`, `instance`, the stable `code`, `details`, `requestId`, and the legacy `error`/`meta` members clients read before. A response with data is the plain envelope. Every service's `/openapi` (and the gateway's) is OpenAPI 3.1 with the `Problem` schema.

**Script integrity on the web shell (PCI DSS v4.0.1 11.6.1; SAQ-A's condition that the site cannot be altered by scripts):** the web image lists every script it serves with a reason and a SHA-256 in `/script-inventory.json`, generated from the bundle at image build by `infra/script-inventory.sh` (a script without a reason fails the build). The gateway's `ScriptIntegrityMonitor` reads the shell's entry page and inventory on a schedule (`storeql.gateway.script-integrity.web-url`, `…interval-minutes`, default daily), reports an unlisted, changed or missing script as drift, logs it at ERROR and exposes it as the gauge `storeql_script_integrity_drift` (Prometheus rules `ScriptIntegrityDrift`, `ScriptIntegrityUnchecked`). `GET /admin/security/script-integrity` returns the last report and `POST /admin/security/script-integrity/check` runs one now — gateway-local routes, PLATFORM_ADMIN only (`403` otherwise; `409 SCRIPT_INTEGRITY_NOT_CONFIGURED` with no web url).

---

## Platform-wide conventions

These apply across (almost) every endpoint below and are called out per-service only where a service deviates from them:

- **Tenant scoping**: every request is scoped to the caller's tenant, taken from the gateway-verified identity (or, on public storefront paths, from the resolved storefront tenant) — never from a client-supplied body field.
- **Default-deny RBAC**: role model is `PLATFORM_ADMIN` (cross-tenant), `OWNER`, `MANAGER`, `STOREKEEPER`, `CASHIER`, `CUSTOMER`. The shared `AdminAuthorizationFilter` enforces four tiers: **(1)** most of the `/admin/...` subtree — every HTTP method, *including GETs* — requires a management role (`PLATFORM_ADMIN`/`OWNER`/`MANAGER`), with bootstrap/receipt exemptions (`POST /admin/tenant` during onboarding, `POST .../receipts`); **(2)** **staff-operable admin surfaces** require any staff role (`STOREKEEPER`/`CASHIER` included): `/admin/inventory/**` (warehouse), `/admin/cash/**` (till — resource layer may still demand MANAGER+ for close/drops), plus support GETs `GET /admin/tenant`, `GET /admin/tenant/obligations`, `GET /admin/tenant/retention` (the schedule the purging services read), `GET /admin/stores…`, `GET /admin/products/variants/resolve`; **(3)** `POST .../refunds` and `POST .../void` require a management role even outside `/admin/`; **(4)** any other mutating request needs at least a staff role, except a small open allowlist (identity endpoints, onboarding, `/prices/resolve*`, `/orders`, `/cart*`, `/payments/online`, `/inventory/reservations*`); **(5)** **reads default-deny too** — since SJ-D11 a non-`/admin` GET needs at least a staff role unless it is on the curated open-read allowlist (storefront catalogue and config, availability, promotions, own cart, own orders, `/auth/me`, onboarding status). A GET added tomorrow ships closed. *(This paragraph previously ended "Non-`/admin` GETs outside the staff-admin list are not gated by this filter", which stopped being true when reads were closed and was found still standing during a live verification pass.)*
- **Granular permissions and custom roles (20.10)**: the five roles stay as *tiers* — the filter above reads them, and nothing about that changed. What a tenant may now do is define a **role of its own that stands on a tier and holds fewer of that tier's permissions** — a shift lead who is a `MANAGER` in every way but cannot void a sale or post a journal, a trainee who is a `CASHIER` who cannot open the drawer. A custom role can only *narrow* its tier, never widen it, so nothing a tier refuses by path becomes reachable by naming a permission. The catalogue (`GET /admin/roles/permissions` on tenant-svc; `Permissions` in common-web) is thirteen decisions: `sales.void`, `sales.refund`, `till.no_sale`, `till.manage`, `stock.adjust`, `stock.transfer` (SJ-D73: raise, ship, receive and cancel transfer and move orders — a cashier could once move stock to another store), `purchasing.approve`, `purchasing.invoices.decide`, `finance.journal`, `finance.payments` (17.10: propose, approve and pay supplier payment runs, and change a supplier's bank details), `pricing.write`, `customers.privacy`, `staff.manage`. Defaults by tier: a manager holds all thirteen, a storekeeper `stock.adjust` and `stock.transfer`, a cashier `till.no_sale`, and both of those `purchasing.approve` as well — the spend ceiling in `storeql.purchase.approval.limits`, not the tier, decides who may approve how much, so the permission is what a custom role takes away and the ceiling is what it still has to clear (rejection needs neither, as before); an owner and the platform hold everything and **are never narrowed**. Each is checked at the one place the action happens with `TenantContext.requirePermission(code)`, *after* the tier gate, and refuses with `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` naming the permission. How it travels: iam-svc mints a `perms` claim for a login that holds any custom role (present and possibly empty — a role narrowed to nothing is a real value); the gateway strips any client-supplied `X-Permissions` and stamps the claim (`-` when empty); a service judges a caller by the header when it is present and by the tiers' defaults when it is absent, so a token minted before the claim existed, and every direct call in an integration test, behaves exactly as before. A token carries what it was minted with: a role redefined reaches its holders at their **next sign-in**.
- **Tenant defaults (SJ-D53)**: where a request may leave out a currency or country — a price list, a customer's VAT status, a supplier, store credit, a Z-report, a gift card, an order, a supplier CSV import — the tenant's own, as declared at onboarding, is used. It is read from tenant-svc, never assumed: the platform used to fill a gap with `GBP` (and `EUR`, and `GB`), which put a yen tenant's price lists and store credit in pounds without an error. When the tenant's profile cannot be read the write is refused with `503 TENANT_PROFILE_UNAVAILABLE` and nothing is stored; naming the currency or country explicitly needs no profile, but a named one must be a real ISO 4217 or ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code (`400 CURRENCY_INVALID`, `400 COUNTRY_INVALID`) — a blank one means the tenant's own. An event that describes money without its currency is treated as malformed by its consumers. **SJ-D54:** the same literals had stayed in the schema — thirteen `DEFAULT 'GBP'`, `'USD'` and `'GB'` column defaults across customer-, order-, payment-, pricing- and purchase-svc — and are dropped, so a write that ever leaves the column out fails instead of storing pounds.
- **Ids are RFC 9562 UUIDv7, in and out**: every id the platform returns is a UUIDv7, and every id it is given — in a path, a query, a header or a body — must be one, in canonical form (`01a0905d-7082-7518-9ec6-aee90d72a43e`; hex in either case). Any other version, the wrong variant or any other spelling is `400 INVALID_UUID` ("A request identifier is not a UUIDv7") before anything is looked up. The `Idempotency-Key` header, where a write takes one, must be a UUIDv7 as well — a new one for each attempt, the same one to retry it — or it is `400 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_INVALID`; so must the legacy `idempotencyKey` body field on the few writes that take one (orders, payments, payment intents, special orders). A key's case does not matter: `01A0…` and `01a0…` are the same key.
- **Pagination**: list endpoints are cursor-paginated — `?after=<meta.nextCursor>&limit=1-100` (default ~20).
- **Idempotency**: money- or stock-moving POSTs (place order, reserve/receive/adjust stock, record payment/refund/cash movement, goods receipt) accept an `Idempotency-Key` header so a retried POST is safe; placing an order requires one. Gift-card redemption has no key of its own and is instead idempotent on the order it is redeemed against. Together these are what let the POS replay a whole sale it captured offline.
- **Eventing**: domain events are written to each service's own outbox table in the same transaction as the state change and published to Kafka at-least-once; consumers dedupe on event id. No service calls another synchronously for these flows.
- **Tenant data export (21.14, EU Data Act ch.VI)**: every one of the twelve business services serves what it holds for the caller's business at `/admin/tenant-data`, to the **owner** alone (anyone else `403`), because it carries every staff member's and customer's records.
  - `GET /admin/tenant-data` — the manifest: each table in the order an import loads it (after the tables it refers to), with its columns (name, type, generated), key, `rows` and `checksum` (the md5 of its rows in key order without `tenant_id`, so an import into another business reads back the same figure), `derived` for projections rebuilt from events, `importSkippedReason` and `dependsOn`; and the register art.26 asks for: `excludedTables`, `excludedColumns` and `keptAtErasure`, each with its reason. The catalog is read from the service's own schema, so a new table or column is exported the day it is migrated; one the service cannot tie to a business, or an exclusion naming something the schema no longer has, fails the manifest with `500 TENANT_DATA_CATALOG_INCOMPLETE` naming it rather than serving an export that is quietly missing a table. Every service's integration tests check this.
  - `GET /admin/tenant-data/tables/{table}?after=&limit=` — the table's rows as JSON objects keyed by column, in key order (`limit` 1–1000, default 500); `meta.nextCursor` fetches the next page. `404 TENANT_DATA_TABLE_UNKNOWN` for a table not exported (the name is looked up in the catalog, never put into SQL); `400 TENANT_DATA_CURSOR_INVALID` for a cursor the pages did not give.
  - `POST /admin/tenant-data/tables/{table}` `{rows: [...]}` — loads a page, as it was served, into the caller's business, all or nothing: rows keep their ids and take the importing business as theirs. `409 TENANT_DATA_IMPORT_SKIPPED` for a table not imported (a business's own record, a notice to leave), `TENANT_DATA_IMPORT_CONFLICT` for a row already here (a page sent twice), `TENANT_DATA_IMPORT_MISSING_PARENT` for a row whose parent is not loaded yet, `TENANT_DATA_IMPORT_NOT_THIS_TENANT` for a child row of a parent the business does not have; `400 TENANT_DATA_IMPORT_INVALID` (not rows, or a value that does not fit), `TENANT_DATA_PAGE_TOO_LARGE` (over 1000).
  - Left out everywhere: `outbox`, `processed_events`, `flyway_schema_history`. Left out by name: credentials (a staff password hash, login and one-time codes, HMRC tokens, a fiscal device's signing key, device push tokens, unsubscribe tokens), delivery machinery (idempotency keys, payment provider webhooks) and platform reference data (allergens, units, the law as recorded). When tenant-svc says a business's retrieval period has ended (`TenantDataErasureDue`), each service erases every table the business's rows sit in, children first, in one transaction with a `TenantDataErased` announcing the counts.

## cart-svc

### Cart (`/cart`)
Server-side pre-checkout cart, keyed by cart id, session id, or the caller's authenticated identity. Note: every call requires a verified token (cart paths are not on the gateway's public storefront whitelist, and no anonymous/guest token exists), so a tokenless guest cannot reach this service — the current Flutter storefront keeps its pre-checkout cart on-device and never calls cart-svc. The session-cart + merge endpoints exist for authenticated clients that want a server-side cart.

- `POST /cart` — create or fetch the caller's active cart.
- `GET /cart` — view the cart and its line items (identified by cart id, session id, or the caller's authenticated identity).
- `POST /cart/items` — add an item to the cart (increments quantity if the variant is already in it).
- `PUT /cart/items/{itemId}` — change the quantity of a cart line.
- `DELETE /cart/items/{itemId}` — remove a line from the cart.
- `POST /cart/merge` — merge a session-keyed cart into the authenticated customer's cart (post-login merge).

**Business rules**
- Cart is not on the gateway's public storefront whitelist — every call needs a verified token.
- Once an order is placed, the cart is asynchronously marked checked-out (via `OrderPlaced`), not synchronously cleared by the checkout call. An **online** order closes the shopper's one active cart whichever store it went to — a delivery resolves to the store serving the postcode and may be split across several (order orchestration); a till sale naming a customer closes only a cart at its store.

**Events**
- Consumes: `OrderPlaced` (marks the cart checked-out), `StoreStatusChanged`, `TenantStatusChanged` (local suspension gate).
- Publishes: none.

---

## customer-svc

### Profile & Addresses (`/customers`)
Customer master data for the storefront and POS.

- `POST /customers` — register a new customer profile.
- `GET /customers?q=&after=&limit=` — list customers, newest first, cursor-paged; staff only. With `q`, only those whose first, last or full name, email or phone holds it, ignoring case — bound as a pattern with `%` and `_` matching themselves, never written into the SQL, and within the caller's tenant only. A phone-shaped `q` (digits with only spaces and `+ - ( ) . /` between them) holding four digits or more also matches the phone on its digits alone, both sides stripped of formatting and the digits bound, so `07700 900111`, `07700900111` and `7700 900111` all find `07700900111` (and a number stored spaced); a `q` with a letter in it is a name or an email and its digits are not searched alone. The same phone-shaped `q` is also read as a phone number **in the business's own countries** — its home country, then each of its stores' — and matched exactly against the stored E.164 form (`customers.phone_e164`, libphonenumber, the first *valid* reading wins), so a Polish shop's `512 345 678` finds `+48 512 345 678`, a British shop's `07400 123456` finds `+44 7400 123456` and an Indian shop's `98765 43210` finds `+91 98765 43210`; a number typed with `+` needs no country. No country or prefix is written in the code. Every match stays inside the caller's tenant (tenant first in the query, so another business's customer never matches, not even by an exact email). `q` is trimmed; blank or absent lists as before; over 100 characters is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. The cursor pages a search exactly as it pages the list, and an erased record is never found.
- `GET /customers/lookup?email=&phone=` — find a customer by email or phone (POS/CRM lookup); a phone is read in the business's own countries and matched on its E.164 form first, then exactly as typed.
- **Phones in E.164.** Every write of a customer's phone also stores `phoneE164` (on the answer; null when the number cannot be read in the business's countries, and the phone is kept as typed). A start-up backfill fills rows written before, a business at a time, and leaves a business whose countries cannot be read for a later start rather than marking its rows. A text to a customer goes to the E.164 form — customer-svc's privacy notices, and notification-svc's recall notice by text, which takes the order's number in international form (order-svc's `contactPhoneE164`, [a phone at the till](../intent/phone-at-the-till.md)) and, when the order's number cannot be texted, the customer's own `phoneE164`; a number kept only as typed is never guessed into one. Numbers are read by common-service's `PhoneNumbers`, shared with order-svc. `phone` stays what the person or staff typed, shown back as written and kept when it cannot be read in the business's countries; `phoneE164` is derived from it for matching and texting, never typed.
- `GET /customers/{id}` — get a customer profile.
- `PUT /customers/{id}` — update a customer profile.
- `DELETE /customers/{id}` — GDPR erasure/anonymize a customer (SJ-D43: also deletes their saved addresses and publishes `CustomerErased` so order-svc and notification-svc redact what they hold about them; the event names the login as well as the customer id, so the redaction reaches web orders — SJ-D44).
- `POST /customers/me` — the signed-in shopper's own record in this shop, created on first use from the token's login and email, or adopted from the unlinked record the till already held for that address (SJ-D44). Identity comes from the token only. order-svc calls this at checkout so an online order carries a customer id the shop can resolve; loyalty, the confirmation email and erasure all key on it.
- `GET /customers/me` — the same record, never created: 404 for a shopper who has not bought here.
- `PUT /customers/me` — **the shopper's own profile (12.10)**: name, phone, date of birth, gender and `preferredLanguage` (13.x: the ISO 639 code their messages are written in — stored lower-case; left out, it is kept; `""` clears it back to the shop's language; anything but two or three letters is `400`), found by the token's login; the email is the login's and is not writable here. `CUSTOMER_NOT_FOUND` (404) until the record is claimed. Free text is bounded (`@Size`: names 100, phone 32).
- `GET /customers/me/addresses` · `POST /customers/me/addresses` · `PUT /customers/me/addresses/{id}` · `DELETE /customers/me/addresses/{id}` — **the shopper's own address book (12.10)**, keyed on the token's login: another shopper's address id is `ADDRESS_NOT_FOUND` (404), not forbidden, and the same login at another shop has no book there. Marking one address default clears the previous default. At most **ten** per shopper — `CUSTOMER_ADDRESS_LIMIT` (409), decided under the customer's row lock so a race for the last place cannot overfill it. Lines are bounded at 120 characters, type and postcode at 20. Admitted by the shared filter by shape (`/customers/me/addresses` and one id-addressed child), so nothing else under `/customers/me/` is open by accident. Reachable from a storefront because the gateway's signed-in-customer allowlist (`JwtAuthFilter.isStorefrontCustomerAccount`) derives the tenant from `X-Storefront-Tenant` for exactly these shapes and methods — the first live run of the flow got `NO_TENANT` on `PUT /customers/me` before that list was taught them, which is the check to make whenever a shopper-facing route is added.
- `GET /customers/me/export` · `GET /customers/{id}/export` — UK GDPR art.20 data export, for the shopper and for staff handling a request that arrived by phone or letter. One JSON document: profile, addresses, loyalty and store credit with both ledgers, marketing preferences with their evidence trail, and every order (fetched from order-svc by both ids). Fails closed: an unreachable order-svc is a 503 `EXPORT_ORDERS_UNAVAILABLE` and no file, never a partial export.

### Marketing consent (`/customers/me/marketing`, `/customers/{id}/marketing`, `/marketing/unsubscribe`)

PECR reg.22/23 and UK GDPR art.7(1). `marketing_preferences` is what is true now; the append-only `marketing_consent_log` is the evidence — who, when, how (`SIGNUP`, `CHECKOUT`, `PREFERENCE_CENTRE`, `STAFF`, `UNSUBSCRIBE_LINK`, `IMPORT`, `PURPOSE_WITHDRAWN`) and the wording shown. Four channels (`EMAIL`, `SMS`, `PHONE`, `POST`); basis `CONSENT` or `SOFT_OPT_IN`, kept apart because the law keeps them apart. **Silence is not consent:** a channel with no row is refused.

**The Marketing purpose governs the channels.** Withdrawing the `MARKETING` purpose (a privacy choice, *withdraw all*, or staff acting for the customer) switches off every channel that is on, on the same transaction, each logged `PURPOSE_WITHDRAWN` — everywhere, whatever the country. A channel cannot be switched on while `MARKETING` stands withdrawn — `409 MARKETING_PURPOSE_NOT_GRANTED`, nothing in the request applied — nor, where the business trades under a per-purpose consent law (the DPDP obligation in force for it, from tenant-svc's jurisdictions), until `MARKETING` is granted; elsewhere a channel with no purpose answer stands on its own (channel consent, soft opt-in). Granting `MARKETING` never switches a channel on. `allowance` answers no under the same gate, re-checked at every send, and fails closed when the business's jurisdictions cannot be read. A start-up reconciliation switches off any channel still on beside a withdrawn purpose (idempotent). A customer created by staff with the marketing tick, in a business where the purpose must come first, is created without the channel.

- `GET /customers/me/marketing` · `PUT /customers/me/marketing` — the shopper's preference centre. Channels left out of a `PUT` are untouched. Body: `{ channels: [{ channel, granted, basis? }], notice? }`.
- `GET /customers/{id}/marketing` · `PUT /customers/{id}/marketing` — the staff view, and consent taken over the counter; the acting staff member is recorded.
- `GET /customers/{id}/marketing/allowance?channel=` — whether one marketing message may be sent. Asked by notification-svc before every `MARKETING` send; answers no for an unknown channel, no preference, an opt-out or an erased record, and returns the per-send `unsubscribeToken` the message must carry when the answer is yes.
- `POST /marketing/unsubscribe` — the opt-out link. Public and unauthenticated by design (the token is the capability); stops every channel unless one is named; a second click is not an error. The gateway locks an address out after five wrong tokens (`429 TOKEN_LOCKED`), the same way it does wrong passwords.
- `POST /customers/{id}/addresses` — add a delivery/billing address.
- `GET /customers/{id}/addresses` — list a customer's addresses.
- `PUT /customers/{id}/addresses/{addressId}` — update an address.
- `DELETE /customers/{id}/addresses/{addressId}` — remove an address.

### Loyalty (`/customers/{id}/loyalty`, `/customers/me/loyalty`)
- `GET /customers/{id}/loyalty` — the customer's points under the business's programme (13.x): `pointsBalance`, `lifetimePoints`, `tier` and `tierSince`, `qualifyingPoints` (lifetime, or the points earned within the programme's qualifying window), `multiplier` (what the tier earns per base point on a sale), `nextTier {name, threshold, pointsToGo}` (absent at the top), `expiringSoon {points, on}` (points dying within thirty days; absent when none) and `expiryMonths` (absent when points never expire). A customer who has never earned gets a zero balance on the programme's first tier.
- `GET /customers/me/loyalty` — the same for the signed-in shopper (`CUSTOMER`); `404 CUSTOMER_NOT_FOUND` before they have a record in this shop.
- `POST /customers/{id}/loyalty/earn` — manually credit loyalty points: a lot under the programme's expiry, the tier recomputed from what qualifies (a move is announced as `LoyaltyTierChanged`). No multiplier: the points typed are the points given.
- `POST /customers/{id}/loyalty/redeem` — redeem points (e.g. against a sale), **from the lot that dies first**, then the oldest; `422 LOYALTY_INSUFFICIENT_POINTS` when the balance is short.
- `POST /customers/{id}/loyalty/adjust` — management correction: an award is a lot like any earning and may move the tier; a deduction comes out of the lots that die first and never below zero.
- `GET /customers/{id}/loyalty/ledger` — loyalty transaction history: `EARN`, `REDEEM`, `ADJUST` and `EXPIRE` (the sweep's write-off, its reason naming the rule and the earliest earning it took).

### Loyalty programme (`/admin/loyalty`) — management only
- `GET /admin/loyalty/programme` — the programme in force: `tiers [{name, threshold, multiplier}]` (lowest first), `expiryMonths` (absent: points never expire), `qualifyingMonths` (absent: a lifetime counts), `reason`, `setBy`, `setAt`, and `isDefault` — true for the platform's default a business has never replaced (BRONZE 0 / SILVER 1,000 / GOLD 5,000 / PLATINUM 20,000 on lifetime points, every tier ×1, no expiry: exactly what loyalty did before 13.x).
- `PUT /admin/loyalty/programme` `{expiryMonths?, qualifyingMonths?, tiers: [{name, threshold, multiplier?}], reason}` — one to six tiers, the first at zero, ascending thresholds, each name distinct (capitals, digits, underscores), each multiplier 1–10 to three decimals (`400 LOYALTY_TIERS_INVALID`); `expiryMonths` 1–120, `qualifyingMonths` 1–36 (`400 LOYALTY_EXPIRY_INVALID`); a reason. **What it does to points already held:** every open lot takes the new expiry — the rule's own date, but **never sooner than thirty days from now** (a rule cannot take points overnight) — or none when the rule is lifted; every account is re-tiered quietly from what qualifies now (a business changing its ladder is not a customer's achievement, so no `LoyaltyTierChanged`).
- `POST /admin/loyalty/expiry/run` — run the sweep for this business now: every lot that has died is written off in one `EXPIRE` entry per customer and announced as `LoyaltyExpired {points, expiredAt}`; every account is re-tiered from what qualifies today, each fall or rise announced. Returns `{customers, points, retiered}`. The sweeper (`storeql.customer.loyalty.sweep-seconds`, hourly by default, 0 off) does the same for every business with a rule.

### Store Credit (`/customers/{id}/store-credit`)
- `GET /customers/{id}/store-credit` — view store-credit balance (per currency).
- `POST /customers/{id}/store-credit/issue` — issue store credit (e.g. for a return).
- `POST /customers/{id}/store-credit/redeem` — spend store credit against a sale.

**Business rules**
- GDPR erasure is restricted to `PLATFORM_ADMIN`/`OWNER`/`MANAGER` — deliberately stricter than the shared default, since this path isn't under `/admin/` and would otherwise be open to any staff role.
- Loyalty earn/redeem/adjust and store-credit issue/redeem are deliberately left open to any staff role for normal POS checkout use.
- Loyalty is auto-accrued once per order from the `OrderConfirmed` event (deduped by event id); guest orders (no customer id) earn nothing. **The accrual is the base points (a point per unit of order currency, `storeql.customer.loyalty.points-per-unit`) at the customer's tier multiplier**, rounded down, and the ledger reason says which tier and multiplier did it (`… · SILVER ×1.5`); the `LoyaltyEarned` event carries the points actually awarded.
- **Tiers (13.x)** come from `qualifyingPoints` — lifetime points, or the points earned within the programme's qualifying window — against the programme's thresholds; **points expire** as lots: every earning is a lot dying `expiryMonths` after it was earned, spending takes the lot that dies first, and the hourly sweep writes off what died. Points held before lots existed become one opening lot the first time the account is touched, dated from its last movement.

**Events**
- Consumes: `OrderConfirmed` (auto-accrues loyalty points).

- Publishes: `CustomerRegistered` (also for a record created by `POST /customers/me`), `LoyaltyEarned`, `LoyaltyRedeemed`, `LoyaltyAdjusted`, `LoyaltyExpired` (13.x: `{customerId, points, expiredAt}` — purchase-svc releases the deferred income the points carried as breakage), `LoyaltyTierChanged` (13.x: `{customerId, fromTier, toTier, qualifyingPoints}`, up or down; nothing consumes it yet), `StoreCreditIssued`, `StoreCreditRedeemed`, `CustomerErased` (SJ-D43; ids only — `customerId` and, when linked, `loginId` — no email/phone, since the event outlives the erasure it announces).
- Calls: order-svc `GET /orders/export` (the sales half of a data export; fail-closed).

---

## iam-svc

**Password policy (NIST SP 800-63B-4, OWASP ASVS 5.0 V6):** a password that is the only factor is at least 15 characters (`400 PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT`) and at most 128 (`PASSWORD_TOO_LONG`), any character including spaces, no composition rules, no expiry; it must not be, or contain, the login (`PASSWORD_IS_IDENTITY`); and it is screened against known breaches through the Pwned Passwords range lookup — only the first five characters of the SHA-1 leave the service (`PASSWORD_BREACHED`; a screen that cannot be reached lets the password through and logs the miss). The same policy holds at `POST /auth/register`, `POST /auth/admin/staff-users`, platform-admin creation, `PUT /auth/change-password` and `POST /auth/password/reset`. It is published at `GET /auth/password-policy` (public, no token) → `{minLength, maxLength, breachScreened, mustNotContainLogin}`, so every form that sets a password shows the rules before anything is typed. Config: `storeql.iam.password.min-length`, `max-length`, `breach-check.enabled|url|timeout-ms`. Below eight characters bean validation answers first (`VALIDATION_FAILED`).

### Authentication (`/auth`)
- `POST /auth/admin/staff-users` — find-or-create a staff account by email (a management action; the store-level role is granted separately via tenant-svc).
- `GET /auth/admin/staff-users?ids=<uuid>,<uuid>` (OWNER, MANAGER, PLATFORM_ADMIN acting for the business) — staff by name, not by id: `[{userId, email}]` for the caller's business's staff among the ids, ordered by email. Another business's staff, customers, a login provisioned but not yet assigned, and ids nobody holds are left out, never refused one by one. `ids` is comma-separated, blanks and repeats ignored; at most 100 (`400 STAFF_IDS_TOO_MANY`, counted before any is read), each a UUIDv7 (`400 INVALID_UUID` refuses the whole request); none asked is `[]`. A cashier, a storekeeper or a shopper is `403`; a platform administrator acting for no business is `401 NO_TENANT`. What the back office's staff list, audit trail and staff reports show in place of a user id. **Held to stores:** a caller assigned to stores (a manager of one branch) is answered only staff who hold a role at one of those stores or a business-wide role (the owner) — the rest are left out, never refused one by one; the owner, a business-wide manager and the platform administrator are answered every staff member among the ids. tenant-svc's `GET /admin/staff` follows the same rule.
- `POST /auth/register` — self-register a new customer account.
- `POST /auth/login` — tenant staff / customer login.
- `POST /auth/platform-login` — platform-console login, kept deliberately separate so a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` credential never works on a store/POS login screen and vice versa.
- `POST /auth/refresh` — exchange a refresh token for a new access token.
- `POST /auth/logout` — revoke a refresh token.
- `POST /auth/login`, `POST /auth/platform-login` — when the login holds a second factor (20.12) the answer is `200 { mfaRequired: true, mfaToken, mfaMethods }` and **no tokens**; when it must have one and has none, `{ mfaEnrolmentRequired: true, accessToken }` where the token (scope `mfa-enrol`, ten minutes, no role or tenant) reaches only `/auth/mfa/**`.
- `POST /auth/mfa/login` — public: answer a waiting sign-in — `{ mfaToken, method: TOTP | RECOVERY_CODE | PASSKEY, code | assertion }` → the token pair (`amr`: `pwd` + `otp` or `hwk`). Five wrong answers end the wait (`401 MFA_CHALLENGE_EXPIRED`); twenty in fifteen minutes over all of a login's sign-ins lock its second factor (`429 MFA_LOCKED`).
- `POST /auth/mfa/login/passkey-options` — public: the WebAuthn challenge for a waiting sign-in.
- `GET /auth/mfa` — the caller's own factors: `totp`, `passkeys[]`, `recoveryCodesLeft`, `required`.
- `POST /auth/mfa/totp` → `{ secret, otpauthUri }` (pending) · `POST /auth/mfa/totp/confirm { code }` → `{ recoveryCodes?, tokens? }` (recovery codes with the first factor; the real session when called with an enrolment token) · `POST /auth/mfa/totp/remove { password }`.
- `POST /auth/mfa/passkeys/options` → creation options + `registrationToken` · `POST /auth/mfa/passkeys { registrationToken, name, clientDataJson, attestationObject }` · `POST /auth/mfa/passkeys/{id}/remove { password }`.
- `POST /auth/mfa/recovery-codes { password }` — ten new codes; the old ones stop working.
- `GET /auth/admin/mfa-policy` (OWNER, MANAGER) · `PUT /auth/admin/mfa-policy { requiredTiers: [OWNER|MANAGER|STOREKEEPER|CASHIER] }` (OWNER) — who must have a second factor. Removing the last factor is then `409 MFA_REQUIRED_BY_POLICY`, and a password-only session is not renewed (`401 MFA_REQUIRED`).
- `POST /auth/sso/start { slug, codeChallenge, returnTo? }` — public: start a sign-in through the business whose sign-in name is `slug` (20.x, OpenID Connect). `codeChallenge` is S256 of a verifier the app keeps; `returnTo` must be on one of `storeql.sso.return-origins`, with no fragment (`400 SSO_RETURN_REFUSED`). → `{ authorizationUrl }`: send the browser there. `404 SSO_NOT_FOUND` for a name nobody uses or a provider switched off; `409 SSO_NOT_READY` without a client secret; `502 SSO_PROVIDER_*` when the provider cannot be used; `503 SSO_UNAVAILABLE` on a deployment with no `storeql.sso.callback-url`.
- `GET /auth/sso/callback?code&state` — public: the redirect URI a business registers with its provider. Always `303` to the app: `#sso_ticket=…` when the person was matched to a login of that business, `#sso_error=CODE` when not (`SSO_NO_ACCOUNT`, `SSO_EMAIL_UNVERIFIED`, `SSO_EMAIL_MISSING`, `SSO_ALREADY_LINKED`, `SSO_CANCELLED`, `SSO_STATE_INVALID`, `SSO_ID_TOKEN_INVALID`, `SSO_PROVIDER_REFUSED`, `SSO_PROVIDER_UNREACHABLE`, `TENANT_INACTIVE`). A state works once.
- `POST /auth/sso/token { ticket, codeVerifier }` — public: trade the ticket and the verifier the start was made with for exactly what `/auth/login` answers — the token pair (`amr`: `sso`, or `sso` + `mfa` when the provider says it asked for two factors), or `mfaRequired` / `mfaEnrolmentRequired`. A ticket is spent by its first redemption, right or wrong (`401 SSO_TICKET_INVALID`), and lasts two minutes.
- `GET /auth/admin/sso` (OWNER, MANAGER) · `PUT /auth/admin/sso { slug, issuer, clientId, clientSecret?, enabled, requiredTiers: [MANAGER|STOREKEEPER|CASHIER], requireVerifiedEmail? }` (OWNER) · `DELETE /auth/admin/sso` (OWNER) — the business's identity provider. The secret is written and never read back (`clientSecretSet`); the answer carries the `callbackUrl` to register. `OWNER` in `requiredTiers` is `400 SSO_OWNER_NOT_REQUIRABLE`; another business's name `409 SSO_SLUG_TAKEN`. A required tier's password is refused `403 SSO_REQUIRED` with `details: ["slug=…"]`, and its password sessions are not renewed; a provider session is renewed for `storeql.sso.session-max-seconds` (a working day), then `401 SSO_REAUTH_REQUIRED`. Changing the issuer unlinks everybody linked at the old one.
- `GET /auth/admin/sso/readiness` (OWNER, MANAGER) — `CALLBACK_CONFIGURED`, `CONNECTION_SAVED`, `SECRET_HELD`, `DISCOVERY_READ`, `KEYS_READ`, `ENABLED`, each with what to do; the provider is read now.
- `GET /auth/admin/sso/identities?after=&limit=` (OWNER, MANAGER) · `DELETE /auth/admin/sso/identities/{id}` (OWNER) — who is linked to which of the provider's people; unlinking matches that login by email again at its next sign-in.
- **API keys (22.7):** `POST /auth/admin/api-keys { name, role: MANAGER|STOREKEEPER|CASHIER, storeIds?, expiresAt?, sandbox? }` (OWNER) → `201` with the key itself, once — `sqk_` and forty random characters; only its SHA-256 is kept, and the first twelve characters as `prefix`. With `sandbox: true` (22.8) the key starts `sqk_test_` (forty-nine characters), acts in the business's sandbox alone and is marked `sandbox: true` on the list; `404 SANDBOX_NOT_FOUND` when the business has none. From inside the sandbox every key minted is a sandbox key, and the list is the live business's whole list. `GET /auth/admin/api-keys?after=&limit=` (OWNER, MANAGER) — in the order made, revoked ones marked, never the key. `DELETE /auth/admin/api-keys/{id}` (OWNER) — `404 API_KEY_NOT_FOUND`; twice, `409 API_KEY_REVOKED`. Refused: `400 API_KEY_ROLE_INVALID` (never OWNER, never the platform), `API_KEY_NAME_INVALID`, `API_KEY_EXPIRY_PAST`, `INVALID_UUID`. `POST /platform/api-keys/introspect { key }` (PLATFORM_ADMIN: the gateway's question) → `{active, keyId, tenantId, roles, storeIds, name}` or `{active: false, reason: unknown|revoked|expired|tenant suspended}`; a use is kept on the key to the minute. At the gateway a key is presented as `Authorization: Bearer sqk_…`: it acts as the business in its tier and stores, stamped as the actor (`X-User-Id` is the key's id, `X-Auth-Methods: api-key`) and counted against the plan's rate; it never reaches iam-svc, onboarding or a service's `/platform` routes (`403 API_KEY_ROUTE_FORBIDDEN`); an unknown, revoked or expired key is `401`, one of a suspended business `403 TENANT_INACTIVE`, and when iam-svc cannot be asked `503 API_KEY_CHECK_UNAVAILABLE` with `Retry-After`. The gateway keeps a verdict ten seconds, so a revocation lands within ten seconds.
- `DELETE /auth/admin/staff-users/{userId}/mfa` — the lost phone: OWNER for their own staff, PLATFORM_ADMIN for anybody, never oneself (`400 MFA_RESET_SELF`); ends the person's sessions.
- `GET /auth/.well-known/jwks.json` — public: the token signing keys' public halves (RFC 7517), `Cache-Control: max-age=300`. Access tokens are RS256 and carry the `kid` of the key that signed them; the gateway and the MQTT broker verify against this set.
- `GET /auth/admin/signing-keys` — PLATFORM_ADMIN: the signing key register (kid, status `ACTIVE`/`RETIRING`/`RETIRED`, dates) — never key material.
- `POST /auth/admin/signing-keys/rotate` — PLATFORM_ADMIN: mint a new signing key now; the old one keeps verifying until the tokens it signed have expired.
- `PUT /auth/change-password` — change the caller's own password.
- **A forgotten password** ([intent](../intent/password-reset.md)): `POST /auth/password/forgot {email, language?}` — public. Always `202 {accepted: true}`, the same for a known address, an unknown one, a suspended login and a request over the limit, and the same work either way (a token is minted and hashed). Every login using the address — a shopper login and staff logins at several businesses — that could sign in gets its own link: 256 random bits, only the hash kept (`password_reset_tokens`), good for 30 minutes (`storeql.iam.password-reset.ttl-minutes`) and once; a newer request replaces the older links. The platform administrator gets none; a staff login whose business signs its tier in through its identity provider is named with no link (`STAFF_SSO`: sign in through the business). At most three accepted requests an hour per address (`storeql.iam.password-reset.max-per-hour`, counted by a SHA-256 of the address, never the address). One `PasswordResetRequested` per request through the outbox, belonging to no business: the address, `language` (`[a-z]{2,3}` or null), `expiresAt`, and `entries[]` of `{kind: SHOPPER|STAFF|STAFF_SSO, userId, businessName?, link?}`; the link is `storeql.platform.web-url` + `/#/reset-password/<token>`. `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for a missing or malformed address.
- `POST /auth/password/reset {token, newPassword}` — public. `200 {reset: true}` and **no tokens**: the person signs in as usual, and a login with a second factor is still asked for it. On one transaction: the token spent, the login's other unspent tokens replaced, the password replaced, every refresh token of the login revoked, `PASSWORD_RESET` audited; other logins with the same address untouched. `400 PASSWORD_RESET_TOKEN_INVALID` for a token that is unknown, expired, used or replaced, or whose login can no longer sign in (suspended, its business suspended, single sign-on now required) — one code, so the page cannot tell which. The policy's own codes for the new password, checked before the token is spent, so a refused password leaves the link usable. `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` without a token or a password.
- `POST /auth/delete-account` — a customer deletes their own login (SJ-D43; requires the password again). Refused (403) for a staff account — that's removed by the business that employs its holder, not self-service.

- **Into the sandbox (22.8):** `POST /auth/sandbox/token` (OWNER, from the live business) → `{ accessToken, tokenType, expiresInSeconds, tenantId }`: a token naming the sandbox as its tenant, an owner there, `amr: ["sandbox"]`, no refresh token — the sandbox session lasts one access token and is re-entered from the live one. `404 SANDBOX_NOT_FOUND` when the business has none; `409 SANDBOX_NESTED` from inside a sandbox; a manager `403`.

### Bootstrap (`/bootstrap`)
- `POST /bootstrap/admin` — one-time creation of the first `PLATFORM_ADMIN` for a fresh deployment (refuses once one already exists; not JWT-gated by necessity).

### Identity (`/auth/me`)
- `GET /auth/me` — the caller's own identity, roles, **permissions** (20.10: the token's own claim when a custom role narrowed it, else the roles' defaults; an owner holds every permission) and profile, as verified by the gateway (the gateway forwards the token's identity, never the token).
- **The permission claim.** A login that holds any custom role carries `perms` in its access token: the union, over its assignments, of the custom role's permissions where there is one and the tier's defaults where there is not. A login with no custom role carries no claim and is judged by its tiers' defaults. An owner never carries one. Consumed events: `StaffAssigned` (now with `roleCode`, `permissions` and `roleUpdatedAt` beside the tier), `RoleDefined` (rewrites every assignment made through the role, **only forward** by the role's `updatedAt` — SJ-D52 found three redefinitions in one second applied out of order, and the outbox now also keys records by aggregate — so the next login carries the newest set), and `StaffRemoved` — **SJ-D51**: removing an assignment in tenant-svc used to stop at tenant-svc's table, and the role stayed on the login for good; a cashier taken off a store could sign in as its cashier the next morning. It is unbound now, and when the last staff role at the tenant goes the login is unbound from the tenant too (a shopper's token that still named a tenant would make the gateway record their next order at another shop against the business that let them go).

### POS Sessions (`/auth/pos/sessions`)
- `POST /auth/pos/sessions` — start a cashier POS session. `409 POS_STORE_HAS_NO_TILL` at a dark store (ship-from-store and dark-store picking): iam-svc keeps each store's type (`store_types`) from tenant-svc's `StoreStatusChanged`, as it keeps the status; a store whose type was never announced is a shop.
- `PUT /auth/pos/sessions/{id}/activity` — heartbeat a session to keep it alive.
- `DELETE /auth/pos/sessions/{id}` — end a POS session.
- `GET /auth/pos/sessions` — list active POS sessions.
- `POST /auth/pos/sessions/sweep` — force-expire sessions idle past their configured timeout and revoke their tokens (platform-wide maintenance sweep).

**Business rules**
- The bootstrap admin endpoint is a self-disabling one-shot: it refuses if any `PLATFORM_ADMIN` already exists, so it's safe to leave enabled.
- The idle-session sweep ignores tenant scope by design (it's a platform-wide op), so it's restricted to `PLATFORM_ADMIN`.
- Provisioning a staff user only mints the identity; the actual store role is granted asynchronously when tenant-svc publishes `StaffAssigned`.

**Events**
- Consumes: `TenantCreated`, `StaffAssigned` (grants the store role), `StoreStatusChanged`, `TenantStatusChanged`.
- Publishes: `UserRegistered`, `AccountDeleted` (SJ-D43; id only, on self-service account deletion).

---

## inventory-svc

> **Store scope (SJ-D74).** A login assigned to a store, and an API key minted for one, are held to it on every inventory route: a `store`/`storeId` query parameter and a body's `storeId`, `fromStoreId` or `items[].storeId` outside the caller's stores is `403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED` (`StoreScopeFilter`, once at the door); a batch, transfer or move order named by id is checked against the store it belongs to in the resource; a transfer is raised and shipped by the sending store's keeper and received by the receiving store's. A stock read that names no store reads the caller's one store, or asks a caller of several to name one (`400 STORE_REQUIRED`); a caller assigned to no store (owner, whole-business manager) reads every store. Raising, shipping, receiving, picking and cancelling transfer and move orders needs `stock.transfer` (SJ-D73), held by storekeepers and managers by default.

Nineteen resource classes covering the full stock/warehouse operations surface, grouped below by business sub-domain. All endpoints sit under `/admin/inventory` unless noted.

### Core Stock Operations
- `POST /admin/inventory/receive` — receive stock into a store/zone (manual goods-in), optionally against a batch number and expiry date. Takes `dutyStatus` (`DUTY_PAID`, the default, or `DUTY_SUSPENDED` for excise goods arriving into bond; `400 INVENTORY_DUTY_STATUS_INVALID`); duty-suspended stock goes only into a store approved as a bonded warehouse (`400 INVENTORY_STORE_NOT_BONDED`). Every batch answer carries `dutyStatus`; a level carries `inBond` (on hand, never available — `available = onHand − inBond − reserved`); a hold never draws a duty-suspended batch and a sale never deducts one. The valuation values bonded stock at cost without the duty and reports `dutySuspendedQty` and `dutyPotential` (the duty it would crystallise at the current rates) beside it.
- **Consignment stock ownership.** `POST /admin/inventory/receive` takes `ownership` (`OWNED`, the default, or `CONSIGNMENT`) and, for consignment, `supplierId` — stock the supplier still owns until it sells (`400 INVENTORY_OWNERSHIP_INVALID`, `400 INVENTORY_CONSIGNMENT_SUPPLIER_REQUIRED`). A `GoodsReceived` event carrying `ownership: CONSIGNMENT` and `supplierId` (a receipt against a consignment purchase order) makes the same batch. Every batch answer carries `ownership` and `ownerSupplierId`; ownership rides with the stock through a transfer and a lot split. A **sale** drawn from a consignment batch (a hold consumed at checkout, or a till sale deducted on `OrderFulfilled`) publishes **`ConsignmentStockSold`** `{storeId, variantId, batchId, supplierId, orderId, qty, unitCost}` on the deduction's own transaction — one per consignment batch drawn, at the batch's cost, which is the order's price — and purchase-svc owes the supplier for it.
- `POST /admin/inventory/receive/batch` — receive multiple lines in one call (partial success per line).
- `POST /admin/inventory/adjust` — adjust on-hand quantity up or down with a reason (shrinkage, damage, etc.).
- `GET /admin/inventory/levels` — on-hand stock levels per store/variant.
- `GET /admin/inventory/levels/summary` — aggregate KPI (total SKUs, low-stock count).
- `GET /admin/inventory/batches` — list stock batches/lots (filter by store, variant, material status).
- `GET /admin/inventory/batches/{id}` — get a batch.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/batches/{id}/material-status` — change a batch's QC/material status (e.g. quarantine → released).
- `GET /admin/inventory/movements` — stock movement/transaction history.
- `POST /admin/inventory/movements/purge` — bulk-delete movement history older than a given date (housekeeping).

### Lots, Serials & Batch Genealogy
- `POST /admin/inventory/lot-genealogy` — link a parent batch to a child batch (e.g. repack/kitting).
- `GET /admin/inventory/lot-genealogy/batch/{batchId}/ancestors` / `.../descendants` / `.../links` — trace a batch's lineage.
- `POST /admin/inventory/lots/split` — split a batch into two (spin off a partial quantity).
- `POST /admin/inventory/lots/merge` — merge one batch's quantity into another.
- `GET /admin/inventory/lots/{batchId}/actions` — audit history of split/merge actions on a batch.
- `GET /admin/inventory/batches/expiring` — batches expiring within N days (default 30) for a store. Since SJ-D71 this includes stock that arrived by transfer, move or return, which used to arrive with no date.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/batches/{id}/grade` — set a batch's quality grade.
- `POST /admin/inventory/serials/register` — register serial numbers against a received batch (explicit list or auto-generated with a prefix).
- `GET /admin/inventory/serials` — list serial numbers (by store/variant/status).
- `GET /admin/inventory/serials/lookup?serial_no=` — find a serial by number.
- `GET /admin/inventory/serials/{id}` — get a serial's detail.
- `GET /admin/inventory/serials/{id}/history` — movement history for one serial.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/serials/{id}/status` — change a serial's status (sold, defective, in-stock, etc.).

### Cycle Counts & Physical Inventory
- `POST /admin/inventory/cycle-counts` — start a cycle count for a store, targeting selected ABC classes with a tolerance %.
- `GET /admin/inventory/cycle-counts` / `.../{id}` — list/get cycle counts and their lines.
- `POST /admin/inventory/cycle-counts/{id}/lines/{lineId}/count` — enter a counted quantity for one line.
- `POST /admin/inventory/cycle-counts/{id}/approve` — approve the count (auto-approves lines within tolerance, flags the rest for review).
- `POST /admin/inventory/cycle-counts/{id}/adjust` — post stock adjustments for the approved variances.
- `POST /admin/inventory/physical-inventories` — start a full physical inventory count for a store.
- `GET /admin/inventory/physical-inventories` / `.../{id}` — list/get physical inventories with their count tags.
- `POST /admin/inventory/physical-inventories/{id}/tags` — add a count tag (system-quantity snapshot) for a variant/zone.
- `POST /admin/inventory/physical-inventories/{id}/tags/{tagId}/count` — record the physically-counted quantity for a tag.
- `POST /admin/inventory/physical-inventories/{id}/complete` — close out the physical inventory.

### ABC Analysis
- `POST /admin/inventory/abc/compile` — run an ABC classification pass for a store (by revenue/velocity criteria and A/AB thresholds).
- `GET /admin/inventory/abc/assignments` / `.../{storeId}/{variantId}` — list/get variant → ABC class assignments.

### Planning, Reorder & Demand
- `POST /admin/inventory/thresholds` / `GET .../thresholds` — set/list a reorder threshold + max qty per store/variant.
- `POST /admin/inventory/planning/run` — run the min/max replenishment plan for a store, generating suggestions.
- `GET /admin/inventory/planning/suggestions` / `PUT .../{id}/status` — list/resolve replenishment suggestions.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/rop-plans` / `GET` / `GET .../by-variant` — set/list/get reorder-point & EOQ parameters (lead time, ordering/holding cost, unit cost).
- `POST /admin/inventory/rop-plans/compute` — recompute reorder points/EOQ for a store. **Expected daily demand comes from the demand forecast when one exists** (the forecast's points over the plan's lead time, 06.x) and from the monthly demand-history average, as before, when it does not; a plan with neither is left as it is.
- `POST /admin/inventory/forecasts/run` `{storeId, variantId?, horizonDays?}` — **the statistical demand forecast (06.x).** Folds yesterday's sales into the daily buckets, then forecasts every variant with history at the store (or one) over the horizon (1–365 days, 28 by default; `400 FORECAST_HORIZON_INVALID`), replacing earlier forecasts. The method is chosen by the shape of the demand as the replenishment literature classifies it (Syntetos–Boylan): **SES** — exponential smoothing with a day-of-week profile once there are four weeks — for a steady seller; **CROSTON_SBA** — Croston's method with the Syntetos–Boylan correction — when demand falls on fewer than three days in four; **MEAN** under fourteen days of history. The smoothing constant is chosen on a hold-out of the last quarter of the history (a week to four), and the same hold-out gives each forecast its own report on itself: `mape` (over hold-out days with demand), `bias` (forecast minus actual as a percentage of actual; negative is under-forecast) and `mase` (against a naive one-day-back forecast; under 1 beats it), each **null rather than zero** when it cannot be honestly computed. Returns `{variants, byMethod, meanMape, horizonDays, fresh}`. **Fresh items** (06.x): each forecast carries what the item's dated batches say — `shelfLifeDays` (the median from receipt to expiry), `fresh` (fourteen days or fewer), `wasteRatePct` (past-date stock plus `EXPIRY` write-offs over what sold plus that, null when neither) and `maxCoverDays` (the shelf life, the longest cover an order should get); a fresh item's level follows its last eight weeks rather than six months. purchase-svc's order proposal caps a line's cover and EOQ to what sells within the shelf life and says so on the line. **Promotions and seasons (06.x):** a run reads two years of daily buckets and asks pricing-svc for the store's promotion windows (`GET /admin/promotions/windows`, below; a pricing-svc that cannot be asked means a forecast with no promotions in it, logged). Once thirteen months have been seen, each forecast carries `seasonalIndices` — twelve monthly indices, January first, from the days no promotion ran, clamped 0.25–4 and normalised — and its level is fitted on the deseasonalised history, each day ahead forecast at its month's index. A promotion's days are taken out of the history at the `uplift` (promoted-day demand over ordinary, measured on the item's own promoted days — `upliftSource: ITEM`, a week of them and four of ordinary ones — or pooled across the store's — `STORE`, four weeks of promoted days; null when no lift shows) and put back into the `promotedAheadDays` a promotion will run on; `promotedHistoryDays` says how many history days one ran on. The hold-out is judged on real demand, so knowing about a promotion is rewarded only if it sold. The run's result gains `seasonal` and `promoted` counts. Any staff tier under `/admin/inventory`; a store-scoped caller is held to their stores.
- `GET /admin/inventory/forecasts?store=&variant=&limit=` — the store's forecasts, newest run first, each with `method`, `intermittent`, `alpha`, `level`, `weekdayProfile`, `historyDays`, `next7`, `next28` and the accuracy figures; a caller of one store reads it without naming it, a caller of several names one (`400 STORE_REQUIRED`). `GET /admin/inventory/forecasts/{storeId}/{variantId}` — one forecast with its daily `points` (`404 FORECAST_NOT_FOUND` before a run).
- `PUT /admin/inventory/rop-plans/{id}/order-modifiers` — set min/max order qty and lot-multiplier constraints on a ROP plan.
- `POST /admin/inventory/safety-stock` / `GET` / `GET .../{storeId}/{variantId}` — set/list/get safety-stock parameters (method, lead time, service level).
- `POST /admin/inventory/safety-stock/compute` — recompute safety-stock levels.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/lots/{batchId}/uom-conversions` / `GET` — define/list lot-specific unit-of-measure conversions.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/par-levels` / `GET` — set/list PAR (periodic automatic replenishment) levels.
- `POST /admin/inventory/demand/aggregate` — aggregate historical sales movements into demand buckets (day/week/month).
- `GET /admin/inventory/demand/history` — list demand-history buckets.

### Transfers & Move Orders
- `POST /admin/inventory/transfers` — create an inter-store transfer order.
- `GET /admin/inventory/transfers` / `.../{id}` — list/get transfer orders with lines.
- `POST /admin/inventory/transfers/{id}/ship` — mark a transfer shipped from the source store. Stock is drawn FEFO from the source batches; a `DIRECT` transfer receives it at the destination in the same call, **one batch per source batch, carrying its lot, use-by date, cost and grade** (SJ-D71), each linked to its source in `lot_genealogy` (`SPLIT`, notes `TRANSFER_ORDER <id>`); a source with no supplier lot arrives under `TO-<ref>`, its date and cost still carried. So the expiring view, a recall on the lot (`IN_SCOPE`, not `LOT_UNKNOWN`) and gross margin all see moved stock as what it is.
- `POST /admin/inventory/transfers/{id}/receive` — receive an `INTRANSIT` transfer at the destination store: what arrives is read back from the `TRANSFER` movements the shipment left at the source, so it carries the same lots, dates and costs as a direct transfer; a shipment the ledger does not account for arrives as one `TO-<ref>` batch.
- `POST /admin/inventory/transfers/{id}/cancel` — cancel a transfer order: a released `PENDING` one, or discard a proposed `DRAFT` (`422 TRANSFER_ORDER_NOT_CANCELLABLE` otherwise).
- `POST /admin/inventory/transfers/{id}/release` (`stock.transfer`, the sending store's) — release a transfer a warehouse's replenishment run proposed: `DRAFT` → `PENDING`, for the warehouse to ship (`409 INVENTORY_TRANSFER_NOT_DRAFT`). A transfer says its `source` (`MANUAL` or `PROPOSAL`) and `proposalRunId`; a proposed line its `reason`. `TransferOrderShipped` and `TransferOrderReceived` carry their `lines` (variant, qty) and both stores.
- `POST /admin/inventory/move-orders` — create an intra-store zone-to-zone move order.
- `GET /admin/inventory/move-orders` / `.../{id}` — list/get move orders with lines.
- `POST /admin/inventory/move-orders/{id}/pick` — pick/complete a move order: drawn FEFO and put down one batch per source batch with its lot, date, cost and grade and a genealogy link (`MOVE_ORDER <id>`); `MO-<ref>` only for stock with no lot.
- `POST /admin/inventory/move-orders/{id}/cancel` — cancel a move order.

### Reservations (`/inventory/reservations`)
Called by order-svc during checkout to hold stock before it's actually deducted.
- `POST /inventory/reservations` — place a time-limited stock hold for an order line.
- **Dropship (stock the business never holds).** A variant purchase-svc sources from a supplier per order (`VariantSourcingChanged`, kept in inventory-svc's `variant_sourcing` projection) is available with nothing on the shelf: `GET /inventory/availability` answers `inStock: true, dropship: true`; `POST /inventory/reservations` places a hold with `fulfilment: DROPSHIP` that checks no stock and draws none, and consuming it moves nothing; a fulfilled sale of it deducts nothing (its revenue is still recorded). Stocked variants are `fulfilment: STOCK` and behave as before.
- `GET /inventory/reservations` / `.../{id}` — list/get reservations.
- `POST /inventory/reservations/{id}/consume` — convert a hold into an actual deduction.
- `POST /inventory/reservations/{id}/release` — release a hold back to available stock.
- `POST /inventory/reservations/batch` — reserve stock for multiple lines in one call (partial success reported).

### Kanban Replenishment
- `POST /admin/inventory/kanban-cards` — create a kanban card for a store/variant (optionally sourced from another store).
- `GET /admin/inventory/kanban-cards` / `.../{id}` — list/get kanban cards.
- `POST /admin/inventory/kanban-cards/{id}/trigger` — trigger a card's replenishment signal.
- `POST /admin/inventory/kanban-cards/{id}/replenish` — mark a triggered card replenished.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/kanban-cards/{id}/order-modifiers` — set order-modifier constraints on a card.

### Costing & Accounting Periods
- `PUT /admin/inventory/costing-methods` / `GET` / `GET .../by-variant` — set/list/get the costing method (e.g. FIFO/average) per store/variant.
- `POST /admin/inventory/accounting-periods` — open an inventory accounting period.
- `GET /admin/inventory/accounting-periods` / `.../{id}` — list/get accounting periods.
- `POST /admin/inventory/accounting-periods/{id}/close` — close a period (locks it from further postings).

### Reference Data & Picking Rules
- `POST /admin/inventory/reason-codes` / `GET` / `.../{id}/activate|deactivate` — manage stock-adjustment reason codes.
- `POST /admin/inventory/source-types` / `GET` / `.../{id}/activate|deactivate` — manage movement source types.
- `PUT /admin/inventory/zone-gl-mappings` / `GET` — map a store/zone to a general-ledger nominal code.
- `POST /admin/inventory/picking-rules` / `GET` / `.../{id}` / `DELETE .../{id}` — define/list/get/deactivate picking strategy rules (e.g. FEFO/FIFO).
- `PUT /admin/inventory/picking-rules/{id}/zone-priorities` / `GET` — set/list the zone pick-priority order for a rule.
- `POST /admin/inventory/picking-rule-assignments` / `GET` / `DELETE .../{id}` — assign/list/remove a picking rule's scope (store/category/variant).
- `GET /admin/inventory/picking-rules/resolve` — resolve which picking rule/zone order applies to a given store + variant.

### Reports (`/admin/inventory/reports`)
These are answered from inventory-svc's own tables rather than from reporting-svc, whose projections carry neither the reason code, the actor, nor any cost. See [reporting-api-gap-analysis.md](reporting-api-gap-analysis.md) for why each landed where it did.
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/low-stock?storeId&limit` — items below their **own** reorder level, live, with `signal` naming which of threshold / safety stock / reorder point bound the row. Items that have run out entirely are included: they have no batch rows at all, and they are the most urgent line in the report. **Item lifecycle:** variants of a product the catalogue has discontinued or delisted are left out, however low they run, as they are from `POST /admin/inventory/planning/run`; a line reinstated or launched comes back.
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/valuation?storeId&groupBy=STORE|VARIANT&limit` — what the holding is worth on its configured FIFO or AVERAGE basis. Stock carrying no cost comes back as `unvaluedQty` rather than valued at zero, which would understate a balance-sheet figure. `value` is the **business's own** holding: consignment stock, the supplier's until it sells, is not its asset and comes back apart as `consignmentQty` and `consignmentValue` (at the cost the supplier will be owed).
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/shrinkage?storeId&from&to&groupBy=REASON|ACTOR|STORE` — write-offs over a period. Losses and finds stay on separate columns: a store that wrote off 100 units and found 100 others is not one that did nothing. `.../shrinkage/by-variant` drills a summary row down to what was actually written off.
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/stock-turn?from&to&storeId&groupBy=STORE|VARIANT&limit` — cost of goods sold over a window against the average value held to produce it, plus `turnoverRatio` and `daysOnHand`. **`from` and `to` are required** — a turnover ratio has no meaning without a window, and `daysOnHand` divides by its length; both take a full ISO-8601 instant, not a bare date. COGS is taken from the cost price of the batches each sale actually drew down, so a historical window is answered with the costs of the day. Two fields say when a figure should not be trusted: `historyComplete` is false when the movement ledger was purged past the window's start, making opening values a floor; `uncostedSaleQty` reports quantity sold out of batches with no cost price, excluded from COGS rather than costed at zero. `turnoverRatio` and `daysOnHand` are **null**, not zero, when there was no stock to turn.
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/gross-margin?from&to&storeId&groupBy=STORE|VARIANT&limit` — gross margin and GMROI (19.7): what the sales in the window earned, net of VAT, discounts and returns, against the cost of the batches they drew down. `revenue`, `cogs`, `grossMargin`, `marginPercent` (of revenue), `averageValue` (the same average holding stock turn reports), `gmroi` (margin ÷ average holding over the window) and `annualisedGmroi`, lowest margin first. Revenue arrives on each `OrderFulfilled` line as `netAmount` and is stored beside the movement it paid for, in the same transaction and under the same per-line dedupe; a return takes back its share of the line's revenue and the cost of the units, never more than the line has unreturned. Money keeps the scale it arrived in — revenue the currency's, cost the cost price's. Voided sales earn nothing. `unpricedSaleQty` counts quantity sold with no revenue recorded (sales from before orders carried it, or a line whose figure was malformed): their cost stays in COGS, so the margin is understated rather than invented. `marginPercent` is null when nothing was earned and `gmroi` when nothing was held. Management roles only, like every report under this path; `from`/`to` required.
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/dead-stock?storeId&asOf&groupBy=BUCKET|STORE|VARIANT&limit` — stock on hand aged into the 0-30 / 31-60 / 61-90 / 91-180 / 180+ ladder. Age runs from the last **sale** of that item at that store, not from receipt: stock that arrived two years ago and sold this morning is not dead. Where nothing has ever sold, age runs from the oldest remaining batch's arrival and `neverSold` says so. `asOf` pins the instant ages are measured from, so a re-run against the same date gives the same answer.
- `GET /admin/inventory/reports/shelf-gaps?storeId&limit` — **what it would take to fill the shelves (07.17)**. Deliberately separate from low-stock: that report answers whether the business will run out, which is the warehouse's question, while this answers whether the bay looks full, which is the shop floor's — and the two differ by exactly the shelf, because 20 units is plenty for a bay holding 12 and a gap in one holding 60. Capacity comes from product-svc's published planograms, projected here from `ShelfCapacityPublished` (never read across the boundary), and is **summed across fixtures**: a line sited on a gondola and an end cap has two bays to fill. `available` is on hand less what is held for an order — the same definition the levels list uses, so two screens never disagree about one number — and `gap` is capacity less available, floored at zero. `belowMinimum` flags a bay under its **merchandising** minimum (`minPresentation`), which is a different number from a stock minimum and the reason the report exists. A line with a shelf and no stock at all still appears: an empty bay is the case the report is for, and it has no batch rows. Deepest gap first. **The one report the shop floor reads:** OWNER, MANAGER and STOREKEEPER (a storekeeper at their own stores only, `403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED` elsewhere); a CASHIER is `403`; another business's store id answers no rows. Every other report stays management only, and shelving and range stay with management in product-svc.

### Food Safety Checks (`/admin/inventory/food-safety` staff · `/admin/food-safety` management)
Temperature monitoring (the cold chain) and HACCP due-diligence records, on one model: a temperature reading is a check with a number and limits, an opening check is one with a yes or no. Legal basis (England): Food Safety and Hygiene (England) Regulations 2013 Sch.4 (chilled ≤ 8 °C, hot-held ≥ 63 °C), Reg (EC) 852/2004 art.5 (HACCP-based procedures), Food Safety Act 1990 s.21 (these records are the due-diligence defence).

*Store staff* (any staff role, by path — a storekeeper or a deli cashier takes the reading):
- `GET /admin/inventory/food-safety/points?storeId&includeInactive` — a store's monitoring points, each with its check type, limits, last check, `dueStatus` (`OK`, `DUE` for the last quarter of the interval, `OVERDUE`) and `openFailures` (failures with no corrective action).
- `GET /admin/inventory/food-safety/check-types` — the platform's reference types (chilled/frozen storage, hot holding, cooking core, chilled/frozen delivery, opening/closing checks, cleaning, pest check) and the tenant's own. `statutory` is true only where the limit is law rather than FSA guidance.
- `POST /admin/inventory/food-safety/records` — record a check: `value` (°C) for a temperature check, `passed` for a pass/fail check, never both. **The server decides the result** and stores the limits it judged against, so a later change to a limit never rewrites a past record. A reading is judged at the precision it is stored at: more than two decimal places is refused (`FOOD_SAFETY_TOO_PRECISE`) rather than rounded, because `NUMERIC(6,2)` would otherwise store 8.004 as 8.00 on a record judged a failure against 8.00. Honours `Idempotency-Key` (201 first time, 200 with the same record on replay). A store-restricted caller may only record at their own stores (`STORE_ACCESS_DENIED`). A switched-off point takes no checks (`FOOD_SAFETY_POINT_INACTIVE`).
- `GET /admin/inventory/food-safety/records?storeId&pointId&result&openOnly&from&to&after&limit` — the diary, newest first, cursor-paginated; `from` inclusive, `to` exclusive.
- `GET /admin/inventory/food-safety/records/{id}` — one record with its corrective actions.
- `POST /admin/inventory/food-safety/records/{id}/corrective-actions` — what was done (`action`) and what happened to the food (`foodDisposition`: `NONE|MOVED|DISCARDED|REHEATED|RECOOKED|OTHER`). A failure stays open until one is recorded; more than one may be. 409 `FOOD_SAFETY_RECORD_PASSED` on a check that passed.

*Management* (under `/admin/` outside the warehouse subtree, so gated to OWNER/MANAGER by path):
- `POST /admin/food-safety/points` · `PUT /admin/food-safety/points/{id}` — a chiller, freezer, hot cabinet, goods-in bay or checklist at a store. Limits default to the check type's and **may only be stricter** — 422 `FOOD_SAFETY_LIMIT_LAXER_THAN_TYPE` for a chiller set to 10 °C when the law says 8. A check is judged against the tighter of the point's limits and its type's current ones, so tightening a type later still binds older points. Store and check type are fixed once created.
- `POST /admin/food-safety/points/{id}/deactivate` · `/activate` — with a required `reason`, kept in an append-only trail; 409 `FOOD_SAFETY_ALREADY_IN_STATE`.
- `POST /admin/food-safety/check-types` · `PUT /admin/food-safety/check-types/{id}` — the tenant's own types. Platform types are read-only (409 `FOOD_SAFETY_TYPE_READ_ONLY`), and a tenant's own type is never marked statutory.
- `POST /admin/food-safety/reviews` · `GET /admin/food-safety/reviews?storeId&after&limit` — a manager's periodic sign-off (the HACCP verification step), storing the record, failure and open-failure counts as they stood when signed.

Records, corrective actions, reviews and point switches are append-only. A background sweep (every 15 minutes, `storeql.inventory.food-safety.overdue-sweeper.*`) raises `FoodSafetyCheckOverdue` once per missed due time.

### Withdrawals and Recalls (`/admin/inventory/recalls` staff · `/admin/recalls` management)
A withdrawal takes stock off sale; a recall also tells the customers who may have bought it. Legal basis: Reg (EC) 178/2002 art.19 (retained), and the FSA's guidance on traceability, withdrawals and recalls. A recall's scope is one or more lines, each a variant optionally narrowed to a lot (`batchNo`) and a best-before or use-by range; a line naming neither covers every pack.

*Management* (gated to OWNER/MANAGER by path):
- `POST /admin/recalls` — open one: `reference` (the notice's, unique per tenant, case-insensitive — 409 `RECALL_REFERENCE_TAKEN`, so a retried open never opens a second recall), `kind` (`WITHDRAWAL|RECALL`), `hazard`, `reason`, `customerNotice` (**required for a RECALL**, 400 `RECALL_NOTICE_REQUIRED`), `source` (`SUPPLIER|FSA|FSS|INTERNAL|OTHER`) and `sourceReference`, and `items` (1 to 100 lines). A RECALL also carries what it offers its buyers (05.10, GPSR arts.35–37): `remedies` (one or more of `REPAIR|REPLACEMENT|REFUND`, 400 `RECALL_REMEDIES_REQUIRED` without), `contactPhone` and/or `contactUrl` (an http(s) address; 400 `RECALL_CONTACT_REQUIRED` / `RECALL_CONTACT_URL_INVALID`), `singleRemedyReason` and `soldFrom` (ISO date; buyers of sales from that day are looked for, every sale of the packs in scope without it).
