Where GPSR's recall-notice rule binds any country the business trades in (`GPSR_RECALL_NOTICE`, via `Jurisdictions.inForceWhereTrading`), at least two remedies are required unless `singleRemedyReason` says why one (400 `RECALL_REMEDIES_INSUFFICIENT`), and a `customerNotice` that plays the risk down — *voluntary*, *precautionary*, *discretionary*, *in rare situations*, *no reported accidents* — is refused (400 `RECALL_NOTICE_MINIMISES_RISK`, the phrase in `error.details`). **Every batch in scope, at every store, is set to `RECALLED` in the same transaction**, so no sale or reservation can draw on it between the recall existing and the stock being held. A batch whose lot or date is not known is held too, as `LOT_UNKNOWN` or `DATE_UNKNOWN`: a pack nobody can rule out is withdrawn. A batch number this service writes itself (`ADJ`, `CC-…`, `MO-…`, `TO-…`, `RET-…`) names a movement, not a lot, and counts as unknown. Publishes `RecallOpened` naming the stores whose stock was held. **The sales that drew on the packs in scope are found in the same transaction** — every `SALE` movement of a scoped variant, live or archived, classified by its batch's lot and date exactly as the held batches are, so a sale from a pack with no lot is reached too — and kept in `recall_sales` (append-only). The response and the list carry `ordersAffected` and `qtySold`. A RECALL publishes `RecallSaleAffected` per order (keyed by the order: the recall's reference, hazard, reason, notice, remedies, contact, and the order's lines with lot, date, quantity and match), which order-svc turns into the notice to the buyer; a WITHDRAWAL records the sales and announces nobody.
- `POST /admin/recalls/{id}/close` — with optional close-out `notes`. **Refused with 409 `RECALL_STORES_OUTSTANDING` while any store still holds recalled stock with no final disposition**; `error.details` lists those store ids.
- `POST /admin/recalls/{id}/cancel` — a recall opened in error, with a required `reason`. Puts its stock back to the status it had before any recall held it, unless another open recall still holds the batch. 409 `RECALL_ALREADY_ACTIONED` once any store has returned or destroyed stock under it — that stock has left the books.

*Store staff* (any staff role, by path):
- `GET /admin/inventory/recalls?status&after&limit` — newest first, with each recall's scope-line count, stores affected, stores still to act and quantity held.
- `GET /admin/inventory/recalls/active` — every scope line of every open recall. **The till keeps this list and checks each item against it**, so a scan never waits on the network.
- `GET /admin/inventory/recalls/{id}` — scope, every batch held (with `match`, quantity at quarantine, remaining, `quarantinedOn` `OPEN|ARRIVAL`, and any release), every store action, and per-store progress.
- `POST /admin/inventory/recalls/{id}/stores/{storeId}/actions` — what a store found and did: `qtyFound`, `disposition` (`HELD_FOR_COLLECTION|RETURNED_TO_SUPPLIER|DESTROYED`), `noticeDisplayed`, `notes`. The response carries `systemQty`, what the system held off sale there, kept beside the count. **`RETURNED_TO_SUPPLIER` or `DESTROYED` takes the store's held stock off the books** — an `ADJUST` movement per batch with reference `RECALL` and reason `RECALL_WITHDRAWAL`, attributed to the caller, and `StockAdjusted` per variant. A store-restricted caller acts only at their own stores (`STORE_ACCESS_DENIED`).
- `POST /admin/inventory/recalls/{id}/batches/{batchId}/release` — a pack checked and found not to be affected, with a required `reason`. Only an uncertain match can be released: 409 `RECALL_BATCH_IN_SCOPE` for a batch whose lot and dates are in scope, `RECALL_BATCH_ALREADY_RELEASED` for a second release.

**Stock that arrives under an open recall is held as it arrives.** Every batch insert — a delivery, a transfer, a return, a count, an adjustment — checks the open recalls for its variant in the same transaction, so a delivery of the recalled lot the next day is never on sale for a moment; the receive response already says `RECALLED`. Scope lines, held batches, releases and store actions are append-only; only the recall's own status changes.

### Public Storefront Availability (`/inventory/availability`)
- `GET /inventory/availability` — public in-stock (yes/no) flag per variant for a store. Each entry also says `dropship` — true when the supplier ships it per order, so it is available with none on the shelf — and `onlyLeft`: the whole number of units available at the named store when it is at or below the business's own threshold, else null (no threshold set, above it, out of stock, dropship, no store named, or a quantity that is not whole — weighed goods never get a count). No quantity above the threshold is ever exposed.
- `GET /admin/inventory/storefront-settings` (any staff of the business) · `PUT {lowStockThreshold: 1..1000 | null}` (OWNER, or a MANAGER held to no store — a business-wide setting; everyone else `403`) — the "Only N left" threshold, off until set, null switches it off again. `400 INVENTORY_LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD_INVALID` outside 1–1000. One row per business (`storefront_stock_settings`); another business's setting is neither readable nor writable.

**Business rules**
- `Idempotency-Key` is honored on receive/adjust/reserve so a retried POS or integration call can't double-move stock.
- Reservations follow hold → consume (fulfilled) or release (cancelled/expired); this is the checkout-time hold order-svc takes before stock is truly deducted.
- Transfer orders (inter-store) run create → ship → receive/cancel; move orders (intra-store, zone-to-zone) run create → pick → cancel.
- Cycle counts auto-approve variances within a configurable tolerance %; anything outside tolerance is flagged for manual adjustment.
- Kanban cards and ROP/EOQ plans both carry shared order-modifier fields (min/max order qty, lot multiplier) that constrain any suggested replenishment quantity.
- Public storefront endpoints expose only availability, never on-hand quantities.

**Events**
- Publishes (consignment): `ConsignmentStockSold` — a sale drawn from a supplier's consignment batch, consumed by purchase-svc.
- Consumes (dropship): `VariantSourcingChanged` (from purchase-svc) — how a variant is fulfilled from now on, STOCK or DROPSHIP.
- Consumes: `GoodsReceived` (from purchase-svc), `OrderConfirmed` (an online order for pickup or delivery waits to be picked at its store from the event's `occurredAt`, the moment order-svc confirmed it; on its own consumer group, started at the latest offset, so a deployment replays no history), `OrderFulfilled` (now with each line's `outstandingQty` and the order's `status`: the waiting line is set to the outstanding figure, a `FULFILLED` order leaves the list for good, and a line a wave already picked is deducted only for the rest), `OrderReturned`, `OrderCancelled`, `OrderVoided` (from order-svc — deduct / restock / release hold / put back a voided till sale). A voided sale is received back as a `RECEIVE` movement with reference type `VOID` — distinct from a return's `RETURN` — because a sale voided after the money is taken and a customer return are different loss-prevention signals. It is received rather than reversed because the void and the fulfil arrive on different topics in either order, and a receipt and a deduction net to the same stock whichever lands first. Stock turn, gross margin, demand history and dead stock all exclude a sale whose order was voided. None of them nets it: the receipt goes into a new return batch, stock turn sums per batch, and a netted sale would leave its cost in one group and a negative sale in another. An `OrderVoided` from before this change has no lines and is skipped. A return or a void comes back **under the lot it was sold from** (SJ-D71): the sale's `SALE` movements say which batches the order drew, each takes back its share less what earlier returns already put back on it, as a child batch carrying that lot's date and cost; what the draws cannot account for comes back as `RET-<ref>`, as before.
- Publishes: `StockReceived`, `StockReserved`, `StockReleased`, `StockDeducted`, `StockAdjusted`, `StockBelowThreshold`, `ReplenishmentSuggested`, `ReplenishmentResolved`, `SerialsRegistered`, `SerialStatusChanged`, `MaterialStatusChanged`, `TransferOrderShipped`, `TransferOrderReceived`, `TransferOrderCancelled`, `MoveOrderCompleted`, `MoveOrderCancelled`, `CycleCountAdjusted`, `PhysicalInventoryCreated`, `PhysicalInventoryCompleted`, `CostingMethodUpdated`, `AccountingPeriodOpened`, `AccountingPeriodClosed`, `KanbanTriggered`, `KanbanReplenished`, `KanbanCreated`, `RopPlanUpdated`, `RopComputed`, `LotSplit`, `LotMerge`, `FoodSafetyCheckFailed` (in the same transaction as the failing record; ids, reading and limits only), `FoodSafetyCheckOverdue`, `RecallOpened` (reference, kind, hazard and the ids of the stores whose stock was held; never the free-text reason or notice), `RecallSaleAffected` (05.10: one per order a RECALL reached — the notice and the offer travel with it, because the buyer is told what the recall said when it opened).

---

### Privacy under India's DPDP Act (`/customers/privacy`, `/customers/me/privacy`, `/customers/{id}/privacy`)
**13.12.** Consent, export and erasure were built to UK GDPR and PECR; India's Digital Personal Data Protection Act 2023 and the DPDP Rules 2025 (notified 13 November 2025; substantive duties from 13 May 2027, the register's `DPDP` obligation for `IN`) ask for more of the same shape, and it is offered to every business and enforced as the Act asks where it binds (`dpdp` true on the notice below). Everything a person is shown when they agree is kept with the consent; the log is append-only.
- `GET /customers/privacy/notice?language=` — **the notice, public**: read before signing up, resolved to the shop by the storefront header. In the language asked for when the business has published one, else in English, else `notice: null`; with `languages` (English and the twenty-two of the Eighth Schedule, ISO 639 codes, each `published` or not — `400 PRIVACY_LANGUAGE_UNKNOWN` for any other), the four `purposes` consent is asked for (`LOYALTY`; and `MARKETING`, `PERSONALISATION`, `ANALYTICS`, which `tracking` marks as never a child's), the grievance contact and `responseDays`, and `dpdp`/`dpdpFrom`. Fulfilling what was bought needs no consent (s.7(a)) and is stated, not asked.
- `GET /customers/privacy/settings` · `PUT /customers/privacy/settings` `{grievanceName, grievanceEmail, grievancePhone, grievanceAddress, responseDays}` — the business contact a person puts questions and grievances to (s.8(9), r.9) and the days it gives itself to answer a request: 1 to 90 (`400 PRIVACY_RESPONSE_DAYS_INVALID`, r.14(3)); `400 PRIVACY_GRIEVANCE_CONTACT_INVALID`. Owners and managers.
- `GET /customers/privacy/notices` · `POST /customers/privacy/notices` `{language, title, body}` → `201` — the current version per language, and a new version every publish (a consent names the version the person read; `409 PRIVACY_NOTICE_BUSY` for two publishes at once; `400 PRIVACY_NOTICE_TEXT_INVALID`). Owners and managers.
- `GET /customers/me/privacy` — the shopper's consents, purpose by purpose, against the notice version read; `child` (under eighteen by the date of birth held, s.2(f)), `guardian` (a standing guardian's consent, or null) and `canTrack` (false for a child with none).
- `PUT /customers/me/privacy/consents` `{choices:[{purpose, granted}], language}` — free, specific, informed, unconditional and unambiguous (s.6): each purpose on its own, against the notice in the language read. `400 PRIVACY_PURPOSE_UNKNOWN`, `PRIVACY_CHOICES_REQUIRED`; `409 PRIVACY_GUARDIAN_CONSENT_REQUIRED` for a child's tracking purpose without a guardian (s.9(3): no tracking, behavioural monitoring or targeted advertising at a child); `409 PRIVACY_NOTICE_REQUIRED` where the Act binds and no notice is published (consent is informed only against one, s.6(1), r.3). `DELETE /customers/me/privacy/consents` — every consent withdrawn in one call: as easy as giving it (s.6(4)). Staff take consent over the counter with `PUT /customers/{id}/privacy/consents` (recorded as the staff member's act, `source: STAFF`), read a customer's with `GET /customers/{id}/privacy`, and the evidence with `GET /customers/{id}/privacy/log` (`customers.privacy`).
- `POST /customers/{id}/privacy/guardian` `{guardianName, verification, reference}` · `DELETE` — a parent's or guardian's consent for a child, with how the parent's identity and age were verified (r.10: `DETAILS_HELD`, `DOCUMENT_SEEN`, `DIGITAL_LOCKER`); the reference notes what was seen, never a document's number (`400 PRIVACY_REFERENCE_IS_A_NUMBER`); `409 PRIVACY_NOT_A_CHILD` for a person of age. Withdrawn, the child's tracking consents fall with it, each withdrawal logged (`source: GUARDIAN`); `404 PRIVACY_GUARDIAN_CONSENT_NOT_FOUND`. Owners and managers.
- `GET /customers/me/privacy/requests` · `POST /customers/me/privacy/requests` `{kind, detail, nomineeName, nomineeContact}` → `201` — `ACCESS`, `CORRECTION`, `ERASURE`, `NOMINATION` (names who may act for the person, s.14: `400 PRIVACY_NOMINEE_REQUIRED`) or `GRIEVANCE` (ss.11–14), each `dueOn` the day the published period runs out and `overdue` past it; `400 PRIVACY_REQUEST_KIND_UNKNOWN`; `409 PRIVACY_REQUESTS_OPEN_LIMIT` at twenty open. `GET /customers/privacy/requests?status=&limit=` — the queue (`OPEN` soonest due first; any staff) and `POST /customers/privacy/requests/{id}/resolve` `{status: RESOLVED|REFUSED, resolution}` (`customers.privacy`; `409 PRIVACY_REQUEST_SETTLED`, `404`).
- `POST /customers/privacy/breach-intimations` `{noticeId, subject, body, customerIds}` → `201 {recipients, failures}` · `GET` — a breach told to each customer named, or to everyone reachable when none is (up to 500 named; `409 PRIVACY_NOBODY_TO_TELL`), by email where there is one and by text where there is only a phone, through notification-svc (`DATA_BREACH_INTIMATION`, one send per person deduped by the intimation); what was sent and to how many is kept for the report to the Board (r.7(1), r.7(2)(b)(vi)), a failure counted, never hidden. Owners and managers. The business then records the duty done on the platform's security notice (tenant-svc, below), the intimation's id as its reference.

## notification-svc

### Notifications (`/admin/notifications` + send)
Fan-in from Kafka events, plus a staff send path for POS receipts etc.
- `GET /admin/notifications/shortage-alerts` — list low-stock shortage alerts (by store or variant).
- `GET /admin/notifications` — in-app notification feed (welcome, order-confirmation, POS receipt, etc.), newest first. Each row names the `language` it was written in and whose words went out (`template`: `default` for the platform's, `v3` for the business's third version) — absent on rows written before 13.x.
- `POST /notifications/send` — staff-triggered send (order-svc uses this for EMAIL receipts). Body: `recipient`, `subject`, `body`, optional `type`/`eventId`/`customerId`/`category`. `category: MARKETING` (PECR reg.22) must name the customer and is sent only after customer-svc's `/marketing/allowance` says yes — otherwise `409 MARKETING_CONSENT_MISSING`, and also when the answer cannot be had (fail-closed: sending without provable consent is the offence). An allowed marketing message has the opt-out link appended by notification-svc itself (reg.23).

### Messages in the business's words (`/admin/notifications/templates`, `/admin/notifications/template-settings`)
What the platform sends a customer, a store or a supplier is a **template** the business may rewrite, form by form and language by language (13.x). Owners and managers only (`403` for everyone else); every route is scoped to the token's tenant, so a rival's templates are neither visible nor writable.
- `GET /admin/notifications/templates` — the catalogue: each message (`ORDER_CONFIRMED`, `ORDER_READY_FOR_COLLECTION` and `ORDER_DISPATCHED` (ship-from-store: a pickup picked in full, a delivery left with a carrier — order, carrier, reference), `ORDER_LINE_SHORT` and `ORDER_LINE_SUBSTITUTED` (substitutions for out-of-stock online lines: an item the store could not include and what goes back; what replaced what, that the shopper pays no more, the difference back), `RECALL_NOTICE`, `SUPPLIER_REMITTANCE`, and the seven store alerts), who reads it (`audience`: `CUSTOMER`, `STAFF`, `SUPPLIER`), why it is sent, the values it may say (`variables`, each with its `kind`: `TEXT`, `MONEY`, `NUMBER`, `DAY`, `MOMENT`, `FLAG`, `LIST`) and, per form (`EMAIL`, `SMS`, `PUSH`, `ALERT`), the subject and body limits, the parts that must be kept (`required`: one name of each group) and which languages the business has `written`.
- `GET /admin/notifications/templates/{type}/{form}/{language}` — the words that go out now: `source: BUSINESS` with the live `version`, or `source: DEFAULT` with the platform's English, the starting point for writing one; plus every version in `history`, newest first, retired ones with `retiredAt`.
- `PUT …/{type}/{form}/{language}` `{subject, body}` — saves a new version and retires the last in one transaction; versions are never edited. `POST …/preview` `{subject, body}` saves nothing and answers the message written out with sample values in that language — sample amounts in the business's own home currency (`XYZ`, no country assumed, when it cannot be read) — how many texts an SMS takes (`smsSegments`: GSM-7 160/153, otherwise 70/67) and every `problems` entry a save would be refused for. `DELETE …` retires the live version: the platform's words go out again, and the history stays.
- The syntax is a Mustache subset, plain text only: `{{name}}`, `{{#name}}…{{/name}}` (a flag that is set, or each item of a list, with `first` and `last` flags on the item), `{{^name}}…{{/name}}` (not set, or empty). No HTML, partials or lambdas. Money, numbers and dates are written the reader's way — the language with the business's country (`en-GB` writes `£12.50`, `pl-GB` writes `12,50 GBP`), dates long-form, moments as a date and `HH:mm UTC`.
- Refusals: `400 TEMPLATE_SUBJECT_REQUIRED` (every form but SMS has one), `TEMPLATE_BODY_REQUIRED`, `TEMPLATE_INVALID` (with the character where the syntax broke), `TEMPLATE_VARIABLE_UNKNOWN` (`details`: the names the message does not have), `TEMPLATE_TOO_LONG` (judged on the sample written out: an email 200/20,000, SMS 1,600, push 65/240, alert 200/2,000), `TEMPLATE_LANGUAGE_INVALID` (an ISO 639 code: two or three letters); `422 TEMPLATE_PART_REQUIRED` (`details`: each group left out, its names joined with `|`) — a recall notice by email must say the recall's reference, the product, what the danger is, what to do, the remedy and whom to contact (GPSR (EU) 2023/988 art.36(2)); `404 MESSAGE_UNKNOWN`, `MESSAGE_FORM_UNKNOWN`, `TEMPLATE_NOT_WRITTEN` (nothing of the business's to retire); `409 TEMPLATE_SAVED_MEANWHILE` when two saves race for the same next version (a partial unique index keeps one live version).
- `GET /admin/notifications/template-settings` · `PUT` `{defaultLanguage, signOff}` — the house language (what goes out when the reader's is not known) and how a customer's email is signed; `signedAs` is what the sign-off resolves to — `signOff`, else the business's name from tenant-svc, else `StoreQL`.
- **Which words go out.** The business's template in the reader's language (a customer's `preferredLanguage`, from customer-svc), else the business's in its house language, else the platform's English. A template the catalogue no longer accepts falls back to the platform's words rather than sending nothing. The notification log records the language and the version used.

**Business rules**
- **Metered texts (21.10).** Every text sent is announced as `SmsSent` (`storeql.notification.sms-sent`, through the outbox: `{eventId, tenantId, parts}`) for tenant-svc to count against the plan; a text that went but could not be announced is logged, never sent twice. A `MARKETING` text past the plan's hard ceiling on texts is `409 USAGE_QUOTA_REACHED`, asked after consent and of the text as it will go, footer and all.
- **Channels (13.7).** `POST /notifications/send` takes `channel`: `EMAIL` (the default: whatever `storeql.notification.channel` configures, below), `SMS` (the recipient is an E.164 number, the body at most 1600 characters; `SMS_RECIPIENT_INVALID`, `SMS_BODY_TOO_LONG`), `PUSH` (the recipient is a login id; every device that login registered with this shop is pushed to; `PUSH_NO_DEVICE` when it has none, and a device the provider no longer knows is forgotten on the spot) or `APP`. `CHANNEL_UNKNOWN` (400) otherwise; `CHANNEL_UNAVAILABLE` (503) when the channel's provider is not configured. Marketing goes by `EMAIL` or `SMS` only, each gated on the customer's consent for that channel (`MARKETING_CHANNEL_UNSUPPORTED` for push or in-app). The response and the log name the channel; `GET /admin/notifications?channel=` narrows the feed. Providers are seams with a simulated twin: SMS via `storeql.notification.sms.provider` = `SIMULATED` (default) or `TWILIO` (`…sms.twilio.account-sid`, `auth-token`, `from`); push via `storeql.notification.push.provider` = `SIMULATED` or `FCM` (HTTP v1: `…push.fcm.project-id`, `service-account` as JSON or a file path; the service account's key signs the OAuth assertion, the token is cached). `GET /admin/notifications/channels` says what this deployment has.
- `POST /notifications/devices` `{platform: ANDROID|IOS|WEB, token}` · `GET /notifications/devices` · `DELETE /notifications/devices/{id}` — **the caller's own push devices**, keyed on the token's login: a shopper from a storefront (the gateway derives the tenant from `X-Storefront-Tenant` for these shapes) or a member of staff from their console. The same token again refreshes the device; at most ten per login per shop (`PUSH_DEVICE_LIMIT`); the whole token is never shown back; another login's device is not found. `OrderConfirmed` pushes to the customer's login when they registered a device (beside the email). The app-side token comes from the platform's push SDK (FCM on Android and iOS, a service worker on the web), which is deployment wiring a Firebase project supplies — the same way the Stripe account is the merchant's.
- Channel is selected by `storeql.notification.channel`: `app` (default, in-app only), `email`/`smtp` (SMTP **plus** in-app via a composite channel so the feed still fills when email is on — email goes only to a recipient that is an email address: a store alert addressed to a store's id, or a device push to a login's, is the in-app feed's alone, and the log records the channel that carried it; before this, the mail server's refusal of a store id took the in-app copy down with it and the alert was retried and dead-lettered; a send naming `channel: APP` is the in-app feed alone whatever the deployment sends), or `mqtt` (device-facing push — POS terminals, kiosk/back-store displays, platform console — **plus** in-app; topic `storeql/notifications/{tenantId}/{recipient}`).
- MQTT auth: every client (including this service's own publisher connection) presents a storeql platform JWT as the MQTT password; the broker (EMQX) verifies it and ties the connecting username to the JWT's `tenant` claim, then ACL-scopes reads to that tenant's own topic subtree — see `infra/emqx.conf` / `infra/emqx-acl.conf`.

**Events**
- **Webhooks to a business's own systems (22.6),** `/admin/webhooks`: `GET /events` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the catalogue, twenty-one event types with a line each (`OrderPlaced`, `OrderConfirmed`, `OrderFulfilled`, `OrderDispatched`, `OrderCollected`, `OrderLineShortClosed`, `OrderLineSubstituted`, `OrderCancelled`, `OrderReturned`, `PaymentCaptured`, `PaymentRefunded`, `StockReceived`, `StockAdjusted`, `StockBelowThreshold`, `RecallOpened`, `GoodsReceived`, `ProductCreated`, `ProductUpdated`, `ProductDelisted`, `PriceChanged`, `CustomerRegistered`). `POST /endpoints { url, description, events }` (OWNER) → `201` with the signing secret (`whsec_…`), once; the URL is HTTPS on a public address with no credentials, or a host the deployment names in `storeql.webhooks.insecure-hosts` (`400 WEBHOOK_URL_INVALID`), the events from the catalogue (`400 WEBHOOK_EVENT_UNKNOWN`, `WEBHOOK_EVENTS_EMPTY`), a description (`WEBHOOK_DESCRIPTION_INVALID`); `503 WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED` when the deployment has no sealing key. `GET /endpoints`, `GET /endpoints/{id}` (OWNER, MANAGER) — never the secret; `PUT /endpoints/{id} { url?, description?, events?, enabled? }` (OWNER; `enabled: true` also clears a switch-off and its run of failures); `DELETE /endpoints/{id}` (OWNER; the delivery log goes with it); `POST /endpoints/{id}/secret` (OWNER) — a new secret, once, the old signs nothing more; `POST /endpoints/{id}/ping` (OWNER, MANAGER) → `202 { deliveryId }`, a `Ping` delivery queued now. `GET /deliveries?endpointId=&status=&after=&limit=` (OWNER, MANAGER) — newest first, cursor on the id, `status` one of `PENDING`, `DELIVERED`, `DEAD` (`400 WEBHOOK_STATUS_INVALID`); `GET /deliveries/{id}` — with the payload and `attemptLog`, every try with its status, error, the first 8 KB of the answer and the duration; `POST /deliveries/{id}/redeliver` (OWNER, MANAGER) — queued now whatever its state.
  - **What a receiver gets:** `POST <url>` signed to the [Standard Webhooks](https://www.standardwebhooks.com/) specification, so any library written to it verifies: `webhook-id` (the delivery id, the same on every retry — dedupe on it), `webhook-timestamp` (unix seconds) and `webhook-signature: v1,<base64>` where the digest is `HMAC-SHA256(base64decode(secret without whsec_), "<id>.<timestamp>.<raw body>")`; beside them `Content-Type: application/json`, `User-Agent: StoreQL-Webhooks/1`, `X-StoreQL-Event` (the type) and `X-StoreQL-Attempt`. The secret is `whsec_` and 32 bytes in base64, as the specification writes it. The body is `{ id, type, eventId, tenantId, attempt, sentAt, occurredAt, data }`, `data` being the event exactly as published. Verify over the raw bytes and compare in constant time; hold the timestamp to a window (five minutes is usual) against replay. Answer 2xx within ten seconds; anything else is tried again after 1 min, 5 min, 30 min, 2 h and 8 h, then marked `DEAD` and left for a redeliver by hand. An endpoint that fails twenty deliveries in a row is switched off with the reason (`disabledReason`), and switched on again with `PUT { enabled: true }`. A delivery is made once per event and endpoint whatever Kafka redelivers; settled deliveries are kept thirty days (`storeql.webhooks.keep-days`).
- Consumes: `TrialNoticeIssued` (21.13: `TRIAL_ENDING` and `TRIAL_ENDED` to the business's billing address, in the platform's name, once per event), `DunningNoticeIssued` (21.12: the email a business gets when it is late paying the platform — `INVOICE_OVERDUE` for each reminder, `SERVICE_SUSPENDED` the day the service goes — to the address on its subscription, in the platform's name, carrying the link that pays with no sign-in; once per event), `OrderConfirmed` (order-confirmation notice), `StockBelowThreshold` (shortage alert), `FoodSafetyCheckFailed`, `FoodSafetyCheckOverdue` (store alerts, addressed to the store's devices like a shortage alert, once per event), `RecallOpened` (one alert per store whose stock was held, each deduplicated on an id derived from the event and the store), `RecallNoticeIssued` (05.10: the written recall notice to a buyer — by email to the shop's customer record, else by text to an E.164 number the order or the record holds, and a push to the login's devices besides; the body carries what GPSR art.36 lists: the headline *Product safety recall*, the product and its lot, the hazard in plain words, what to do, the remedies to choose from, where to turn, and a request to pass it on; each send keyed on the event, so a redelivery tells nobody twice; a buyer with no email and no usable number is logged, and order-svc keeps the notice for when they come back), `UserRegistered` (welcome/registration notice), `CustomerErased` (SJ-D43; redacts the recipient/subject/body of that shop's messages to the customer), `AccountDeleted` (SJ-D43; redacts the platform's own messages to the deleted account, e.g. `WELCOME` and `PASSWORD_RESET` — a shop's own messages are redacted by its own `CustomerErased` instead).
- Consumes `PasswordResetRequested` ([password reset](../intent/password-reset.md)): one email per request listing each login with its own link — *your shopper account*, *staff — Corner Stores Ltd*, and for a business that signs its staff in through its identity provider a line with no link — in the platform's own words, never a business's (not in the catalogue: a business cannot reword a message that carries a key to its staff's logins), in the request's language where the platform has it (en, ar, bn, gu, pa, pl, ro, ur; English otherwise), saying when the links stop working (UTC). **Email only**, through its own SMTP sender built from `storeql.notification.smtp.*` and live only when `storeql.notification.channel` is `email`/`smtp`: never in-app, MQTT or text; with no email transport, or an SMTP failure, the row says `NOT_SENT` and nothing is retried. The notification-log row belongs to no business (`tenant_id` null, so no business's log read finds it), holds the address and the words with every link replaced by `[link removed]`, is recorded against the shopper login (so `AccountDeleted` erases it) and is deleted by the platform after `storeql.notification.password-reset.retention-days` (30). Once per event.
- `OrderConfirmed` with a window (`slotStartsAt`, `slotEndsAt`, `slotTimeZone`): the confirmation's `{{window}}` says *Delivery:* / *Collection:* with the weekday, date and times in the store's own zone, written the reader's way; a message without a window reads exactly as before.
- Publishes: `SmsSent` (21.10: each text sent, by the parts the carrier bills; tenant-svc meters it) and `RetentionRunCompleted` (21.16), both through its outbox.

---

## order-svc

### Order Lifecycle (`/orders`)
- `GET /orders` — list orders for the tenant (filter by store/channel/status/fulfilment type, creation date range `from`/`to`, and — ship-from-store — `handover=PENDING|DONE` and when handed over, `handedFrom`/`handedTo`).
- `GET /orders/mine` — the signed-in customer's own order history (never another customer's, never the tenant's full book).
- Order lines (`items[].weighingInstrumentId`) — for a line sold by weight, the certified instrument the reading came from, from tenant-svc's register; the till refuses to sell by weight from one that is not certified, and the line records which it was, so an inspector can go from a receipt to a certificate. Null for a line sold by the each.
- `POST /pos/age-checks` — the due-diligence record behind an age-restricted sale (Licensing Act 2003 s.139 and its equivalents: the defence is "all reasonable precautions", and a precaution nobody can show was taken is none). One append-only entry per check the till made, pass or refusal, carrying the rule that applied at that moment (category, minimum age, country, whether it was store policy), the cashier from the token, and — required on a refusal — why: `UNDER_AGE`, `NO_ID`, `ID_REJECTED`, `PROXY_SALE`, `BORN_AFTER_CUTOFF`, `OTHER`; optionally what was shown on a pass (`PASSPORT`, `DRIVING_LICENCE`, `PASS_CARD`, …). Any staff role, at a store the cashier is assigned to. A refusal with no reason, a pass with one, or a value outside the vocabulary is a 400. A check against a birth-date cut-off also carries `bornBefore` and `bornBeforeStorePolicy`, copied from product-svc's answer; a `BORN_AFTER_CUTOFF` refusal or a cut-off policy without the date is `AGE_CHECK_CUTOFF_REQUIRED`, and a malformed or impossible date `AGE_CHECK_BORN_BEFORE_INVALID`. The customer's own date of birth is never sent or stored.
- `GET /admin/audit/events?store=&actor=&type=&from=&to=&after=&limit=` — **the business audit trail (20.11)**: every discount granted, sale voided, drawer opened without a sale, order cancelled and return taken, newest first, each naming the member of staff responsible (`actorId`), the store, the order, the money (`amount`: the discount or the refund) and the reason, plus the log's own qualifier in `detail` (the role that authorised a discount, the status a cancel came from, a return's refund method, the supervisor who authorised a no-sale). One `UNION ALL` over the five append-only logs, tenant-filtered in every branch, cursor-paged on `(occurredAt, id)`. `type` is `DISCOUNT|VOID|NO_SALE|CANCEL|RETURN` (`AUDIT_TYPE_UNKNOWN` otherwise); `from` must be before `to` (`AUDIT_RANGE_EMPTY`). Management-only by path; a store-bound manager reads their own stores, and a `store` outside their scope is `403`. Read-only: the resource has no writing method. Stock adjustments live on inventory-svc's movements ledger (`actorId` on each row) and the admin Audit screen reads both.
- `GET /admin/pos/age-checks?store=&outcome=&from=&to=&after=&limit=` · `GET /admin/pos/age-checks/summary?store=&from=&to=` — the register and its counts (total, passed, refused, refusals by reason, checks by category): what a licensing officer asks for first. Management-only.
- `GET /orders/export?customer=&login=` — every order one person placed here, with lines, for a data export (art.20). Staff-only, and read service-to-service by customer-svc, which assembles the whole document. Both ids are accepted because a person has both: an online sale is filed under their login and a till sale under the shop's customer record (SJ-D44). Neither id is a 400; a shopper token is a 403 — the read allowlist no longer takes a literal such as `export` for an order id, and the resource requires a staff role itself.
- `POST /orders` — place a new order (POS or ONLINE channel); requires an `Idempotency-Key`.
- **A phone at the till** ([intent](../intent/phone-at-the-till.md)): a POS sale at a store whose `tillPhone` is `REQUIRED` needs a `contactPhone` or a `customerId` (`409 ORDER_CONTACT_PHONE_REQUIRED`); an `OPTIONAL` or `OFF` store takes one with neither. Any order's `contactPhone` is read in the store's own country, then the business's home and its other stores' (common-service `PhoneNumbers`, the first valid reading), and the order answer carries it as `contactPhoneE164` beside the number as typed. At the till a number that is no phone anywhere the business trades is `400 ORDER_CONTACT_PHONE_INVALID`; online it is kept as typed with no `contactPhoneE164`, never refused. The store's choice and the countries come through `TenantProfiles` (cached five minutes, so a changed choice reaches order-svc within five minutes; the till reads it at once): unreadable, the choice counts as Optional and the number is kept as typed — a sale is never refused for want of an answer. A customer's erasure and the retention purge blank both forms.
- `POST /pos/log/orders/{orderId}` — journal a completed POS sale to the transaction log. **Staff-reachable** (CASHIER/MANAGER/OWNER) and idempotent on the order, so a retry or a replayed offline sale returns the existing entry. This write used to sit under `/admin/pos-log`, which is management-gated — the cashier who took the sale could not journal it, so nothing ever did and the table was empty for the life of the product. The read side stays at `GET /admin/pos-log`.
- `GET /admin/reports/exceptions?from&to&storeId&groupBy=ACTOR|STORE` — staff exception report: discounts, voids and no-sale drawer opens per staff member or store, with journalled sales as the denominator. Check `journalCoverage` before reading any rate; when false, nothing journalled a sale in the period and the counts have nothing to divide by.
- `GET /admin/reports/sales-by-hour?from&to&storeId&channel&tz` — takings bucketed by hour of the trading day, so a manager can staff to the actual peak. `tz` takes an IANA zone name (`Europe/London`) or a bare ISO offset (`+05:30`); without it the hours are counted in UTC, which puts a shop outside UTC at the wrong time of day. Only CONFIRMED and FULFILLED orders count. Hours with no trade are absent rather than returned as zero.
- `GET /admin/reports/deposits?from&to&storeId` — **container deposits (09.16):** deposits charged on sales that stand (cancelled and voided excluded) and refunded at the till over [from, to), by material, with the VAT inside the deposits where the scheme taxes them and the unredeemed difference. OWNER or MANAGER; `400 ORDER_REPORT_PERIOD_INVALID`.
- `GET /admin/reports/sales-by-staff?from&to&storeId&limit` — takings per cashier with average basket and discount rate, biggest taker first. Read from the POS transaction journal, so **in-store only** — an online order has no cashier, and these totals will not reconcile to the sales summary for that reason. `UNATTRIBUTED` buckets journal entries naming nobody.
- `GET /orders/{id}` — get an order with its line items.
- `POST /orders` with `awaitingPrice: true` (POS only) — a **catalog-mode till order** (SJ-D41): the goods are known, the prices are not. It is placed as `AWAITING_PRICE`, which the stranded-order sweeper never touches (it cancels `PENDING` orders whose last change is older than `storeql.order.pending-sweeper.ttl-hours`), and waits for a manager. Refused for an online order (`400 ORDER_AWAITING_PRICE_POS_ONLY`).
- `POST /orders/{id}/price` `{lines:[{variantId, unitPrice}], taxAmount}` — a manager prices it: every line on the order must get a unit price of at least zero (`400 ORDER_PRICE_LINE_MISSING` / `ORDER_PRICE_LINE_UNKNOWN` / `ORDER_PRICE_INVALID`), the totals are recomputed, the history records "priced: total X", and the order becomes `PENDING` — payable at the till or via *Collect payment*, and swept as stranded only if it then sits unpaid past the TTL. Management-only; `409 ORDER_NOT_AWAITING_PRICE` for any other state. An `AWAITING_PRICE` order can also be cancelled.
- `POST /orders/{id}/confirm` — confirm an order (e.g. once payment settles). A **till sale** — channel `POS`, fulfilment `INSTORE` or `PICKUP` — is confirmed *and* handed over in the same transaction, emitting `OrderFulfilled` so its stock is deducted.
- `POST /orders/{id}/cancel` — cancel an order pre-fulfilment.
- `POST /orders/{id}/fulfil` — hand an order over, or part of it (SJ-D35). Without a body, everything still outstanding goes — for an untouched order the plain all-or-nothing fulfilment this always was. With `{lines:[{variantId, qty}]}`, only those quantities go now: each line keeps a cumulative `fulfilledQty`, the order is `PARTIALLY_FULFILLED` until every line is complete and then `FULFILLED`, the status history records "part-fulfilled: 4 of 5 units handed over", and each call emits one `OrderFulfilled` carrying only that call's quantities, so inventory-svc deducts what left the store now and nothing twice (its per-line dedupe is keyed by event, so several events per order are fine; a checkout hold that does not match a partial line is released and the deduction taken directly). Refused: more than is outstanding (`409 ORDER_FULFIL_QTY_EXCEEDS_OUTSTANDING`, saying how much is), a variant not on the order (`400 ORDER_FULFIL_LINE_UNKNOWN`), a zero or negative quantity (`400`), an order that is not `CONFIRMED`/`PARTIALLY_FULFILLED` (`409 ORDER_NOT_FULFILLABLE`), a caller without access to the order's store (`403`). A part-fulfilled order cannot be cancelled (`409 ORDER_PARTLY_FULFILLED` — return the goods or hand over the rest); a short close of the remainder is not built. For online and delivery orders; a till sale never needs it, because it is handed over whole when it is paid for.
- `GET /orders/{id}/history` — order status history/audit trail.
- `POST /orders/{id}/void` — void a completed POS sale (post-fulfilment correction, distinct from cancel). **Puts the stock back**: `OrderVoided` carries `eventId`, `storeId` and the lines to restock — each line net of anything already returned, and empty when the sale was never handed over, so nothing that was not deducted is restocked. Decided under a row lock on the order, and "handed over" is read from the append-only status history rather than the current status, which a refund would have moved on. **Management roles only**: the shared `AdminAuthorizationFilter` refuses a `CASHIER` or `STOREKEEPER` with `403` before the resource is reached; `ORDER_VOID_ONLY_POS` (409) for an online order, `ORDER_CANNOT_VOID` (409) for a sale already voided or cancelled. The void is recorded against the caller in `pos_void_log`, which the staff exception report reads. The admin Orders screen offers it as *Void sale* on a till sale that is still standing (09.13).
- `POST /orders/{id}/returns` / `GET /orders/{id}/returns` — create/list returns against an order (full or partial). With `recallNoticeId`, the return settles that recall notice as `REFUNDED` in the same transaction (05.10): 409 `RECALL_NOTICE_ORDER_MISMATCH` when the notice is about another order, 409 `RECALL_NOTICE_RESOLVED` when already settled, and in either case no return is recorded.
- `POST /orders/container-refunds` / `GET /orders/container-refunds/{id}` — **deposit return (09.16).** Empties brought back to the till: `{storeId, tillSessionId, lines:[{material, volumeMl, count}]}` with an `Idempotency-Key`; the scheme in force where the store trades (from tenant-svc's register) says which containers it takes back and for how much, and the refund is recorded with one line per kind and published as `ContainerDepositRefunded`, which payment-svc books as a `PAY_OUT` on the till session once. `400 ORDER_CONTAINER_NOT_IN_SCHEME` for a container the scheme does not take back, `ORDER_CONTAINER_LINES_INVALID` for none or more than 20 kinds, `ORDER_CONTAINER_COUNT_TOO_MANY` past 500 of a kind or 2,000 in all, `MISSING_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`; `409 ORDER_DEPOSIT_SCHEME_NOT_IN_FORCE` where no scheme is in force. A retry with the same key returns the first refund. CASHIER, MANAGER or OWNER.
- **Deposits on the sale (09.16):** `POST /orders` puts the return-scheme deposit on each item sold in a container the scheme covers — by the `depositMaterial`/`depositVolumeMl` product-svc records — as its own line (`deposits[]`: material, volume, qty, `depositEach`, `amount`, `vatTreatment` `OUTSIDE_SCOPE` or `STANDARD`, `vatRate`/`vatAmount`, `schemeScope`, `citation`) with `depositAmount` added to `total` and no part of `subtotal`, `taxAmount` or any discount. The UK deposit is outside the scope of VAT (VATA 1994 ss.55B–55D); Germany's Pfand is taxed as the drink (VerpackG §31), the VAT inside it shown on the line.

### Split delivery across stores (`/orders`, `/order-groups`) — order orchestration
[intent](../intent/order-orchestration-and-split-fulfilment.md). An **online delivery** order the delivery-area store cannot fill alone is routed to the shops that can, at `POST /orders` (only when stock holds are on, `storeql.order.inventory.reserve-enforce`):
- **The rule** (`domain/Routing`, pure): the delivery-area store takes every line it holds whole; what is left goes whole to the **nearest** other shop that holds all of it (straight-line distance between the stores' `geoLat`/`geoLng`, a store with no coordinates last); failing one, to the **fewest** shops — each time the one holding most of what is left, the nearer on a tie; a line is split across shops only when no one shop holds it. Candidates are the business's **shops** (type `STORE`) that are trading and that the caller may act at; a **warehouse never fills a shopper's order**. A product a supplier ships per order (dropship) is held nowhere and rides with the first part.
- **Stock by store** comes from inventory-svc `GET /admin/inventory/network/stock?variants=` (staff; at most 200 products; per store and product `available`, plus the `dropship` products), read as a storekeeper. When the stock or the stores cannot be read, the order is placed at the area store as it always was, and its holds decide.
- **One shop other than the area store holds it all:** an ordinary order at that shop. **Several:** an **order group** (`order_groups`: shopper, total, currency, the checkout's key) and one **child order per shop** (`orders.group_id`, `orders.group_part` — the area store's part first). Each part is an order like any other — its own lines (a split line shared by quantity), its share of the tax (its lines' own VAT when the quote carried it, else by subtotal; the last part takes the rounding, so the parts add up to the quote exactly), its own holds at its own store, its own deposits and its own `OrderPlaced` naming the `groupId`; confirmed, picked, fulfilled, cancelled and refunded as an order is. Holds are all or nothing: a shop refusing its hold releases what the others held and the checkout fails with that refusal. The key places the group once; a retry returns the first part.
- **Not split:** a pickup order, a till sale, a basket with a staff discount or a whole-basket offer (placed at the area store as before). A line promotion goes with the parts that hold its product, shared by quantity; a coupon is spent once, against the first part.
- The answer to `POST /orders` and `GET /orders/{id}` carries `group` (`id`, `loginId`, `total`, `currency`, `createdAt`, `parts[]`: `orderId`, `storeId`, `status`, `total`, `units`) for a part of a split checkout; `GET /orders` and `/orders/mine` carry `groupId` on each part.
- `GET /order-groups/{id}` — the checkout and its parts, for the shopper who placed it or the business's staff (at least one part's store); anyone else `404 ORDER_GROUP_NOT_FOUND`. The gateway's storefront whitelist and common-web's read allowlist admit exactly this shape to a customer token.
- Refused: `409 ORDER_UNFULFILLABLE` when no combination of shops holds the order — nothing is held anywhere.

### Ship-from-store and dark-store picking (`/orders/{id}/dispatch`, `/orders/{id}/collect`, `handover=` on the lists)
[intent](../intent/ship-from-store-and-dark-store-picking.md). **FULFILLED is picked and packed** — the goods off the shelf and the sale recognised, exactly what a completed wave leaves — and the **handover** after it is its own recorded step, once per order, on `order_handovers` (append-only; the order's status is not changed by it, so every consumer keeps its one meaning of FULFILLED).
- `POST /orders/{id}/dispatch {carrier, reference?, parcels?}` — a FULFILLED online **delivery** left with a carrier: the carrier's name (required, ≤80), its reference or tracking number when it gave one, the parcel count when counted. Any member of staff assigned to the order's store (CASHIER, STOREKEEPER, MANAGER, OWNER; `403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED` otherwise). Appends "dispatched: DPD, ref 1Z…" to the status history and publishes `OrderDispatched {eventId, occurredAt, tenantId, orderId, storeId, customerId, loginId, carrier, reference, parcels}`; notification-svc tells the shopper *on its way* with the carrier and reference (`ORDER_DISPATCHED`). Answers the order with `handover {kind: DISPATCHED, carrier, reference, parcels, at, by}`.
- `POST /orders/{id}/collect {collectedBy?}` — a FULFILLED online **pickup** was collected at the counter, naming who took it when staff noted it; the body may be empty. Publishes `OrderCollected {…, collectedBy}`; the shopper hears nothing (they were there). `handover {kind: COLLECTED, collectedBy, at, by}`.
- Refused: `409 ORDER_NOT_PICKED` (not yet FULFILLED — still being picked, part-picked, or cancelled: a delivery is dispatched whole, once every line is picked), `409 ORDER_HANDOVER_KIND_MISMATCH` (dispatch on a pickup, collect on a delivery, either on a till sale — a till sale is handed over when it is paid), `409 ORDER_ALREADY_HANDED_OVER` (once; the unique row is the guard against two members of staff at once), `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` (no carrier, a field too long, parcels below 1), `404 ORDER_NOT_FOUND` for another tenant's order, whatever the caller's role or store scope.
- **The queue:** `GET /orders?store=&status=FULFILLED&fulfilmentType=DELIVERY&handover=PENDING` is the packed parcels awaiting the courier, `…fulfilmentType=PICKUP&handover=PENDING` the bags awaiting their shopper, `handover=DONE` what was handed over; `handover` is `PENDING` or `DONE` (`400 ORDER_HANDOVER_FILTER_INVALID`), `fulfilmentType` PICKUP, DELIVERY or INSTORE.
- **Handed over when:** `handedFrom=` (inclusive) and `handedTo=` (exclusive), ISO-8601 instants, bound **when the order was handed over** — its `order_handovers.handed_at`, read inside order-svc's own schema — not when it was placed, which `from=`/`to=` still bound. *Handed over today* is `GET /orders?store=&handedFrom=<the store's midnight>`: yesterday's delivery dispatched this morning is in it, where `handover=DONE&from=<midnight>` would miss it. Either bound on its own implies `handover=DONE` (`handover=DONE` may be said as well); with `handover=PENDING` it is `400 ORDER_HANDOVER_FILTER_INVALID` (an order not handed over has no handover time), and a bound that is not an instant is `400 INVALID_DATE`. The two ranges combine: `handedFrom=&from=` is the orders placed **and** handed over since then. Tenant-scoped like every list — another business's handovers are never listed, whatever store is named. Every order and summary — `GET /orders`, `/orders/mine`, `/orders/{id}` — carries `handover` once it has one. What waits to be picked is inventory-svc's `GET /admin/inventory/waves/awaiting?storeId=`.
- **The shopper told:** `OrderFulfilled` now names the buyer (`customerId`, `loginId`; JSON null for a guest or a till sale) so notification-svc sends `ORDER_READY_FOR_COLLECTION` once, only on the `OrderFulfilled` that makes an online pickup order FULFILLED — not a part handover, not a delivery, not a till sale.
- **Dark store:** a store of type `DARK_STORE` (tenant-svc; a shop with no shop floor) fills online orders for delivery only: `POST /orders` there is `409 ORDER_PICKUP_NOT_OFFERED` for a PICKUP (or INSTORE) and `409 ORDER_NO_TILL_AT_DARK_STORE` for a POS order; a DELIVERY is placed, routed and picked as at any shop. The type is read through `TenantProfiles.Stores.isDark` (REST, cached); when the stores cannot be read the store is taken for a shop and nothing is refused.

### Delivery and collection slots (`/admin/fulfilment-windows`, `/storefront/fulfilment-slots`, `slotWindowId` on `POST /orders`)
[intent](../intent/delivery-and-collection-slots.md). Each store offers weekly **windows** for delivery and for collection separately; a shopper picks one at checkout, and the order holds a place in it.
- `GET /admin/fulfilment-windows?storeId=` · `POST /admin/fulfilment-windows {storeId, fulfilmentType: DELIVERY|PICKUP, weekday: 1–7 (ISO, Monday 1), startTime "HH:mm", endTime "HH:mm", capacity ≥ 1, cutoffMinutes ≥ 0, active?}` → `201` · `PUT /admin/fulfilment-windows/{id} {weekday, startTime, endTime, capacity, cutoffMinutes, active}` (store and type fixed) — OWNER and MANAGER; a manager held to stores only at theirs (`403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED`). A window is `{id, storeId, fulfilmentType, weekday, startTime, endTime, capacity, cutoffMinutes, active, timeZone, updatedAt, updatedBy}`; switched-off windows are listed too. Times are the **store's own** (its IANA zone from tenant-svc through `TenantProfiles.Stores.zoneOf`, also kept on the window as the fallback when tenant-svc cannot be read). `400 ORDER_SLOT_STORE_REQUIRED`; `404 ORDER_SLOT_STORE_NOT_FOUND` for a store that is not the business's; `404 ORDER_SLOT_WINDOW_NOT_FOUND`; `400 ORDER_SLOT_WINDOW_INVALID` (start not before end, capacity below 1, a negative cut-off, a weekday outside 1–7, a bad type, or overlapping another active window of the store, type and weekday — touching windows do not overlap; `details` say which).
- `GET /storefront/fulfilment-slots?store=&type=DELIVERY|PICKUP` — public (the storefront's tenant): `{storeId, fulfilmentType, timeZone, offered, days[7]: {date, slots: [{windowId, startsAt, endsAt, startTime, endTime, left, full}]}}`. `offered` is whether the store has any active window of that type; `days` are today and the next six in the store's zone, a day with nothing bookable `slots: []`; an occurrence past its cut-off (now + cut-off ≥ start) is left out, a full one is `left: 0, full: true`. A window on a day the clocks change keeps its local times; `startsAt`/`endsAt` are UTC, `date`/`startTime`/`endTime` the store's. For a delivery, ask for the store the postcode resolves to (`GET /fulfilment/resolve`). `400 ORDER_SLOT_STORE_REQUIRED`, `ORDER_SLOT_TYPE_INVALID`; `404 ORDER_SLOT_STORE_NOT_FOUND`.
- `POST /orders {…, slotWindowId, slotStartsAt}` — ONLINE DELIVERY and PICKUP only (`400 ORDER_SLOT_NOT_APPLICABLE` otherwise). Where the fulfilling store (for a delivery, the area store the postcode resolves to) offers windows of that type, one is required (`400 ORDER_SLOT_REQUIRED`); a window not of that store, type and business, only one of the two fields, or a `slotStartsAt` that is not an occurrence of the window in the next seven days is `400 ORDER_SLOT_UNKNOWN`; past its cut-off `409 ORDER_SLOT_CLOSED`; at capacity `409 ORDER_SLOT_FULL`. The place is taken on the order's own transaction under a lock on the window row, counting orders at that occurrence that are not CANCELLED or VOIDED, a split checkout once (`COUNT(DISTINCT coalesce(group_id, id))`), so the last place is taken once however checkouts race; an `Idempotency-Key` replay takes no second place; a cancelled, voided or lapsed order gives its place back. Every part of a split delivery carries the window. A store with no windows checks out exactly as before.
- Every order answer (by id, the lists, `/orders/mine`, order groups) carries `slot: null | {startsAt, endsAt, timeZone, date, startTime, endTime}` — the local ones computed by the server, so no client converts times. `GET /orders?sort=slot` lists in window order, orders without one last, with a cursor that carries both keys (the Fulfilment queue).
- `OrderPlaced` and `OrderConfirmed` carry `slotStartsAt`, `slotEndsAt`, `slotTimeZone` when the order has a window (absent otherwise); notification-svc says it in the confirmation. No fee in this cut; windows are weekly (no dated holidays); a window cannot be changed after placing.
- Another business's owner, manager, cashier or storekeeper — even naming our store or window id — reads no windows, sets none and takes no place; its storefront lists none of ours.

### Substitutions for out-of-stock online lines (`/orders/{id}/lines/{variantId}/short`, `…/substitute`, `…/substitutes`, `/orders/owing`)
[intent](../intent/substitutions-for-out-of-stock-online-lines.md). A picker who finds a line of a CONFIRMED or PARTIALLY_FULFILLED online pickup or delivery order short either **closes it short** or, where the shopper allowed it at checkout, **puts a substitute in the bag**; a till sale, a pending, cancelled or picked order has no line to close (`409 ORDER_LINE_NOT_ADJUSTABLE`). Every adjustment is a row of `order_line_adjustments` (append-only) and a status-history line; the order's `subtotal`, `taxAmount` and `total` are recomputed as `/price` computes them; **the order becomes FULFILLED once every line is picked or closed**, so it can be handed over.
- `POST /orders {…, allowSubstitutions?}` — the shopper's choice, **on unless they turn it off**; the order and its summaries carry `allowSubstitutions`. Read from the order, never from the request that substitutes.
- `POST /orders/{id}/lines/{variantId}/short {qty?, reason?}` — the quantity (everything still outstanding when omitted) will never be handed over: `shortQty` on the line rises, its `lineTotal` and `vatAmount` (and any container deposit on it) shrink pro rata to what stands, and the shopper is refunded the difference. Publishes `OrderLineShortClosed {eventId, occurredAt, tenantId, orderId, storeId, customerId, loginId, currency, orderTotal, channel, fulfilmentType, variantId, variantName, qty, refundAmount}`. `409 ORDER_LINE_QTY_EXCEEDS_OUTSTANDING` (more than the line owes, or nothing owed), `400 ORDER_LINE_UNKNOWN`, `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` (a quantity below 0.001, a reason over 200 characters).
- `POST /orders/{id}/lines/{variantId}/substitute {substituteVariantId, qty?, unitPrice?, reason?}` — a new line for the substitute, **priced at the order's store by pricing-svc as at checkout and charged at no more than the original line's gross unit price** (a dearer substitute is held to the original's price with its own VAT rate inside it; a cheaper one is charged its own price and the difference refunded), already picked (`OrderFulfilled` for it deducts the stock and records the revenue, its `outstanding` naming the original's new figure), `substitutesItemId` naming the line it stands in for; the original closed short for the quantity. Publishes `OrderLineSubstituted {…, fromVariantId, fromName, toVariantId, toName, qty, chargedAmount, refundAmount}` beside the `OrderFulfilled`. `409 ORDER_SUBSTITUTION_NOT_ALLOWED` (the shopper said no), `400 ORDER_SUBSTITUTE_SAME_VARIANT`, `409 ORDER_SUBSTITUTE_NOT_SELLABLE` (pricing-svc has no price for it at the store, or inventory-svc says the store has less than the quantity — an unreadable shelf refuses nothing, a dropship product passes), `503 ORDER_PRICING_UNAVAILABLE`; with server-side pricing off (`storeql.order.pricing.enforce=false`) `unitPrice` is required (`400 ORDER_PRICE_REQUIRED`) and still capped.
- `GET /orders/{id}/lines/{variantId}/substitutes` — the stand-ins the business declared (product-svc's `SUBSTITUTE` item relationships for the variant), each with `productName`, `sku` and what the order's store has `available` (inventory-svc), most available first; none when none is declared. The picker may still name any variant the store sells and has.
- `GET /orders/owing?store=` — the store's CONFIRMED and PARTIALLY_FULFILLED online orders with the lines each still owes (`qty`, `fulfilledQty`, `shortQty`, `outstandingQty`) and `allowSubstitutions`, oldest first: the Fulfilment screen's outstanding lines.
- Every write and read above: any member of staff assigned to the order's store (CASHIER, STOREKEEPER, MANAGER, OWNER; `403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED` otherwise; a shopper `403`); `404 ORDER_NOT_FOUND` for another tenant's order whatever the caller's role or store scope; the two writes take an `Idempotency-Key` (a retried press closes or substitutes once: the key's adjustment is answered again).
- **What follows the events:** payment-svc refunds `refundAmount` against the order's captured tenders once per event, the `PaymentRefunded` carrying `kind: ORDER_ADJUSTMENT` so order-svc records the money without moving the order to PARTIALLY_REFUNDED (the goods are still to be handed over); inventory-svc gives back that much of the order's hold on the variant (a hold shrinks, or is released and `StockReleased` published) and shortens the waiting line on the same transaction, once per event; notification-svc tells the shopper (`ORDER_LINE_SHORT`: what could not be included and what goes back; `ORDER_LINE_SUBSTITUTED`: what replaced what, that they pay no more, the difference back, and that they may hand it back at handover — a refused substitute is a return, refunded and restocked); both events are in the webhook catalogue.

### Recall notices to buyers (`/orders/recall-notices`)
A recall's notice to the buyer of one order (05.10; GPSR (EU) 2023/988 arts.35–37: a direct written notice to every consumer who can be identified, saying what art.36 lists, and a remedy the consumer chooses under art.37). inventory-svc finds the sales that drew on a recalled pack and publishes `RecallSaleAffected` per order; this service knows who placed the order — the login, the shop's customer record, or a phone number the buyer left at checkout — and issues the notice: `recall_notices` (one per recall and order; the recall's text, remedies and contact as they stood, so what the buyer was told never changes under them) and append-only `recall_notice_lines`. `status` is `ISSUED` (someone to tell, and told), `UNIDENTIFIED` (an anonymous till sale, kept for the count and for the buyer who comes back with the receipt), `REMEDY_CHOSEN` or `RESOLVED`. An `ISSUED` notice publishes `RecallNoticeIssued`, which notification-svc writes to the buyer. The consumer is idempotent on the event and on (recall, order); a withdrawal, a malformed event and an order this service never saw are skipped. The buyer's phone never leaves the service and is forgotten with the customer's erasure; `buyerIdentified` — whether the order named anyone to write to — is fixed when the notice is issued (V29), so settling an anonymous sale at the counter never counts as a buyer told.
- `GET /orders/recall-notices/mine` — the signed-in shopper's own notices, newest first, keyed on the token's login like `/orders/mine` (401 `NO_CUSTOMER` without one). This and `POST /orders/recall-notices/{id}/remedy` are the two shapes the gateway's storefront whitelist and common-web's read/mutation allowlists open to a customer token with `X-Storefront-Tenant`; nothing else under `/orders/recall-notices` is. Each carries the headline material: reference, hazard, reason, `customerNotice`, `remedies`, `singleRemedyReason`, `contactPhone`/`contactUrl`, the order and when it was sold, and `lines` with `productName`, `sku`, `batchNo`, `expiryDate`, `qty` and `match`.
- `POST /orders/recall-notices/{id}/remedy` — `{ remedy }`. The shopper chooses on a notice issued to their login (any other notice is 404, so ids cannot be probed); staff choose for a buyer at the counter on any notice of the business (`remedyChosenVia` `SHOPPER|STAFF`). Once: 409 `RECALL_REMEDY_ALREADY_CHOSEN`; only what was offered: 409 `RECALL_REMEDY_NOT_OFFERED`; not once settled: 409 `RECALL_NOTICE_RESOLVED`. Twenty at once record one.
- `GET /orders/recall-notices?recallId&status&after&limit` — a recall's notices, newest first, cursor-paginated. Staff only.
- `GET /orders/recall-notices/progress?recallId` — `notices`, `identified`, `unidentified`, `remedyChosen`, `resolved`, and `chosen` per remedy. Staff only.
- `POST /orders/recall-notices/{id}/resolve` — `{ resolution: REPLACED|REPAIRED|DECLINED, notes }`. Staff only. A remedy nobody had chosen is recorded as the one the settlement implies. `REFUNDED` is refused here (400 `RECALL_REFUND_THROUGH_RETURN`): a refund is a return of the order naming the notice, so the goods, the money and the notice settle together — and the returned pack is held on arrival by the open recall.
- Publishes: `RecallNoticeIssued` (the notice's text and offer, the order, and the ids an address is resolved from: `customerId`, `loginId`, `buyerPhone`).
- Consumes: `RecallSaleAffected` (inventory-svc).

### POS Operations
- `POST /pos/parked-sales`, `GET /pos/parked-sales`, `GET /pos/parked-sales/{id}`, `DELETE /pos/parked-sales/{id}` — park (suspend), list, get, or cancel an in-progress POS sale so a cashier can serve another customer and resume later.
- `POST /pos/no-sale` — log a no-sale/open-drawer event.
- `POST /admin/pos-log/orders/{orderId}` — record the POSLog transaction-journal entry for a fulfilled POS order.
- `GET /admin/pos-log`, `GET /admin/pos-log/orders/{orderId}` — list the POS transaction journal, tenant-wide or per order.
- `GET /admin/pos/stock-positions` — live (eventually-consistent) on-hand snapshot for POS screens, fed asynchronously from inventory-svc events.
- `POST /admin/orders/{orderId}/receipts`, `GET /admin/orders/{orderId}/receipts` — log/list receipt generation events. `receiptType` records how the receipt came out: `PRINT` (the browser's dialog), `THERMAL` (ESC/POS to a receipt printer, 09.12), `SAVE` (kept as a file), `EMAIL` (sent; `emailedTo` required). The frontend renders the receipt itself.

### Alternative Sale Types
- `POST /gift-cards` — issue a gift card. `GET /gift-cards/{code}` — look one up. `POST /gift-cards/{code}/reload` — top up balance. `POST /gift-cards/{code}/redeem` — spend from balance; **idempotent per `orderId`** (a repeat redemption against the same order returns the card unchanged rather than deducting twice, matching how a `STORE_CREDIT` tender is keyed in payment-svc). A redemption sent without an `orderId` is a manual adjustment and is not deduplicated. `GET /gift-cards/{code}/transactions` — transaction history. **Issuing and reloading say how the value was paid for (17.11):** `POST /gift-cards` `{storeId, amount, currency?, expiresAt?, paidBy}` and `POST /gift-cards/{code}/reload` `{amount, reference?, paidBy}`, where `paidBy` is `CASH`, `CARD`, `UPI` or `WALLET`, or `PROMOTIONAL` for value given away; missing is `400`, and another gift card, a voucher, store credit or anything else is `400 GIFT_CARD_PAID_BY_INVALID` — stored value bought with stored value would only move a liability. Each issue and reload publishes `GiftCardLoaded` (`storeql.order.gift-card-loaded`: card, transaction, kind, amount, currency, paidBy, store) with the card write, which purchase-svc posts against the gift card liability.
- `POST /layaways` — create a layaway (reserve goods against installment deposits). `GET /layaways/{id}` — get with items/deposits. `POST /layaways/{id}/deposits` — record a deposit. `POST /layaways/{id}/complete` — final payment received, hand over goods. `POST /layaways/{id}/cancel` — cancel.
- `POST /admin/special-orders` — create a special order (customer order for future delivery, no immediate stock deduction); `Idempotency-Key` header (or the body's `idempotencyKey`), a repeat is `409 SPECIAL_ORDER_DUPLICATE_KEY`. `GET /admin/special-orders`, `GET .../{id}` — list/get. `POST .../{id}/confirm`, `.../fulfil`, `.../cancel` — lifecycle actions.

**Business rules**
- Placing an order requires an `Idempotency-Key` (rejected with 400 otherwise) — the one hard requirement across the whole surface.
- `POS` channel orders require a `CASHIER`/`MANAGER`/`OWNER` role; `ONLINE` orders need no staff role — but the gateway only forwards order placement for a verified, signed-in customer token (there is no anonymous guest checkout). Only an `ONLINE` order is the caller's own: it carries their `loginId` and the customer record customer-svc links to it (SJ-D44). A `POS` sale names its customer only through `customerId`, whatever roles the cashier's token carries — every staff login carries `CUSTOMER` too, and linking on the role alone filed anonymous till sales under the cashier's own record (SJ-D59).
- Special orders deliberately skip immediate inventory deduction, unlike regular POS/online orders.
- Void, cancel, and return are three distinct lifecycle actions: void corrects a completed sale, cancel stops an unfulfilled order, return is a post-sale reversal.
- **A till sale is handed over the moment it is paid for (SJ-D40).** The capture that completes it moves it `PENDING → CONFIRMED → FULFILLED` in one transaction and writes `OrderFulfilled` alongside `OrderConfirmed`. Before this, inventory-svc deducted stock only on `OrderFulfilled` and nothing ever fulfilled a till sale — the till places the order, payment capture confirmed it, and there it stopped. Stock moved only if a manager later opened each sale and clicked *Mark fulfilled*. `PICKUP` counts as a till sale as well as `INSTORE` because the till sent `PICKUP` for every tendered sale until the fix, and sales already queued offline replay with it. `DELIVERY` is not: goods leaving on a van are handed over when they arrive. A partial tender or a redelivered capture writes nothing, so a sale is fulfilled — and deducted — exactly once.
- The POS stock-position projection is explicitly documented as eventually consistent and must not be used to make reservation decisions.

- **A customer this shop erased keeps their delivery details only until an open order finishes (SJ-D43).** `CustomerErased` redacts a settled order's contact/delivery fields and any parked-sale/special-order name at once; an order still in flight is left alone until it reaches a settled state (fulfilled, cancelled, voided, refunded) and is caught by an hourly sweep — the address is still needed to deliver it. The sale record itself (amounts, tax, lines) is never touched; only what identifies the customer is.

**Events**
- Consumes: `StockReceived`, `StockDeducted`, `StockAdjusted` (feeds the POS stock-position projection), `PaymentCaptured`, `PaymentFailed`, `PaymentRefunded` (order confirmation/refund status), `StoreStatusChanged`, `TenantStatusChanged`, `CustomerErased` (SJ-D43).
- Publishes: `OrderPlaced`, `OrderConfirmed`, `OrderCancelled`, `OrderFulfilled`, `OrderReturned`, `OrderVoided`, `LayawayCreated`, `LayawayCompleted`, `LayawayCancelled`.

---

### Fiscal receipts (`/admin/fiscal-receipts`)

A **gapless legal receipt sequence** — required by fiscal law in Italy, Germany (KassenSichV), Portugal, Poland, Brazil and India, and required to be demonstrable to an inspector. Distinct from `/admin/orders/{id}/receipts`, which logs how many times a document was printed or emailed; this is the document itself.

- `POST /admin/orders/{orderId}/fiscal-receipt?series=` — issue the numbered receipt. **Normally unnecessary**: the number is taken automatically when the sale completes — by the payment capture that completes a till sale, or by `confirm`. (The first version hooked only `confirm`, which the till never calls, and so numbered almost no till sales.) This is the recovery path for a sale that completed while issuance was failing, and it is idempotent — a second call returns the number already issued, because a reprint is not a sale and two numbers for one sale is how a day's takings get counted twice. Refused for a `PENDING` or `CANCELLED` order (`ORDER_NOT_SELLABLE`): numbering a basket that is never paid for is where gaps come from.
- `GET /admin/orders/{orderId}/fiscal-receipt` — the number, when it was issued, and whether the sale was later voided.
- `GET /orders/{orderId}/fiscal-receipt` — the same, authorized like the order it belongs to: any staff member, or the customer who placed it. In practice a staff read today: a signed-in customer's token carries no tenant, and the gateway derives one only for its storefront paths, which include `/orders/mine` but no read by id. The authorization filter runs at the gateway as well as in order-svc, so both had to be redeployed for this path to be reachable. This is what the till reads to print the legal number — the admin route above is management-only, so a cashier could never reach it. The number is issued when the payment that completes the sale reaches order-svc, so for a few seconds after a sale this answers `404 ORDER_RECEIPT_NOT_ISSUED`. **`?wait=N`** holds the request up to N seconds (capped at 20) for the number to be issued, so the till prints with the number after one round trip instead of polling and giving up; before payment lands a bounded wait still ends in `404`, and the cap is tested — asking for an hour gets twenty seconds. A customer who did not place the order gets the same `404`, at once.
- `GET /admin/fiscal-receipts/series?storeId=` — the counters a store runs: series code, fiscal period, the next number it will hand out, and the prefix it prints. Management-only; a cashier gets `403`, another tenant an empty list.
- `PUT /admin/fiscal-receipts/series` `{storeId, seriesCode, period, prefix}` — what is printed in front of the number, e.g. `GB-LDN-01` → `GB-LDN-01-2026-000042`. Opens the series if it is new. **The counter is never touched**: a prefix change affects the documents issued after it, and every document already issued keeps the full number it was printed with — the test issues under `OLD`, changes to `NEW`, and checks the count runs 1, 2. Prefix is letters, digits and hyphens, at most 16, upper-cased (`RECEIPT_PREFIX_INVALID`); period is a year, `2026` or `2026-04` (`RECEIPT_PERIOD_INVALID`); a refusal opens nothing. Management-only.
- `GET /admin/fiscal-receipts?storeId=&series=&period=` — the register, by number. `series` defaults to `MAIN`, `period` to the current fiscal year.
- `GET /admin/fiscal-receipts/export?storeId=&series=&period=&format=csv|json` — the register as a file (18.4): every document in number order with `prevHash` and `hash`; JSON adds the order lines behind each document. The in-repo input an accountant or a SAF-T / KassenSichV / corrispettivi export needs; **not itself a certified file** — those need a tax-authority signing key, a certified TSE, or an SDI channel this repo cannot supply. Management-only.
- `GET /admin/fiscal-receipts/settings?storeId=` / `PUT /admin/fiscal-receipts/settings` `{storeId, regime, taxRegistrationNumber, certificateNumber, seriesValidationCode, tseProvider, tseTssId, tseClientId}` — **the fiscal regime a store trades under (18.5)**, behind a seam the way payments are: `NONE` (the register alone, every store's start), `DE_KASSENSICHV` (every sale is a transaction a security module signs — §146a AO, KassenSichV §2/§6; the receipt prints the module's serial, transaction number, signature counter, start and end, signature and the DSFinV-K QR; an outage is recorded on the document, which is what `TSE_TA_FEHLER` is for) or `PT_SAFT` (every document carries an RSA-SHA1 signature over `InvoiceDate;SystemEntryDate;InvoiceNo;GrossTotal;PreviousHash` per Despacho 8632/2014, computed under the same lock as its number so the chain is as gapless as the numbers; the receipt prints characters 1, 11, 21 and 31 and "Processado por programa certificado n.º NNNN/AT"). The read returns what the deployment offers beside the settings: the regimes deployed, the module providers configured (`SIMULATED` always — a module in software whose key lives in `tse_devices`, which is exactly why it is not certified; `CLOUD` when `storeql.fiscal.tse.cloud.api-key/api-secret` are set — a fiskaly-shaped REST module whose certificate covers the signing), and whether the Portuguese signing key (`storeql.fiscal.pt.private-key`) is installed. What each regime refuses, by code: `FISCAL_REGIME_UNKNOWN`, `FISCAL_TAX_NUMBER_REQUIRED`, `FISCAL_TSE_REQUIRED`, `FISCAL_TSE_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN`, `FISCAL_TSE_CLIENT_ID_INVALID`, `FISCAL_TSE_ID_REQUIRED`, `FISCAL_TSE_CLOUD_NOT_CONFIGURED` (409), `FISCAL_NIF_INVALID` (mod-11 check), `FISCAL_SERIES_CODE_INVALID`, `FISCAL_PT_KEY_NOT_CONFIGURED` (409), `STORE_NOT_OPERATIONAL` (409, another tenant's store). A refusal stores nothing. Documents already issued keep the stamps they were issued with; the change applies from the next sale, and the audit's hash chain covers the stamp (a swapped device signature breaks it at that document). Management-only; another tenant reads its own unset view of the store id.
- `GET /admin/fiscal-receipts/export?…&format=dsfinvk|saft-pt` — **the inspector's file for the regime (18.5).** `dsfinvk` is the German DSFinV-K 2.3 zip: `cashpointclosing`, `location`, `cashregister`, `tse`, `vat`, `businesscases`, `payment`, `transactions`, `transactions_tse`, `transactions_vat`, `lines`, `lines_vat`, `datapayment` and the GDPdU `index.xml` describing them — semicolon-separated, decimal comma, CRLF, one cash-point closing per day the store traded (this register has no separate end-of-day document, so the day is the closing); not written: the tables for agencies, vouchers, sub-items, allocation groups and references, which this register has nothing to put in. `saft-pt` is the Portuguese SAF-T (PT) 1.04_01 sales-invoice file: header, the final consumer (NIF 999999990), the products the lines name, the tax table, and every `Invoice` with its `Hash`, `HashControl`, `ATCUD` (the AT series validation code joined to the number; `0-n` until the series is registered), lines by rate and the payment mechanism. Both name the business as tenant-svc holds it (legal name via `GET /admin/tenant`, the store's address via `GET /admin/stores/{id}`, the caller's identity forwarded) and the products as product-svc names them, and refuse with `503 FISCAL_EXPORT_DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` rather than write a file naming nobody. Lines carry `vat_amount` since 18.5 (what the quote priced; apportioned from the order's tax when a line was placed with pricing enforcement off), so both files list the sale by rate. **What is code and what is not:** the German module's certificate and the Portuguese producer's certification are market gates a deployment passes with configuration — the cloud provider's credentials, the AT-issued key and certificate number — not with code; the software half of each, including the file an inspector reads, is here and driven live in `k6/compliance-flow`.
- `GET /admin/fiscal-receipts/audit?storeId=&series=&period=` — **the inspector's question, answered by the database rather than by assertion.** Returns the first and last numbers, how many were issued, how many the span implies, and every gap with its range. `intact: true` with an empty `gaps` array is the proof. Since 18.4 the same call gives a second, independent verdict: every document issued carries a SHA-256 over its own figures and the hash of the document before it (`prevHash`, `GENESIS` for the first), and the audit re-derives every hash in the series — `chainIntact`, `chainFrom` (documents issued before the chain existed are passed over), `chainBrokenAt` (the first document whose stored figures no longer match). A missing number and an altered figure are different findings, and now each has its own line. Contiguous holes come back as one gap with a range, because "2 to 3" is what gets explained, not two separate findings.

**Why not a Postgres `SEQUENCE`.** Sequences deliberately do not roll back: two transactions take 41 and 42, the first aborts, and 41 is gone forever. That is right for a surrogate key and wrong for a legal document, where the missing number is exactly what an inspector asks about. The counter is a row updated with `UPDATE … RETURNING` inside the sale's own transaction, so a rollback puts the number back and concurrent tills serialise on the row lock. That trade — concurrency for gaplessness — is the one every fiscal system makes, and a test drives eight tills at once to prove it holds.

**A voided sale keeps its number**, marked void with a reason. Deleting or renumbering it would close the hole, and closing the hole is the trick the numbering exists to expose: ring the sale, take the cash, void the receipt, and a till that balances hides a theft.

Numbering restarts per `period` (fiscal year) and runs per `(store, series)`, so a jurisdiction that numbers per till — Italy does — uses one series per device.

## payment-svc

### Invoices and credit notes to business buyers (`/admin/sales-invoices`)
**18.9.** A sale to a customer recorded as a VAT-registered business is owed an invoice — in Belgium, France, Poland and (from 2027) Germany a structured EN 16931 one, in India one reported to the Invoice Registration Portal, in the UK a full VAT invoice on request — and the till's receipt is not it: it names no buyer and is numbered per store. Issued **without anyone asking** when the sale completes (on `PaymentCaptured` for a till sale, on `POST /orders/{id}/confirm` otherwise), in the background so the till never waits on four services; and again for a return, as a credit note. The business is named as tenant-svc's identity holds it (`legalName`, `vatNumber`, `einvoiceScheme`/`einvoiceId`) at the store's address; the buyer as pricing-svc's `customer-vat-status` records it (`legalName`, `vatNumber`, `countryCode`, `reverseChargeEligible`, its e-invoicing address) at its customer-svc `BILLING` address (else its default); every line at the rate its quote applied (`order_items.vat_code`/`vat_rate`, recorded at placement), named as product-svc names it, with its HSN code.
