Numbered gaplessly per series and calendar year (`INV/2026/000001`, `CRN/2026/000001`; short enough for the IRP's 16 characters) from `sales_invoice_series`, moved under its row lock inside the issuing transaction: a refusal gives its number back, and ten requests at once for one sale answer with one document. The document is checked against EN 16931 — and Peppol BIS Billing 3.0 when both parties have a network address and the order is referenced — before its number is kept, and stored as the UBL issued; CII and Factur-X are written from it on request. A till discount is taken off what the customer pays, VAT included, after the tax was worked out; the invoice states it **net** (`SalesInvoiceDraft.netDiscount`), as an allowance on every rate it came off, so the VAT follows what was actually paid and the document comes to exactly what the sale charged — a sale whose recorded tax does not follow from its lines' rates is refused rather than made to look as if it did. An Indian business's document is also written as the IRP's INV-01 v1.1 (CGST+SGST within a state, IGST across; discounts spread into the lines, since the portal takes no document allowance), or kept with what the portal would refuse.
- `POST /admin/orders/{orderId}/invoice` — issue the invoice by hand, or read back the one issued: idempotent. `404 ORDER_NOT_FOUND`; `409 ORDER_INVOICE_NOT_SOLD` (a basket never paid for), `ORDER_INVOICE_NO_BUYER`, `ORDER_INVOICE_BUYER_NOT_REGISTERED`, `ORDER_INVOICE_BUYER_NOT_FOUND`, `ORDER_INVOICE_BUYER_ADDRESS_MISSING`, `ORDER_INVOICE_SELLER_VAT_MISSING`, `ORDER_INVOICE_SELLER_NAME_MISSING`, `ORDER_INVOICE_STORE_NOT_FOUND`, `ORDER_INVOICE_TOTALS_DIFFER`, `ORDER_INVOICE_NOT_COMPLIANT` (the rules broken in `details`); `503 ORDER_INVOICE_DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` when pricing-svc, customer-svc, product-svc or tenant-svc could not be reached — the document can be issued once it is. Management-only, like everything under `/admin`.
- `GET /admin/orders/{orderId}/invoices` — the sale's invoice and credit notes, newest first; empty when never invoiced.
- `POST /admin/returns/{returnId}/credit-note` — credit a return against an invoiced sale, by hand or read back: the returned goods at the rates they were sold at, the invoice named as the preceding document (BG-3). `404 ORDER_RETURN_NOT_FOUND`; `409 ORDER_CREDIT_NOTE_NO_INVOICE`.
- `GET /admin/sales-invoices?after=&limit=` · `GET /admin/sales-invoices/{id}` — the register, newest first, cursor-paged on `(issued_at, id)`; one document's facts (`kind`, `fullNumber`, amounts, `peppol`, the `formats` it offers, its `irpProblems`). Another tenant's is `404`.
- `GET /admin/sales-invoices/{id}/document?format=UBL|CII|FACTURX|IRP` — the file, with `Content-Disposition` (named by the number), `nosniff` and `no-store`: the UBL as issued (default), the same document as CII, a Factur-X PDF/A-3, or the INV-01 JSON. `400 ORDER_INVOICE_FORMAT_UNKNOWN`; `404 ORDER_INVOICE_IRP_NOT_APPLICABLE` outside India; `409 ORDER_INVOICE_IRP_NOT_READY` with the portal's objections in `details`. Sending over a network (Peppol access point, France's PDP, Poland's KSeF, the IRP itself) is the transport seam, not built yet.

### Where e-invoices leave (`/admin/einvoicing/transport`, the transport seam)

**The seam behind 07.13 and 18.9.** An EN 16931 document is only the format; the mandates name a network. A business chooses one — `PEPPOL` (an access point: Belgium now, Germany 2027, the UK 2029), `FR_PDP` (France's approved platform), `KSEF` (Poland), `IRP` (India) — and the provider behind it: `SIMULATED` on every network (the platform stands in for the network and nothing leaves it; a receiver whose identifier contains `REJECT` is refused as unknown, one containing `LATER` is taken and answered when asked after), or a real one once the deployment holds its credentials, each deployed always and choosable only configured: the Peppol `ACCESS_POINT` (the HTTP facade certified providers put in front of AS4: `storeql.einvoice.peppol.base-url`, `.api-key`), France's approved platform `PDP` (`storeql.einvoice.fr-pdp.base-url`, `.api-key`; the document deposited with the business's SIREN as its account and the buyer's directory address, its life read back as the reform's statuses — 200 déposée and 201 émise on their way, 210 refusée by the buyer and 213 rejetée by the platform final, every other status with the buyer's platform), and India's `NIC` portal (`storeql.einvoice.irp.base-url`, `.client-id`, `.client-secret`, `.public-key`; the business signs in as the taxpayer with its portal user as its account and its password as its **secret** — the password and a fresh AppKey sealed under the portal's RSA key, the session key back sealed under the AppKey, the INV-01 AES-sealed with it, and the IRN, acknowledgement, signed invoice and QR code back the same way; an invoice already registered is registered with the portal's own IRN; wrong credentials are refused, not retried). A provider's credential (`providerSecret`) is kept sealed under the deployment's `storeql.einvoice.secrets-key` (AES-GCM) and never shown; `hasSecret` says one is kept, leaving it out keeps it, blank removes it; `409 EINVOICE_SECRETS_KEY_MISSING` on a deployment with no key, `409 EINVOICE_PROVIDER_SECRET_REQUIRED` for a provider that signs in without one. Poland's `KSEF` (KSeF API 2.0, `storeql.einvoice.ksef.base-url`; the business's KSeF token as its **secret**, its NIP from its VAT number): KSeF alone takes no EN 16931 document, so the invoice as issued is read back and written as **FA(3)** (`shared/einvoice` `Fa3`: the header naming schema FA (3) 1-0E, Podmiot1 by NIP, Podmiot2 by NIP, EU VAT number or none, the totals per rate bucket P_13_x/P_14_x, the annotations, VAT or KOR naming the corrected invoice, one FaWiersz per line with its rate as the schema spells it — 23, 8, 5, 0 KR, zw, oo, np I — and a document allowance spread over its lines as P_10; a credit note's lines are the differences, negative; `Fa3.check` refuses what KSeF would: a seller without a valid NIP, a rate Poland has not); the token is sealed with the challenge's timestamp under the ministry's RSA-OAEP key, the access token opens an interactive session whose AES key is sealed under the ministry's other key, the FA(3) goes in AES-256-CBC-encrypted with its hashes and sizes, the session is closed, and the system is asked after by session and invoice reference until it gives the KSeF number (`ACCEPTED`, the reference becoming the number), refuses (4xx with its description and details, final), reports a duplicate (its number) or fails on its own side (5xx, asked again). A contract with a certified provider is not code. **Delivery in** is the other half of the seam: a network delivers a supplier's document to purchase-svc's `POST /e-invoices/inbound/{network}` (below), and on the simulated network, delivered means delivered — when the deployment holds `storeql.einvoice.inbound.key`, the simulated provider hands every document it takes to that route as an access point would, so a receiver that is a business on this platform finds it in its inbox (`ACCEPTED`, "delivered into the receiver's inbox on this platform", the inbox's receipt kept in the attempt's `response`), a receiver nobody here holds is delivered the simulated way (`ACCEPTED`, "no business on this platform holds …"), an inbox that cannot be reached or refuses the key is a network that is down (tried again later), and an inbox that refuses the document is a refusal with its reason. Every document issued while a network is chosen is queued (`sales_invoice_transmissions`) and sent by a worker (every 15 s, `storeql.order.einvoice-transport.*`) that takes due rows under `SKIP LOCKED`; a network that could not be reached is tried again, later each time (30 s doubling, an hour at most, 8 attempts, then `FAILED`); a network that took the document but has not answered is asked after (`PENDING`); a network that refused is `REJECTED` for good, until someone sends the document again by hand. The database holds one open or delivered attempt per document, so ten sends at once send once.
- `GET /admin/einvoicing/transport` — the settings: `network`, `provider`, `providerAccount`, the business's `senderAddress`, `suggestedNetwork` (what tenant-svc's obligations say the business's country asks for today), the `networks`, the `providers` deployed per network and the `available` ones (configured). Management-only.
- `PUT /admin/einvoicing/transport` `{network, provider, providerAccount, providerSecret}` — choose; `NONE` clears. The response also lists the providers `needingSecret`, by network. `400 EINVOICE_NETWORK_UNKNOWN`, `EINVOICE_PROVIDER_REQUIRED`, `EINVOICE_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN`, `EINVOICE_PROVIDER_ACCOUNT_INVALID`; `409 EINVOICE_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `EINVOICE_SENDER_ADDRESS_MISSING` (Peppol without the business's own electronic address).
- `GET /admin/sales-invoices/{id}/transmissions` — every attempt for a document, newest first; `POST /admin/sales-invoices/{id}/transmissions` — send now, the first time or again after a refusal; the answer is the attempt with what the network said (`ACCEPTED`, `REJECTED` with `detail`, `PENDING`). `409 EINVOICE_TRANSPORT_NOT_SET`, `EINVOICE_ALREADY_SENT`, `EINVOICE_RECEIVER_ADDRESS_MISSING`, `EINVOICE_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN`. The document itself (`GET /admin/sales-invoices…`) carries its newest `transmission`.
- `GET /admin/einvoicing/transmissions?status=&after=&limit=` — the outbox, newest first, cursor-paged; `400 EINVOICE_TRANSMISSION_STATUS_UNKNOWN`.
- `GET /admin/einvoicing/readiness` — **what stands between this business and its first e-invoice, with the network asked rather than assumed.** A checklist (`checks[]`: `NETWORK_CHOSEN`, `SELLER_VAT_ID`, `SENDER_ADDRESS`, `PROVIDER_DEPLOYED`, `CREDENTIAL_HELD`, each `satisfied` with a `detail` that says what to do when it is not), plus — once everything holds and the provider is deployed — the provider **tried with the credentials the business actually holds**: `networkState` `READY`, `UNREACHABLE` or `REFUSED`, with `networkDetail` in the network's own terms, and `ready` true only when both the list and the network agree. Nothing is sent: a Peppol access point is asked whether it knows the participant, France's platform whether it knows the taxpayer, KSeF and India's portal sign in and stop. The distinction is the point — a key someone must fix (`REFUSED`) and a network that is down (`UNREACHABLE`) have different remedies, and "it did not work" is what a shop already knows; a check never throws, so whatever the network does is reported as what it did. The simulated provider answers `READY` and says what ready means there: documents are taken and reach a receiver on this platform, and nothing leaves it. Management-only.

**Fetching, where nothing is delivered (`/admin/e-invoices/inbox`).** Every network above delivers; **KSeF does not**. A Polish buyer's invoices sit in the ministry's system until the buyer asks, so a platform that only listens receives nothing in Poland however correct the rest of it is — and what comes back is **FA(3)**, not an EN 16931 document. `shared/einvoice`'s `Fa3Reader` reads Poland's structure into the same model a UBL or CII document produces, so the checks, the supplier matching, the three-way match and the posting are the ones every other invoice gets; the document is kept with `syntax: FA3` and `channel: KSEF`, and is validated against **FA(3)'s own rules** rather than Peppol's, which it was never written to. `GET`/`PUT /admin/e-invoices/inbox/settings` `{network, provider, providerAccount, secret?}` choose where a business fetches from — `KSEF` with the real ministry API, or `SIMULATED`, the platform standing in, which says plainly that nothing left it rather than answering as an empty success. The business's KSeF token is sealed under the deployment's key (`storeql.einvoice.secrets-key`, shared with the sending side so a rotated key is not a secret unreadable in the other half) and never returned; the account is the business's NIP, checked. `POST /admin/e-invoices/inbox/fetch?from=&to=` asks for a window and takes everything in it: the window **carries on from the last fetch** (a range, not a cursor — a range can be asked again safely because the inbox knows a document by its bytes, where a lost cursor leaves a gap nobody can see), a document already held is counted and not taken twice, one the inbox will not take is named while the rest are still taken, and a network that goes away partway leaves the window where it reached. Refusals: `400 PURCHASE_INBOX_NETWORK_UNKNOWN` / `PURCHASE_INBOX_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN` / `PURCHASE_INBOX_ACCOUNT_INVALID` / `PURCHASE_INBOX_WINDOW_INVALID`, `409 PURCHASE_INBOX_NOT_DEPLOYED` / `PURCHASE_INBOX_SECRET_REQUIRED` / `PURCHASE_INBOX_NOT_SET`, `422 PURCHASE_INBOX_NETWORK_DOWN` / `PURCHASE_INBOX_REFUSED`. `GET /admin/e-invoices/inbox/readiness` asks the same question of the fetching side and fetches nothing: `outstanding[]` names what is missing in the order worth doing it, and once nothing is, the ministry is **signed in to and stopped there** — `networkState` `READY`, `UNREACHABLE` (the system down: wait) or `REFUSED` (the token refused, or the platform standing in, where nothing arrives however long a shop waits), with `networkDetail`. The window is not moved by a check, so the next fetch still asks from where the last one reached. Management-only.

### E-reporting (`/admin/ereporting`)

France's reform has two limbs and 18.9 built one. The other is **e-reporting**: the transactions no e-invoice covers — a sale to a shopper, a sale abroad — transmitted to the administration through the same platform, plus the payment data for services. A shop selling to the public issues no e-invoices at all and still owes a report three times a month, so a platform with only the invoice limb leaves a French retailer in breach from 1 Sep 2026. Management only.

The **calendar is tenant-svc's** (07.14): `EREPORTING_TX_FR` and `EREPORTING_PAY_FR` are two returns on the statutory calendar at the new `DECADAL` frequency — three ten-day periods a month, each due ten days after it ends — derived on read, with the filing recorded there as for every other return. This service holds the data and the transmission.

- `GET /admin/ereporting/preview?return=&from=&to=&currency=` — **what a period would report, before anything is sent**: each day with its operation count and the money split by VAT rate, the cross-border sales one by one, and every currency the period took money in. The count sits on the **day**, not on each rate, because a basket with a zero-rated loaf and a standard-rated bottle is one transaction. `nothingToReport` says the period is empty — which is still reportable.
- `POST /admin/ereporting/submissions` `{returnCode, periodStart, periodEnd, currency?, corrects?}` — builds the period from this service's own sales, keeps the document byte for byte, and deposits it with the platform the business sends on. **Scope is decided by the absence of an e-invoice** (`NOT EXISTS` on `sales_invoices`), not by a judgement about the buyer: the invoice is the evidence that the other limb already carries the sale. The day a sale falls in is its **first transition to `FULFILLED`** in the append-only history, never `updated_at`, which a later edit would move. A **refund is a negative operation** on the day it happened, split by the rate of the line it returns (the refund amount is the line's net, so the VAT is computed from the rate rather than divided out). An **empty period transmits a `néant`** rather than nothing, because silence is indistinguishable from a platform that stopped working. The answer carries the payload's SHA-256 — put that on the filing recorded against the calendar and the two can be tied together later. Refusals: `400 EREPORTING_RETURN_UNKNOWN`, `400 EREPORTING_PERIOD_INVALID` (a period that has not ended, or longer than a month — a mistyped date must not sweep a year into one filing that looks complete), `409 EREPORTING_NOT_DUE` (asked of tenant-svc's obligations and **failing closed**, unlike a plan limit: reporting to a network that never asked for it puts trading data where it does not belong), `409 EREPORTING_TRANSPORT_NOT_SET`, `409 EREPORTING_NETWORK_CANNOT_REPORT` (only France's platform has an e-reporting flow; a Peppol access point carries invoices, and silently accepting a report would leave a business believing it had reported), `409 EREPORTING_ALREADY_SUBMITTED`.
- `GET /admin/ereporting/submissions` · `GET …/{id}` · `GET …/{id}/document` — what was reported, and the document as transmitted. A submission is **never edited**: a correction supersedes it and both stay on the record, keyed per period, stream **and currency**, since a business that took money in two currencies owes a report for each.

---

### Payments & Refunds (`/payments`)
- `POST /payments` — record a payment tender against an order (POS, staff-only). **Cash limit (09.17):** a `CASH` tender that would bring the cash taken for the sale — this tender plus every cash tender already captured on the order, since pieces for the same goods are one payment — to the limit in force where the store trades (the store's country, else the business's own; the register's lowest limit in the tender's currency) is refused `409 PAYMENT_CASH_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, naming the law, the amount and what was already taken; the balance goes another way. The limits come from tenant-svc's obligations sheet through `Jurisdictions.cashLimit`.
- `POST /payments/terminal` `{terminalId, orderId, amount, currency}` + `Idempotency-Key` — **take a card on an EMV terminal (07.16)**. Sends the amount to the pinpad and returns what it said. Until this row a `CARD` tender was recorded because a cashier said so; nothing asked a terminal whether the card was approved, and nothing kept what a card receipt must carry. **The card number never reaches the platform:** the terminal performs the EMV transaction itself and returns a verdict, which is what keeps payment-svc out of PCI-DSS scope — `terminal_payments` has no column for a PAN beyond the four digits a receipt may print, and `TerminalService.refuseCardData` refuses anything card-shaped (Luhn-valid, 13–19 digits) arriving by any route, as belt to the gateway's braces. **The attempt is recorded, with its key, BEFORE the device is asked**, so a retried press returns the *first* attempt rather than starting a second EMV transaction on a real card — the canonical double-charge, since the screen does not change and the cashier presses again. Answers `201` with the attempt: `state` is `REQUESTED`, `APPROVED`, `DECLINED`, `CANCELLED`, `FAILED` or `TIMED_OUT`, plus `scheme`, `panLast4`, `authCode`, `aid`, `applicationLabel`, `entryMode` (`CHIP`/`CONTACTLESS`/`SWIPE`/`MANUAL`), `verification` (`PIN`/`SIGNATURE`/`NONE`/`DEVICE`), `providerRef`, `outcomeDetail` and `receiptLine` (assembled once, so every printer agrees). **Only `APPROVED` records a tender.** `TIMED_OUT` is not a decline — the card *may* have been charged, so no tender is recorded, nothing is retried automatically, and the attempt keeps its `providerRef` because that is how the charge is traced in the acquirer's settlement file. Refusals: `400 TERMINAL_AMOUNT_INVALID` (not positive, or more than two decimal places — refused, never rounded), `400 TERMINAL_CARD_DATA_NOT_ACCEPTED`, `404 TERMINAL_NOT_FOUND`, `409 TERMINAL_RETIRED`, `409 TERMINAL_VENDOR_UNAVAILABLE`, `409 TERMINAL_REQUEST_IN_FLIGHT` (two identical presses racing — ask again for the outcome). `CASHIER`/`MANAGER`/`OWNER`.
- `POST /payments/terminal/{id}/refunds` `{amount}` — **put money back on the card that paid.** Linked to the original attempt, never to a card presented again: an unlinked refund is how card fraud is done, and most acquirers refuse them outright. `409 TERMINAL_NOT_APPROVED` against an attempt that took nothing, `409 TERMINAL_REFUND_TOO_LARGE` beyond what it took. Management only, like every refund here.
- `POST /payments/terminal/{id}/cancel` — stop the device asking for a card, for a tender the cashier abandoned. Best effort: the state moves once out of `REQUESTED` and never back, so a cancel racing an approval **loses** and the attempt comes back approved.
- `GET /payments/terminal/by-order/{orderId}` / `GET /payments/terminal/{id}` — every attempt against an order, declines included, oldest first: a declined card then a cash tender reads in the order it happened, which is what a cashier and an auditor both need.
- `GET /admin/payments/terminals` · `POST /admin/payments/terminals` `{storeId, label, vendor, serial?}` · `POST /admin/payments/terminals/{id}/retire` `{reason?}` · `GET /admin/payments/terminals/vendors` — **the register** (management). A terminal belongs to a **store**: it is a physical object on a counter and a payment taken on it is taken there. `vendor` is `SIMULATED`, `STRIPE_TERMINAL`, `ADYEN` or `VERIFONE`, and only vendors whose credentials are configured are offered — said at registration rather than at the till with a customer waiting. One label per store's active terminals and one serial per vendor, both by partial unique index. Retiring never deletes: payments point at the device, and its label is freed for the one that replaces it. `SIMULATED` is always deployed and says on the record that nothing left the building; it can **decline, be cancelled, time out and fail**, chosen from the amount's minor units (`.01`/`.02`/`.03`/`.04`) as the acquirers' own test-amount convention — a simulator that only approves is how a broken decline path ships.
- **Two resource classes, not one, and it matters:** the register is rooted at `/admin/payments/terminals` and the till at `/payments/terminal`. JAX-RS picks one resource class by the best match on its **root** path and then resolves the method only inside it, so a single class rooted at `/` with absolute method paths left every `/payments/terminal/…` route 404 — `PaymentResource` owns `/payments` and beat it. Do not merge them back.
- `POST /payments/online` — capture an online storefront payment (cashless only — cash is explicitly rejected here). Name the order (`orderId`) or, for a delivery split across shops, the checkout (`groupId`) — not both (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). **A checkout is paid once** (order orchestration): the claim is checked against order-svc's `GET /order-groups/{id}` — the caller's own (`404 PAYMENT_GROUP_NOT_FOUND`), every part still `PENDING` (`409 PAYMENT_ORDER_NOT_PAYABLE`), the amount the checkout's total (`400 PAYMENT_GROUP_AMOUNT_MISMATCH`), `CARD`/`UPI`/`WALLET` only — and one tender is captured per part (its total, its store, a key derived per part) on one transaction, each announcing its own `PaymentCaptured` so each part confirms; the answer is `{groupId, total, tenders[]}` and a retry replays the tenders. A part is never paid alone: `409 PAYMENT_ORDER_IN_GROUP`. A refund stays per part — each tender is its order's. The acquirer's one settlement line for the checkout matches one part's tender by reference and shows as an amount mismatch to resolve by hand.
- `GET /payments/{id}` — get a payment tender.
- `GET /payments/by-order/{orderId}` — list all tenders for an order.
- `POST /payments/by-order/{orderId}/refunds`, `GET /payments/by-order/{orderId}/refunds` — record/list refunds against an order's captured tender.

### Chargebacks (`/admin/disputes`, 11.9)
OWNER or MANAGER throughout.
- `GET /admin/disputes?status=&storeId=&after=&limit=` — the register, newest first. `status` is `NEEDS_RESPONSE`, `UNDER_REVIEW`, `WON`, `LOST` or `ACCEPTED` (`400 DISPUTE_STATUS_UNKNOWN`). Each row says `overdue` when an answer is still owed past its date.
- `POST /admin/disputes` — record a chargeback the acquirer has told the business about, for a card taken on a terminal the platform does not talk to: `{paymentId, amount?, feeAmount?, currency?, reason, networkReasonCode?, caseReference, evidenceDueBy, fundsWithdrawn?}` with an `Idempotency-Key` (a retry is the same chargeback). `404 PAYMENT_NOT_FOUND`; `409 DISPUTE_NOT_DISPUTABLE` (only a captured card, UPI or wallet tender), `DISPUTE_ALREADY_OPEN` (one open dispute per payment); `400 DISPUTE_AMOUNT_EXCEEDS_PAYMENT`, `DISPUTE_DUE_DATE_PAST`, `DISPUTE_REASON_UNKNOWN`. A payment provider's own disputes arrive on `POST /payments/webhooks/{provider}` and are never recorded this way.
- `GET /admin/disputes/{id}` — the dispute with its append-only history (`OPENED`, `FUNDS_WITHDRAWN`, `EVIDENCE_SUBMITTED`, `WON`/`LOST`/`ACCEPTED`, `FUNDS_REINSTATED`) and the answer given.
- `POST /admin/disputes/{id}/evidence` — the business's answer: `{productDescription?, customerName?, customerEmail?, receiptReference?, fulfilmentProof?, customerCommunication?, refundPolicy?, notes?}`. Once, and not after its date: `409 DISPUTE_NOT_AWAITING_RESPONSE`, `DISPUTE_EVIDENCE_LATE`; `400 DISPUTE_EVIDENCE_EMPTY`. A provider's dispute is sent to the provider and submitted (`502 PAYMENT_PROVIDER_REFUSED` if it will not take it); the acquirer's is kept here and sent by the business.
- `POST /admin/disputes/{id}/accept` — not contested: lost by the business's own decision. `409 DISPUTE_CLOSED`.
- `POST /admin/disputes/{id}/resolve` `{outcome: WON|LOST, note?}` — how a dispute the acquirer told the business about ended. `409 DISPUTE_DECIDED_BY_PROVIDER` for a provider's dispute, `DISPUTE_CLOSED`; `400 DISPUTE_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`.
- `GET /admin/disputes/summary?from=&to=&storeId=` — opened, by standing, amounts disputed and lost, fees, the card payments of the period, `disputeRatio` and `aboveMonitoringThreshold` (0.9%, where the card schemes start monitoring a merchant).

### Settlement reconciliation (`/admin/settlements`, 11.10)
OWNER or MANAGER throughout. The gateway gives `POST /admin/settlements` its own 6 MB body cap; a file carrying a full card number is refused there (`400 CARD_DATA_NOT_ACCEPTED`) like any other request.
- `POST /admin/settlements` — import one payout's file: `{provider, format, reference?, payoutDate?, currency?, declaredNet?, storeId?, content}`. `format` is one of `GET /admin/settlements/formats` (`STOREQL`, `STRIPE`, `ADYEN`). What the file says about itself — payout number, date, currency, the sum paid — wins over what is typed; `STOREQL` says none of it, so `reference` and `payoutDate` are needed. Takes an `Idempotency-Key`. `201` with the batch: `RECONCILED` when every line matched, else `EXCEPTIONS` with `openExceptions`. `400 SETTLEMENT_FORMAT_UNKNOWN`, `SETTLEMENT_FILE_WRONG_LAYOUT`, `SETTLEMENT_FILE_INVALID` (says which line), `SETTLEMENT_FILE_MANY_PAYOUTS`, `SETTLEMENT_REFERENCE_MISSING`, `SETTLEMENT_PAYOUT_DATE_MISSING`/`_INVALID`, `SETTLEMENT_CURRENCY_MIXED` (not the business's currency, or a converted line), `SETTLEMENT_OUT_OF_BALANCE` (the lines do not add up to `declaredNet`), `SETTLEMENT_STORE_UNKNOWN`; `409 SETTLEMENT_ALREADY_IMPORTED`; `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`.
- The `STOREQL` layout: CSV columns `type, reference, original_reference, gross, fee, net, occurred_at` (`fee`, `net`, `original_reference`, `occurred_at` optional). `type` is `SALE`, `REFUND`, `CHARGEBACK`, `CHARGEBACK_REVERSAL`, `FEE` or `ADJUSTMENT`; amounts are signed from the business's side — a refund or a chargeback is negative — and `net = gross - fee`.
- `GET /admin/settlements?status=&after=&limit=` — payouts, the newest import first; `status` is `EXCEPTIONS`, `READY` or `RECONCILED` (`400 SETTLEMENT_STATUS_UNKNOWN`).
- `GET /admin/settlements/{id}?open=&after=&limit=` — the batch with a page of its lines in file order (`limit` ≤ 500); `open=true` keeps to the lines waiting for a decision. Each line: `type`, `reference`, `gross`, `fee`, `net`, `matchStatus` (`MATCHED`, `UNMATCHED`, `AMOUNT_MISMATCH` with `expectedAmount`, `DUPLICATE`, `NOT_APPLICABLE` for a fee), what it matched (`tenderId` / `refundId` / `disputeId`), and the decision on it (`resolution`, `resolvedBy`, `resolvedAt`, `note`).
- `POST /admin/settlements/{id}/lines/{lineId}/resolve` `{resolution, targetId?, note?}` — `MATCHED_BY_HAND` (needs `targetId`: the payment for a sale, the refund, or the dispute; the sums must agree), `DIFFERENCE_ACCEPTED` (keeps the link, or takes `targetId`; needs `note`), `UNALLOCATED` (lets go of any link; its money goes to unallocated receipts; needs `note`). May be changed until the batch is reconciled. Answers the batch with its open lines. `404 SETTLEMENT_TARGET_NOT_FOUND` (not there, another business's, or cash); `409 SETTLEMENT_ALREADY_SETTLED`, `SETTLEMENT_AMOUNT_DIFFERS`, `SETTLEMENT_NOTHING_TO_ACCEPT`, `SETTLEMENT_LINE_NOT_AN_EXCEPTION`, `SETTLEMENT_RECONCILED`; `400 SETTLEMENT_TARGET_REQUIRED`, `SETTLEMENT_NOTE_REQUIRED`, `SETTLEMENT_RESOLUTION_UNKNOWN`.
- `POST /admin/settlements/{id}/reconcile` — sign a `READY` batch off; the ledger is told in the same transaction. `409 SETTLEMENT_HAS_EXCEPTIONS`, `SETTLEMENT_RECONCILED`.
- `GET /admin/settlements/unsettled?olderThanDays=&storeId=&after=&limit=` — captured card, UPI and wallet payments no settlement line has, the oldest first, with `daysOutstanding` (`olderThanDays` 0–365, 3 when absent).
- Publishes `SettlementReconciled` → `storeql.payment.settlement-reconciled`.

### Cash & Till Management
- `POST /admin/cash/till-sessions` — open a till session with an opening float.
- `GET /admin/cash/till-sessions/current?storeId=` — the caller's own open till at that store (the session they opened there and have not closed; the latest, should there be two), answered as the session. What a till screen resumes after a reload. A colleague's till, a closed one and another business's are never current: `404 TILL_SESSION_NOT_OPEN`. `400 INVALID_UUID` when `storeId` is missing or not a UUIDv7; `403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED` for a store the caller is not assigned to (checked before anything is looked up). CASHIER, MANAGER or OWNER; any other role is `403 FORBIDDEN`. The answer has the shape of `GET /admin/cash/till-sessions/{id}`; the path is matched before `{id}`, so `current` is never read as a session id.
- `GET /admin/cash/till-sessions/{id}` — get session status/float.
- `POST /admin/cash/till-sessions/{id}/drops` — record a mid-shift cash drop (safe drop).
- `GET /admin/cash/till-sessions/{id}/x-report` — X-report: read-only mid-day cash snapshot.
- `POST /admin/cash/till-sessions/{id}/close` — Z-report: end-of-day till close.
- `POST /admin/cash/movements`, `GET /admin/cash/movements` — record/list pay-in / pay-out (petty cash) movements against an open till.
- `POST /admin/cash/z-report`, `GET /admin/cash/z-report` — generate/retrieve the daily store-level Z-report reconciliation.

### Tender Mix (`/admin/reports`)
- `GET /admin/reports/tender-mix?from&to` — how the take split across payment methods: captured and refunded amounts per method, each method's share of the net, and a count of tenders that failed to capture. Refunds are subtracted **within their own method** rather than netted globally — a card sale refunded to store credit is not a zero-card day, and the split is what a merchant statement reconciles against. `shareOfNet` is null when the window's total is zero or negative, where a percentage would be misleading rather than merely odd.

**Business rules**
- Online storefront payments must be cashless (`CARD`/`UPI`/`WALLET`); cash tenders are POS-staff-only via the in-person endpoint.
- Online payment is verified against order-svc (order exists, is `ONLINE`, belongs to the caller if authenticated, amount matches the order total) before capture.
- Role tiers: `CASHIER`+ can record a POS tender (`POST /payments`). `/admin/cash/**` is on the filter's **staff-operable** tier, so `CASHIER` can reach till endpoints; `CashManagementResource` then allows `CASHIER` to open/get a till, while drops, X-report, and Z-report (close) stay `MANAGER`/`OWNER`. Cash movements (pay-in/pay-out) under `/admin/cash` also require `MANAGER`/`OWNER` at the resource layer.
- `Idempotency-Key` is honored on tender recording, refunds, and cash movements.

**Events**
- Consumes: `OrderReturned`, `OrderCancelled` (auto-refunds a previously captured, paid order).
- Publishes: `PaymentCaptured`, `PaymentFailed`, `PaymentRefunded`.

---

## pricing-svc

### Pricing (`/price-lists`, `/admin/price-lists`, `/admin/price-overrides`, `/prices`)

**Writes are management-only, reads are not.** Setting a price used to sit on `/price-lists`, outside `/admin/`, where `AdminAuthorizationFilter`'s mutation tier asks only for *some* staff role — so a CASHIER could create a price list and set what customers are charged (proved against the running stack: 201). The writes moved under `/admin/`; the reads stayed, because the POS and storefront need them and neither runs as management.

- `POST /admin/price-lists` — create a price list. `effectiveFrom` is an **ISO-8601 instant** (`2026-01-01T00:00:00Z`); the column is `TIMESTAMPTZ` and a bare date is refused with `INVALID_DATE`.
- `POST /admin/price-lists/{id}/items` — set a single variant's price on a list.
- `POST /admin/price-lists/{id}/items/batch` — set many variants' prices in one call (partial-failure tolerant).
- `POST /admin/price-lists/{id}/deactivate` · `POST /admin/price-lists/{id}/activate` — switch a price list off or on, `{"reason": "..."}` **required in both directions**. `409 PRICING_ALREADY_IN_STATE` if it is already in that state, so two people stopping the same list are not both told they did it. The resolve query filters on `active`, so a stopped list stops setting prices from the next request; orders already placed keep what they were charged. Before this, `price_lists.active` had no writer of any kind and a wrong price could only be corrected by editing the database.
- `GET /admin/price-lists/{id}/status-history` — append-only on/off trail, newest first: who switched it, when, and why.
- `GET /price-lists`, `GET /price-lists/{id}`, `GET /price-lists/{id}/items` — reads, open to any staff role.
- **Price zones (03.x).** `POST /admin/price-lists` takes an optional `zoneId`: the list then prices the stores of that price zone and no other (`400 PRICING_ZONE_UNKNOWN` for a zone that is not the business's). `POST /prices/resolve`, `/prices/resolve-batch` and `/prices/quote` are **store-aware**: with `storeId`, a list bound to the store's zone beats the tenant-wide list; a store in no zone, or no store, gets the tenant-wide list alone — never another zone's. Every list answer carries `zoneId` (null for tenant-wide).
- `POST /admin/price-overrides`, `GET /admin/price-overrides` — log/list staff-approved ad-hoc POS price overrides (an append-only audit trail, not a mutable price).
- `POST /prices/resolve` — compute the effective price + VAT breakdown for one variant/channel/quantity. Applies **line-level** promotions only: basket rules are excluded deliberately, because this answers "what does this item cost" for a product page and quoting a spend-threshold price against one item advertises a total the shopper will not be charged.
- `POST /prices/resolve-batch` — resolve prices for several lines in one call. Each line is still priced **independently**, so no basket rule can apply; use `/prices/quote` at checkout.
- `POST /prices/quote` — **price a whole basket.** Resolves every line, then runs the promotion engine over the basket as a unit, returning per-line net prices, the whole-basket discount, every promotion that applied, and the VAT computed after discounts (the basket discount is apportioned across lines by value first, so it is not VAT-free money). Takes `couponCodes`; any that do not apply come back in `rejectedCoupons` with a reason — `NO_SUCH_COUPON`, `NOT_APPLICABLE`, `COUPON_EXHAUSTED` or `COUPON_LIMIT_REACHED` — rather than being silently ignored. **Quoting never spends a coupon**: a basket is quoted on every change a shopper makes, so redemption is a separate call. A line's `qty` may be a fraction — a weighed, poured or measured line arrives as its weight or volume — and price-list tiers are matched as if it were at least one, so 0.375 kg is charged 0.375 of the list price rather than refused; a list holding only a bulk tier (say `minQty` 100) still gives one item, or half of one, no price (SJ-D55). **Shown in another currency (03.x):** `displayCurrency` on `/prices/resolve`, `/prices/resolve-batch` lines and `/prices/quote` adds `display {currency, rate, unitPrice, totalWithVat}` (or the basket's `{currency, rate, subtotal, totalDiscount, vatAmount, total}`) at the business's own rate — what a shopper sees, never what is charged; the price and the order stay in the business's currency. `400 FX_RATE_MISSING` when the business keeps no rate for it, `400 FX_CURRENCY_INVALID` for a code that is not a currency; the home currency displays as itself at a rate of 1.
- `GET /prices/currencies` — the currencies a shop can show prices in: `{home, currencies}`, the business's own first, then every currency it keeps a rate for. Public, like a price: what the storefront's picker offers.
- **Unit prices (03.13).** `POST /prices/resolve`, `/prices/resolve-batch` and every `/prices/quote` line carry `unitPricing` `{amount, unit, quantity, label}` — the price per kilogram, litre, metre, square metre or item (`KG`, `L`, `M`, `SQM`, `EA`; label *per kg*, *per litre*, *per metre*, *per m²*, *each*) of **what the shopper pays**: `totalWithVat` after line promotions on resolve, the line's net plus VAT per one on a quote — so a promotional price is unit-priced too, as the Price Marking Order 2004 (as amended, from 6 Apr 2026) and Directive 98/6/EC art.3 require. Rounded half-up to the currency's minor unit (whole yen for JPY). It is computed from the variant's measure, which product-svc announces as `VariantMeasured` (topic `storeql.catalog.variant-measured`) with a `version` taken under the variant's row lock; pricing-svc's projection (V10 on `catalogue_variants`) keeps a measure only when its version is newer, so an older announcement arriving late never stands. No declared measure means `unitPricing` is absent — never a guessed unit. Resolve also returns `unitPriceRequired`: whether `UNIT_PRICING` binds the business today by tenant-svc's jurisdiction rules (the UK row, or the EU regime's), taken as true when the rules cannot be read, so a price is never refused over it and a missing unit price is never excused.
- `POST /prices/shelf-labels` `{variantIds, storeId?, channel?}` — **shelf-edge labels** (staff): for each distinct variant (1–200), the regular selling price with its unit price and, while a promotion applies at that store and channel (POS by default), the promotional price with its own unit price and the promotion's name, plus `measureDeclared` and `unitPriceRequired`. A variant with no price in force is a label saying `priced: false`, not a failed batch. `400 PRICING_LABELS_INVALID` for none or more than 200; `400 INVALID_UUID` for an id that is not one. **Prior price (03.12):** while a promotion applies, each label also carries `priorPrice`, `priorPriceStatus`, `priorPriceRequired` and `reductionAnnounceable`; a label shows a was price only when that is true — and then the prior price, never the regular price, where art.6a binds.
- `GET /admin/unit-pricing/gaps` — **priced items that cannot show a unit price** (management): variants with a price in a list in force and no declared measure, with `catalogued` (whether product-svc has announced the variant at all) and `required`. The fix is the variant's net content and unit in product-svc.
- **The prior price of a reduction (03.12, Directive 98/6/EC art.6a).** pricing-svc records every price a shopper is offered — per variant, channel (`ONLINE`, `POS`) and, where a store-scoped promotion applies, store — in an append-only **applied-price ledger** (V11 `applied_prices`; UPDATE and DELETE refused by trigger). Every change that can move a price queues an evaluation in its own transaction (`price_evaluations`): a list price set (whose history is now kept, append-only, in `price_list_item_prices`), a list or promotion made, scoped or switched, a VAT rate or category, the catalogue feed; a scheduled start or end queues one for its moment. A worker (every 5 s; a price or VAT-category write also records its own change before it answers) takes each variant's evaluations **in order** — one is claimed only when nothing earlier for that variant, or for the whole tenant, is still queued — evaluates each **as the definitions stood at its moment** (lists, promotions, scopes and switches by when they were made and thrown, list prices from their history, on the database's clock) and appends a row only when the offer changed. What cannot be read as of an earlier moment — a VAT rate or category overwritten in place, or the catalogue where a category-scoped promotion lets it move a price — makes a still-queued earlier evaluation of the same variant **uncertain** (`uncertainSince`), as does an evaluation made visible after a newer one was worked; the next certain evaluation closes the span. `POST /prices/resolve` returns `priorPrice` (the lowest price applied, VAT included, in the 30 days before the reduction began), `priorPriceStatus`, `reductionStartedAt`, `priorPriceRequired` and `reductionAnnounceable`. Statuses: `ANNOUNCEABLE`; `NO_HISTORY` (reduced from its first recorded price); `SHORT_HISTORY` (under 30 days recorded before it — so in a bound business nothing is announceable for its first 30 days on the ledger); `NOT_LOWER` (it sold at this price or less in the window); `PENDING` (an evaluation covering it is still due); `UNCERTAIN` (the window crosses a span the ledger could not establish). A reduction increased step by step keeps the price before its first step **only where every bound country has taken up art.6a(5)** (`PRICE_REDUCTION_PROGRESSIVE`: Germany); elsewhere each step is measured against the 30 days before it. Whether art.6a binds is asked of **every country the offer reaches** — the business's and, at a store, the store's; with no store, every store's — so a British business with a German shop is bound there and online. Where it does not bind, a promotional price may be announced against the regular price.
- `GET /admin/prices/history?variantId=&limit=` — a variant's ledger, newest first (management): `channel`, `storeId`, `priced`, `price`, `netPrice`, `regularPrice`, `promotionName`, `appliedFrom`, `uncertainSince`, `recordedAt`, `cause`, and `pending` (evaluations due for the business). `400 INVALID_UUID`; another business's variant has no rows.
- `GET /admin/prices/reductions?channel=ONLINE|POS` — **every reduced price on offer**, as the ledger records it, with `priorPrice`, `priorPriceStatus`, `reductionStartedAt`, `priorPriceRequired`, `reductionAnnounceable` and `pending` (management). `400 PRICING_CHANNEL_INVALID`.
- `POST /prices/redemptions` — record that an order used these promotions, spending their usage caps. Idempotent on `(tenant, promotion, order)`, so a retried checkout or a replayed offline sale cannot burn a second use; `recorded: 0` is a successful replay, not a failure.

### Price zones and competitor-driven repricing (`/admin/price-zones`, `/admin/competitor-prices`, `/admin/repricing`; 03.x)

**A business does not charge the same everywhere.** A **price zone** is the group of stores that price alike; a price list bound to it (`zoneId`) is what those stores charge, and every other store falls back to the tenant-wide list. A store sits in **one zone at most**, so the price a till charges is never a toss-up between two lists. What a **competitor** charges is recorded as seen — typed in or imported — and kept append-only per variant, optionally per zone; a **repricing rule** on a price list turns the freshest sighting of each rival into a proposal, and **a person applies it**: a run never changes a price on its own. pricing-svc holds no cost, so a rule's floor is a **share of the current price** and the API says so; protecting a margin proper is a matter for the buyer's cost in purchase-svc. All management-only (`pricing.write`); a storekeeper or cashier is refused, and another business sees none of it.

- `POST /admin/price-zones` `{name, description?}` → `201` `{id, name, description, storeIds: [], createdAt}`; `409 PRICING_ZONE_NAME_EXISTS`.
- `GET /admin/price-zones` — every zone with its `storeIds`.
- `PUT /admin/price-zones/{id}/stores` `{storeIds}` — **replaces** the zone's membership; a store named here leaves any other zone. Each store is checked against tenant-svc's list of the business's own (`400 PRICING_ZONE_STORE_UNKNOWN`); `404 PRICING_ZONE_NOT_FOUND`. The applied-price ledger is re-evaluated, since the price at those stores may have changed.
- `POST /admin/competitor-prices` `{variantId, competitor, price, currency?, zoneId?, observedOn?}` → `201` — one sighting, in the business's **own currency** (the default; another is `400 PRICING_COMPETITOR_CURRENCY_MISMATCH`, so rivals are compared like for like), on a day between 2000-01-01 and today (`400 PRICING_COMPETITOR_DATE_INVALID`; today when omitted), everywhere or in one zone (`400 PRICING_ZONE_UNKNOWN`). `source: MANUAL`.
- `POST /admin/competitor-prices/batch` `{observations: [1–500 of the above]}` → `{recorded}` — all or none, `source: IMPORT`; the first bad row's refusal names it.
- `GET /admin/competitor-prices?variantId=&zoneId=&limit=` — newest first, at most 100.
- `POST /admin/repricing/rules` `{name, priceListId, strategy, value?, floorPercent, rounding?, maxAgeDays?}` → `201`. `strategy`: `MATCH_LOWEST`, `UNDERCUT_PERCENT` (value 0–100, `400 REPRICING_VALUE_INVALID` at 100 or more) or `UNDERCUT_AMOUNT`; `floorPercent` 0.01–100 (`VALIDATION_FAILED` outside); `rounding` `NONE` (the currency's minor unit, half up) or `ENDING_99` (**down** to the largest .99 not above the target, so a rounded proposal is never above what the rule aimed at; a currency with no minor unit rounds down to whole units); `maxAgeDays` 1–365, 14 by default. The rule's zone is its list's (`zoneId` in the answer). `400 PRICING_LIST_UNKNOWN`, `REPRICING_STRATEGY_INVALID`, `REPRICING_ROUNDING_INVALID`, `REPRICING_MAX_AGE_INVALID`; `409 REPRICING_RULE_NAME_EXISTS`.
- `GET /admin/repricing/rules`.
- `POST /admin/repricing/rules/{id}/run` → `{ruleId, examined, proposed, proposals}` — for every variant priced on the rule's list (its single-unit price) that a rival has been seen for within `maxAgeDays`: each rival's **freshest** sighting (a newer sighting supersedes an older one even when lower), the **lowest** of those, and the rule's arithmetic against the current price. The rule reacts to being **undercut only** — a dearer rival is never a reason to raise a price — and proposes nothing when the floor forbids any cut or the tidied price is the current one. A zoned list answers sightings in its zone or everywhere; a tenant-wide list answers those seen everywhere. One **open proposal per rule and variant**: a later run refreshes it rather than piling up, and withdraws (dismisses) an open proposal the run no longer makes. `404 REPRICING_RULE_NOT_FOUND`.
- `GET /admin/repricing/proposals?status=PROPOSED|APPLIED|DISMISSED&limit=` — open ones by default, newest first: `{id, ruleId, priceListId, zoneId, variantId, currentPrice, competitor, competitorPrice, observedOn, proposedPrice, currency, status, proposedAt, decidedAt}`.
- `POST /admin/repricing/proposals/{id}/apply` — the proposed price becomes the list's single-unit price for the variant, **on the same transaction** as the decision, with the `PriceChanged` event and price history every price write owes; the applied-price ledger catches up. `POST …/dismiss` leaves the price as it is. A proposal is **decided once**: `409 REPRICING_PROPOSAL_DECIDED`; `404 REPRICING_PROPOSAL_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Not here (yet):** a competitor price feed or scraper (sightings are typed or imported); margin floors from cost (purchase-svc's); scheduled runs (a run is a call); raising prices toward a dearer market.

### Promotions (`/promotions`, `/admin/promotions`)

Same split, and for the same reason: creating a promotion is money leaving the business, and a CASHIER could do it. Writes are under `/admin/`; the storefront read is not.

- `POST /admin/promotions` — create a time-bounded promotion. Seven types: `PERCENT` and `FLAT` (per line), `BASKET_PERCENT` and `BASKET_FLAT` (whole basket), `SPEND_THRESHOLD` (a flat amount once the basket clears `minOrderAmount`), `BOGO` (`buyQty` / `getQty` / `getDiscountPct`, where 100 = free), and **`MIX_MATCH` (03.8): any `buyQty` units from the scope for `value`** — "any 3 for £10". Whole bundles only; the dearest in-scope units make up the bundles, so the saving is the one the offer promises; the units left after the last whole bundle are charged in full; a bundle priced above its units saves nothing; each bundle's saving is split across its lines by price. `buyQty` is a whole number of at least 2 and `getQty` / `getDiscountPct` do not apply (`400 PRICING_INCOMPLETE_MIX_MATCH`, `PRICING_INVALID_PROMOTION_SHAPE`). Also takes `priority` (ascending, lower runs first), `exclusive` (stops every promotion after it), `couponCode` (unique per tenant, matched case-insensitively), and the `maxRedemptions` / `maxPerCustomer` caps.
- `GET /promotions` — list active promotions (also powers the public storefront offers banner). Each carries `reductionAnnounceable` (03.12): a basket, spend-threshold, coupon, buy-one-get-one or mix-and-match offer is a condition, not a reduced price, and is always `true`; a `PERCENT` or `FLAT` item promotion, where art.6a binds, only while every reduced price on the storefront is `ANNOUNCEABLE` and nothing is pending — a banner never says *20% off* over a product whose own page may not call its price reduced.
- `POST /admin/promotions/{id}/items` — scope a promotion to `ALL`, a `VARIANT` or **a `CATEGORY` (03.8)**. A category scope resolves at quote time to the variants of every product whose category path carries it — so a promotion on *Drinks* reaches the products of *Soft drinks* beneath it — through the catalogue product-svc announces (`ProductCategorised`, `VariantCreated`; see product-svc). A category nothing has been announced for discounts **nothing**, not everything (the engine now tells an empty scope from no scope). `VARIANT` and `CATEGORY` need a `scopeId` (`400 PRICING_INVALID_SCOPE`). *Before this branch `CATEGORY` was accepted, stored and silently never applied, then refused with an explanation; it is honoured now.*
- `POST /admin/promotions/{id}/deactivate` · `POST /admin/promotions/{id}/activate` — stop a running promotion or start a stopped one, `{"reason": "..."}` **required in both directions**; `409 PRICING_ALREADY_IN_STATE` if it is already in that state. Before this there was no route, no service method and no SQL statement anywhere that wrote `promotions.active` — `endsAt` is nullable, so a promotion created without one ran forever and could only be stopped by editing the database.
- `GET /admin/promotions/{id}/status-history` — append-only on/off trail, newest first. A table rather than a pair of columns on the row, because a promotion can be switched repeatedly and a record keeping only the last change cannot answer "who turned this back on?".
- `GET /admin/promotions/windows?store=&from=` — **the promotion windows for a store (06.x)**, read by inventory-svc for the demand forecast's uplift: every promotion touching the store (its own and the business-wide ones) since `from` (`yyyy-MM-dd`; two years back by default), on or off, each as `{promotionId, storeId?, name, type, value, channel, active, startsAt, endsAt?, variantIds, allVariants}` — its scope resolved to variant ids (a category through the catalogue projection, `allVariants` for an unscoped one) and its end the end date **or the moment it was switched off** if that came first; a promotion whose window closed before `from` is left out. `400 STORE_REQUIRED` without a store, `400 PRICING_INVALID_DATE` for a `from` that is not a date. Staff-readable — the leaf common-web lists for one service's read of another — where the rest of `/admin/promotions` is management's.

### Date-code markdown — reduce to clear (`/markdowns`, `/prices/markdown-labels`, `/prices/markdown-redemptions`)
The single most common daily task on a fresh counter (05.4, 03.9): a batch coming up to its date is stickered at a lower price, the sticker carries a new barcode, and the till sells it at that price. Nothing here is a promotion — a sticker is the reduction, and no promotion applies on top of it.
- `GET /markdowns/ladder?storeId=` / `PUT /markdowns/ladder` `{storeId?, steps:[{daysToExpiry, percentOff}]}` — **the ladder (03.9)**: how much off at how many days to expiry. A store's own steps, else the business's, else the default (`3` days `25 %`, `1` day `50 %`, the day itself `75 %`); `source` says which (`STORE`, `TENANT`, `DEFAULT`). The step that applies at *n* days is the tightest whose threshold is at or above *n* — two days out takes the three-day step. `PUT` replaces the steps wholesale; two steps at the same day are `400 PRICING_LADDER_DUPLICATE_STEP`, a percentage over 100 or an empty ladder `400`. Reading is for warehouse and management roles; setting is **management-only** in the resource itself — how much margin the counter may give away is a policy.
- `GET /markdowns/plan?storeId=&withinDays=7` — **what to sticker this morning**: every batch at the store expiring within the horizon (1–60 days, `PRICING_INVALID_HORIZON`), read from inventory-svc's `/admin/inventory/batches/expiring` with the caller's identity forwarded, with its current POS price, the ladder step for its days to go, the suggested price (half-up to the penny) and the live markdown already on it if any. `inventoryReachable: false` with an empty list when inventory-svc could not be read — the screen says so rather than showing nothing to do. Warehouse and management roles, assigned to the store (`403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED` otherwise); a cashier is `403`.
- `POST /markdowns` `{storeId, variantId, batchId?, batchNo?, expiryDate, qty, percentOff | markdownPrice, reason}` — **sticker a batch**: records the decision and issues the sticker's code — an EAN-13 in the shop's own range, prefix `21`, five digits of item number from the tenant's series (a counter row moved under its lock), five digits of the reduced price in pence, and the check digit — so a plain scanner reads it like any pack. Give `percentOff` or `markdownPrice`, not both (`PRICING_MARKDOWN_AMOUNT_REQUIRED` / `_AMBIGUOUS`); the other is derived from the current POS price (`404 PRICING_MARKDOWN_NO_PRICE` when there is none); the result must be below it (`PRICING_MARKDOWN_NOT_A_REDUCTION`) and fit the code (`999.99`, `PRICING_MARKDOWN_LABEL_RANGE`); a batch already past its date cannot be stickered (`PRICING_MARKDOWN_EXPIRED_DATE`); `reason` is `SHORT_DATED`, `CLEARANCE`, `DAMAGED_PACK` or `OVERSTOCK` (`PRICING_MARKDOWN_REASON_UNKNOWN`). Warehouse and management roles at the store; a cashier is `403`.
- `GET /markdowns?storeId=&status=`, `GET /markdowns/{id}` — a store's markdowns, newest first, each with `qty`, `redeemedQty`, `remainingQty` and its `status`: `ACTIVE`, `EXPIRED` (active but past its date — shown, never stored) or `CANCELLED`. Another tenant's is `404`.
- `POST /markdowns/{id}/cancel` `{reason}` — **take the stickers off**: the code stops scanning; what already sold at the price stays sold. Not active (already cancelled) is `409 PRICING_MARKDOWN_NOT_ACTIVE`; an expired one can still be cancelled to tidy up. The code is then free for a new sticker (uniqueness is on live codes only).
- `GET /prices/markdown-labels/{code}` — **what the till calls when it scans a sticker**: the live markdown behind the thirteen digits — `markdownId`, the product, the store, the reduced and the original price, the date, how many packs are left at that price. `404 PRICING_MARKDOWN_LABEL_UNKNOWN` for a code no live sticker carries (the till then looks it up as an ordinary barcode); `409 PRICING_MARKDOWN_EXPIRED` when the batch is past its date, `409 PRICING_MARKDOWN_EXHAUSTED` when every stickered pack has sold. Any staff role including the cashier; another tenant does not know the code. **03.12:** also `wasPrice` — the price the till may strike through, before VAT — with `reductionAnnounceable`, `priorPrice`, `priorPriceStatus`, `priorPriceRequired` and `perishableExempt`. Where art.6a does not bind, or every bound country exempts goods reduced as they near their date (`PRICE_REDUCTION_PERISHABLE_EXEMPT`, art.6a(3): Germany) and the sticker's reason is `SHORT_DATED`, the was price is the original price; otherwise it is the prior price the ledger proves for the variant at that till (the sticker's price with VAT against the 30 days before), and absent when none is proven. A sticker's price is not itself a row of the ledger: it prices marked packs of one batch, not the product's offer.
- `POST /prices/quote` lines now take `markdownId` — the line is priced at the sticker's price and **stands outside the promotion engine**: no line or basket promotion touches it, and the last promotable line, not the last line, takes the rounding remainder. pricing-svc checks the markdown as the till would: `404 PRICING_MARKDOWN_NOT_FOUND`; `400 PRICING_MARKDOWN_VARIANT_MISMATCH` / `_STORE_MISMATCH` when the sticker is for another product or was issued at another store; `409 PRICING_MARKDOWN_CANCELLED` / `_EXPIRED` / `_EXHAUSTED` (the line's `qty` against what is left). Each response line carries `markdownId` back. order-svc's `POST /orders` items take the same `markdownId`, forward it in the quote, keep it on the stored line (`order_items.markdown_id`, read back as `items[].markdownId`) and pass these refusals through with their own code instead of a `503` — the cashier is told the sticker is sold out, not that pricing is down.
- `POST /prices/markdown-redemptions` `{orderId, lines:[{markdownId, qty}]}` — **what an order sold at reduced prices**, called by order-svc once the order stands (quietly, like promotion redemptions: the order is placed and paid, and a counter that could not be written is caught up, not a reason to lose the sale). Once per `(markdown, order)`: a replay records `0`. This is what makes `remainingQty` fall and a sticker run out.
- Parked sales carry `markdownId` per line (`POST /pos/parked-sales`), so a resumed sale still rings at the sticker.

**Business rules**
- **Ordering is explicit.** Line-level promotions run first in `priority` order against each line's original price, then basket-level ones against the subtotal that remains. Two line-level percentages therefore compound on the original price — two 10% offers take 20%, not 19%.
- Nothing can drive a line or a basket below zero, and the clamp is **per line**, so an oversized flat discount on a cheap item cannot eat into another line's value.
- A `BOGO` counts across every line it is scoped to, not within one line — three different shirts on a buy-2-get-1 is the common case a per-line implementation gets wrong — and discounts the **cheapest** qualifying units.
- `minOrderAmount` is tested against the basket **after** line-level discounts. A basket that only clears £100 before a half-price offer has not spent £100.
- Four defects were fixed in this rebuild, all of which failed silently: `minOrderAmount` was never read, `CATEGORY` scope never matched, `store_id` was never filtered (so a store promotion ran everywhere), and the winner was chosen by `ORDER BY value DESC`, which compared a percentage against a sum of money.

### VAT & Tax Compliance
- `POST /customer-vat-status`, `GET /customer-vat-status/{customerId}` — upsert/look up a B2B customer's VAT registration & reverse-charge status. Since 18.9 also the buyer an invoice names: `legalName` (BT-44), `countryCode`, and an e-invoicing address (`einvoiceScheme` + `einvoiceId`, a Peppol EAS code with its check digits, both or neither: `400 PRICING_EINVOICE_ADDRESS_INVALID`). The VAT number is parsed for its country — a GSTIN for IN (state code, PAN, Luhn mod-36 check character), a VAT identifier elsewhere: `400 PRICING_VAT_NUMBER_INVALID`. A customer recorded here as registered is invoiced by order-svc when a sale to them completes. Quote lines (`POST /prices/quote`) carry `vatCode` and `vatRate` so the sale records the rate each line was taxed at.
- `POST /product-vat-categories`, `GET /product-vat-categories/{variantId}` — upsert/look up a variant's HMRC VAT tax code.
- `POST /vat-rates`, `GET /vat-rates`, `GET /vat-rates/{code}`, `PUT /vat-rates/{code}` — create/list/get/update the business's VAT rates (T1/T5/T0/etc.). The code is at most 8 characters and the rate a fraction from 0 to 1; outside those, `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. **SJ-D56:** `T1` is the business's standard rate, charged on any variant with no VAT category; rates are written by management only (`403` to a cashier or storekeeper, and the same for `POST /product-vat-categories`) and read by any staff role. **No rate is ever assumed:** until the code a price needs has a rate, `POST /prices/resolve`, `/prices/resolve-batch`, `/prices/quote`, shelf labels and a scanned sticker's prior price answer `409 PRICING_VAT_RATE_NOT_CONFIGURED` — pricing used to charge a literal 20% — order-svc relays that refusal to the till and the shop rather than a 503, and the applied-price ledger records the variant as not offered. A business that charges no VAT sets `T1` exempt.
- `POST /tax-transactions`, `GET /tax-transactions?orderId=` — record/list a POSLog-style tax transaction journal entry per order.
- `GET /vat-return/mtd/registration` / `PUT /vat-return/mtd/registration` `{vrn, provider}` — **Making Tax Digital for VAT (18.5): the digital link.** The number the business files under (nine digits with HMRC's modulus-97 or 9755 check; `GB` and spaces allowed; `MTD_VRN_INVALID`) and the path it files through: `SIMULATED` (HMRC as its sandbox behaves — quarterly obligations, the nine-box rules the API enforces, one accepted return per period, a form bundle number back; nothing reaches HMRC) or `HMRC` (the VAT (MTD) API, offered only when `storeql.mtd.hmrc.client-id/client-secret` and `storeql.mtd.token-key` are configured — `MTD_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` 409 otherwise). The read returns `registered: false` with the offer until a number exists. `GET /vat-return/mtd/hmrc/authorize-url?redirectUri=` and `POST /vat-return/mtd/hmrc/connect {code, redirectUri}` complete HMRC's OAuth grant for the taxpayer; the tokens are held AES-GCM encrypted under the configured key and refreshed when about to expire; `MTD_NOT_HMRC` (409) for a simulator registration.
- `GET /vat-return/mtd/obligations?from=&to=` — the periods HMRC expects a return for (`periodKey`, start, end, due, `O`pen or `F`ulfilled). `MTD_NOT_REGISTERED` (404) before a number is registered.
- `POST /vat-return/mtd/submissions` `{periodKey, from, to, finalised, client}` — **files the return**: the nine boxes are computed from the period by the same method `GET /vat-return` uses (nobody re-keys them), boxes 6–9 rounded down to whole pounds as the API requires, sent with `finalised: true` (`MTD_NOT_FINALISED` 400 otherwise) and the fraud-prevention headers HMRC mandates — what the server knows plus what the manager's browser sent in `client` (`timezone`, `screens`, `windowSize`, `userAgent`, `deviceId`, `doNotTrack`, `publicIp`). What HMRC answers is recorded in `vat_return_submissions`, append-only, accepted or refused: `201` with `processingDate`, `formBundleNumber`, `paymentIndicator`, `chargeRefNumber`, `receiptId`; `422` with HMRC's own code when it refused (the row is kept as `REJECTED`); `503 HMRC_UNREACHABLE`; `409 MTD_DUPLICATE_SUBMISSION` before any call when the period is already accepted (HMRC would refuse it too); `MTD_PERIOD_KEY_INVALID` for a key that is not HMRC's four characters. `GET /vat-return/mtd/submissions` and `/{id}` read the record, tenant-scoped. Every route is management-only in the resource itself: the path is not under `/admin/`, and the default-deny filter would let a cashier file a VAT return.
- `GET /vat-return?from=&to=` — the boxes of an HMRC Making Tax Digital VAT return for a date range. Management-only (`PLATFORM_ADMIN`/`OWNER`/`MANAGER`), enforced in the resource rather than by path. Boxes 1, 3, 5 and 6 come from `tax_transactions` (output VAT at sale); **boxes 4 and 7 from `input_tax_transactions`** — the projection of purchase-svc's `SupplierInvoiceCaptured`, one row per invoice by invoice date, idempotent on the event id (SJ-D39 closed). Boxes 2, 8 and 9 are zero: no Northern Ireland protocol acquisitions or supplies are modelled. The response says so itself: `computedBoxes: [1,3,4,5,6,7]`, `notComputedBoxes: [2,8,9]`, `fitToFile: true` and a `caveat` naming the NI assumption.

**Business rules**
- `POST /prices/resolve(-batch)` is deliberately open with no staff-role requirement — it's a service-to-service call order-svc makes without identity headers during checkout pricing.
- The customer-VAT-status GET endpoint has its own explicit role check (`PLATFORM_ADMIN`/`OWNER`/`MANAGER`/`STOREKEEPER`/`CASHIER`) because non-`/admin` GETs aren't covered by the shared default-deny filter (see Platform-wide conventions).
- Price overrides are append-only audit records, never edited or deleted.

**Events**
- Consumes: none.
- Publishes: `PriceChanged`, `PromotionActivated`.

---

## product-svc

### Public Storefront Catalog (`/catalog`)
- `GET /catalog/products` — browse/search products (category, free-text, SKU, or barcode; separate POS vs. online channel filtering).
- `GET /catalog/categories` — browse categories.
- `GET /catalog/products/{id}` — get a product.
- `GET /catalog/products/{id}/variants` — list a product's variants.
- `GET /catalog/products/{id}/image` — fetch the product's primary image.
- `GET /catalog/variants/by-barcode/{code}` — POS barcode-scan lookup (variant + parent product in one round-trip).
- `GET /catalog/scan?code=` — **one lookup for every kind of scanned code (07.15)**. A linear EAN or UPC is matched as it always was; a **GS1 element string** (DataMatrix, GS1-128, GS1 QR — `010950600013435210LOT-7`, or the bracketed form a person writes) and a **GS1 Digital Link URI** (`https://id.gs1.org/01/09506000134352/10/LOT-7?17=261231`, or the same on the brand's own domain, which the standard expects) are read first and the item is then found by **the GTIN they carried**. That is the point: GS1's rule is that GTIN-8, -12 and -13 *are* the 14-digit form with leading zeros, so `product_variants.gtin14` (generated, check-digit verified, V25) lets the packet whose 2D code says `05012345678900` find the variant a shop entered as `5012345678900` — and the other way about. The column is **generated** rather than written by the service so it cannot drift from the barcode: no write path can forget it. Returns `{item, code}`: `item` is the same variant shape `by-barcode` returns, and `code` is what the code itself carried — `format` (`PLAIN_GTIN`/`ELEMENT_STRING`/`DIGITAL_LINK`), `gtin`, `batch` (AI 10), `serial` (21), `sscc` (00), `expiry` (17), `bestBefore` (15), `productionDate` (11), `netWeightKg` (310n), `netWeightLb` (320n, **not** converted — a conversion is a decision about which unit the shop sells in), `amountPayable` (392n/393n) and `currencyNumeric`. Money and measures are strings, scaled by the AI's own decimal places. `code` is **absent** when the scan was not a GS1 code at all (an internal code, a PLU, a shelf label), which still matches exactly. Nothing is half-read: a code the reader cannot read exactly is treated as an opaque string, and an application identifier the platform does not know **refuses** the code rather than skipping it, because skipping means guessing where its data ended and a wrong guess reads the rest of a food label as different fields. A GTIN failing its own check digit fails the reading — the alternative is selling a different item at a different price. `400 INVALID_BARCODE` for a blank code; `404 VARIANT_NOT_FOUND` naming the **GTIN** when the code carried one; `409 PRODUCT_NOT_ON_SALE_YET` for a listed line before its launch date, the same refusal `by-barcode` gives. A public catalogue read, like the rest of `/catalog`. The parser is `shared/gs1` (`Gs1Reader`, `Gtin`, `ApplicationIdentifier`), a standards module with no service state, so inventory-svc's goods-in reads a case label the same way the till reads a packet.

### Admin Catalog Management (`/admin`)
**Brands, Categories, Products & Variants**
- `POST/GET/GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/brands(/{id})` — brand CRUD (delete = deactivate).
- `POST/GET/GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/categories(/{id})` — category CRUD (delete = deactivate).
- `POST/GET/GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/products(/{id})` — product CRUD (delete = delist); admin list supports status/category filters.
- `POST/GET /admin/products/{id}/variants`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/products/{id}/variants/{variantId}` — variant CRUD (delete = delist; editing a delisted variant is `409 VARIANT_NOT_ACTIVE`).
- `GET /admin/products/variants/resolve?ids=` — batch-resolve up to 200 variant ids to name/SKU/product context.

**Images & Store Assortment**
- `PUT/DELETE /admin/products/{id}/image` — upload/replace (raw bytes, strictly <256KB) or remove a product's primary image. The admin app downscales and re-encodes to that budget before uploading; the service rejects anything at or above it, and a CHECK constraint on `product_images` enforces the same bound at the storage layer.
- `GET/PUT /admin/products/{id}/stores` — get/replace a product's per-store assortment (empty list = sold everywhere).

**Units of Measure**
- `GET /admin/uom/classes`, `GET /admin/uom/units` — list UOM classes and unit definitions.
- `GET /admin/uom/convert` — convert a quantity between two UOMs (global or variant-specific factor).
- `POST/GET/DELETE /admin/uom/item-conversions(/{id})` — variant-specific UOM conversion factors.

**Item Templates**
- `POST/GET/GET/DELETE /admin/item-templates(/{id})` — attribute-template CRUD.
- `POST /admin/item-templates/{id}/apply/{variantId}` — apply a template's attributes to a variant.

**Cross-References, Relationships & Revisions**
- `POST/GET/DELETE /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/cross-references(/{id})` — supplier/customer part-number cross-references.
- `POST/GET/DELETE /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/relationships(/{id})` — related-item links between variants (e.g. substitute, accessory).
- `POST/GET /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/revisions`, `GET .../current`, `GET .../{id}` — create/list/get dated revisions of a variant's spec.

**Bulk Import**
- `POST /admin/import` — bulk-import categories + products/variants from a JSON payload (partial success, per-row errors, duplicate SKUs reported not fatal).
- `POST /admin/import/supplier-csv` — import a supplier catalogue CSV.

**Catalog Groups & Container Types**
- `POST/GET/GET/DELETE /admin/catalog-groups(/{id})`, `POST/DELETE .../elements(/{elementId})` — merchandising catalog-group CRUD and membership.
- `POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/catalog-assignment` — assign a variant's catalog group.
- `POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/container-types(/{id})` — packaging/container type CRUD.
- `POST/GET/DELETE /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/container-links(/{id})` — link a variant to a container type.

**Attribute Groups & Category Sets**
- `GET /admin/attribute-groups(/{groupCode})` — structured attribute-group definitions and fields.
- `PUT/GET/GET/DELETE /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/attribute-groups(/{groupCode})` — set/get/remove a variant's attribute values.
- `POST/GET/GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/category-sets(/{id})`, `POST/GET/DELETE .../members(/{categoryId})` — alternate category-hierarchy ("category set") CRUD and membership.
- `POST/GET/DELETE /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/category-set-assignments(/{setId})` — assign a variant into a category set.

**Business rules**
- Public catalog shows only `ACTIVE` products; the online list further filters to `sellable_online`, POS channel to `sellable_pos`.
- Bulk import never hard-fails a batch — it always returns 200 with a success count plus any per-row errors.

**Events**
- Consumes: none.
- Publishes: `ProductCreated`, `ProductUpdated`, `ProductDelisted`, `VariantCreated`, `ItemTemplateCreated`, `ItemTemplateApplied`, `ItemRevisionCreated`, and **`ProductCategorised`** (03.8, topic `storeql.catalog.product-categorised`): a product's `categoryPath` — its own category, then its parent, up to the root; empty when it has none — and its `variantIds`, published on every create and update and re-announced for every product when a category is moved under a new parent. `POST /admin/products/republish-catalogue` (management) re-announces the whole catalogue for a consumer that arrived after the products did and returns how many were announced; pricing-svc projects the event (and `VariantCreated`'s `productId`) to honour category-scoped promotions, and anything else that needs a variant's category — sales by category, say — has the same event to read.

---

### Shelf space (`/admin/merchandising`) and range (`/admin/assortment`)

Two buying decisions about a shelf: how much of it a line gets, and which shops carry the line at all. Management only, both.

- `POST /admin/merchandising/fixtures` `{storeId, zoneId?, code, name, kind, shelfCount, shelfWidthMm}` · `GET …/fixtures?store=` · `POST …/fixtures/{id}/retire` — **the furniture a layout is drawn for**: `GONDOLA`, `END_CAP`, `CHILLER`, `FREEZER`, `SHELF_RUN`, `BIN`, `COUNTER`. Its shelves and their width are what make a plan checkable. `zoneId` is optional because a buyer plans a fixture before anybody decides which aisle it stands in. A fixture is retired, never deleted — planograms point at it — and its code is then free again. `409 FIXTURE_CODE_TAKEN` among active fixtures; retiring publishes `FixtureRetired`.
- `PUT /admin/merchandising/variants/{id}/facing-width` `{facingWidthMm}` — **how wide one facing of a line is**, 1–5000mm, the measurement every fit check is made of. Kept on the variant rather than in a layout: a bottle is the width it is on every shelf it stands on. Null says it is not known after all.
- `POST /admin/merchandising/fixtures/{id}/planograms` `{effectiveFrom, note?}` · `PUT /admin/merchandising/planograms/{id}/positions` `{positions:[{variantId, shelf, sequence, facings, depth, minPresentation}]}` · `POST …/planograms/{id}/publish` · `GET …/planograms/{id}` · `GET …/fixtures/{id}/planograms` · `GET …/fixtures/{id}/planogram` — **a layout, version by version**. One draft per fixture (`409 PLANOGRAM_DRAFT_EXISTS`: two people drawing one shelf is a merge nobody wins) and one version in force, both partial unique indexes rather than checks in code. Positions are replaced whole, because half a shelf saved is not a smaller layout but a wrong one, and **each shelf is checked as it is saved**: facings times each variant's width against the shelf (`409 PLANOGRAM_SHELF_OVERFLOWS`), and a shelf the fixture does not have is `400 PLANOGRAM_SHELF_BEYOND_FIXTURE`. Checked at save rather than at publication because a layout found not to fit after a reset has been scheduled around it is found out too late. A position whose variant has **no recorded width is placed and not checked** — the answer's per-shelf fit says how many it could not account for, since refusing a whole layout for want of one measurement would be worse than a partial check that says so. `capacity` (facings × depth) is a **generated column**, so no write path can leave it disagreeing with the layout. Publishing supersedes the version in force rather than editing it (`409 PLANOGRAM_NOT_DRAFT` on an edit, `409 PLANOGRAM_EMPTY` on publishing nothing, which over a full layout would quietly tell replenishment the shelf holds nothing) and emits `ShelfCapacityPublished` in the same transaction.
- `PUT /admin/merchandising/space-plans` `{storeId, categoryId, targetShare, reviewOn?, note?}` · `GET /admin/merchandising/space?store=` — **what a category was promised against what its bays give it**. `targetShare` is a fraction of the store's shelf width (`400 SPACE_SHARE_INVALID` outside 0–1); the actual share is **measured from the layouts in force**, so it moves when a shelf is re-laid and not when somebody updates a number, and the variance is signed because over- and under-spaced are different problems. Only planned categories appear.
- `POST /admin/merchandising/resets` `{categoryId, name, scheduledFor}` · `GET …/resets` · `POST …/resets/{id}/planograms` `{planogramId}` · `POST …/resets/{id}/complete` · `POST …/resets/{id}/cancel` `{reason}` — **the day a category is re-laid, all at once**, because half a reset is a mess in an aisle. `overdue` is derived, never stored. A layout is claimed by one reset (`409 RESET_PLANOGRAM_CLAIMED`, or `RESET_PLANOGRAM_ATTACHED` when this reset already has it) and a cancellation needs a reason: an abandoned reset with none is what somebody asks about in six months.
- `PUT /admin/merchandising/brands/{id}/own-brand` `{ownBrand}` — own-brand changes the margin, the protection a range review gives the line, and who answers for a recall.
- `POST /admin/assortment/clusters` `{code, name, note?}` · `GET …/clusters` · `GET …/clusters/{id}` · `POST …/clusters/{id}/stores` `{storeIds}` — **a named group of stores to range against**. The complaint about per-store assortment was never that it could not express a range but that expressing one costs a row per store. A store may belong to several clusters, deliberately: "Scotland" and "has a bakery" are both true of the same shop. `409 CLUSTER_CODE_TAKEN` among active clusters; stores already in a cluster are left alone rather than refused.
- `POST /admin/assortment/changes` `{productId, storeId|clusterId, action, effectiveFrom?, reason}` · `GET …/changes?product=` · `GET …/changes/due?asOf=` · `POST …/changes/apply?asOf=` — **the dated, reasoned decision log in front of `product_stores`**. `product_stores` stays the range as it stands, which is what the till and the storefront ask; this is the intent, and `applied_at` is what separates them, so a range is planned three weeks out rather than typed on the morning. `LIST` or `DELIST` (`400 ASSORTMENT_ACTION_UNKNOWN`), aimed at exactly one store or one cluster (`400 ASSORTMENT_TARGET_REQUIRED`), with a reason that is required because a de-list with none is what the log exists to stop. The sweep applies **oldest first** — a line listed in March and de-listed in June, applied the other way round, is on the shelf — resolves a cluster's membership **on the day it is applied** (so a shop that opened since the decision is included), and applies each change exactly once. Two refusals are reported and leave the change **due** rather than swallowing it: `CLUSTER_EMPTY`, and `ASSORTMENT_RANGED_EVERYWHERE` for a de-list against a line with no rows at all — under the convention that no rows means every store there is nothing to take it out of, and the list of stores to fill in belongs to tenant-svc, not here.
- `POST /admin/assortment/reviews` `{categoryId, name, periodFrom, periodTo, note?}` · `GET …/reviews` · `GET …/reviews/{id}` · `POST …/reviews/{id}/lines` `{lines:[{variantId, unitsSold?, revenue?, margin?, currency?, rankInCategory?, ownBrand}]}` · `POST …/reviews/{id}/decisions` `{variantId, decision, note?}` · `POST …/reviews/{id}/close` `{storeId|clusterId, effectiveFrom?}` · `POST …/reviews/{id}/abandon` — **the comparison behind an add or a drop**.
