The worth of a review is not the decision, which the lifecycle could already record, but that the **figures it was taken on are kept beside it** — as a snapshot supplied by the caller, because sales and margin belong to order-svc and reporting-svc and product-svc reads neither, and because a report re-run next year shows different numbers and makes an old decision look arbitrary. A currency is required exactly when money is given (`400 REVIEW_LINE_CURRENCY`). `KEEP`, `DELIST` or `INTRODUCE`; dropping an **own-brand** line asks for a note (`400 REVIEW_OWN_BRAND_NOTE`) — not a refusal, a buyer may be right, but the remedy for a poor own-brand line is more often a reformulation or a price, and the margin lost is the business's own. Closing needs every line decided (`409 REVIEW_LINES_UNDECIDED`) and records the changes the decisions imply, with the review, the period and the rank written into each reason. Variant decisions become product-level range actions by three rules: **`INTRODUCE` wins** (a new flavour replacing an old one must not de-list the product on its way in), a product is **de-listed only when every one of its variants was reviewed and every one was dropped** (otherwise the drop would take lines nobody looked at off the shelf), and `KEEP` does nothing. Products left ranged come back as `leftAlone` with the reason, rather than silently.

**Events** — publishes `ShelfCapacityPublished` (topic `storeql.catalog.shelf-capacity-published`: the whole fixture's capacity per variant with its `version`, summed across positions so a multi-sited line is not under-counted) and `FixtureRetired` (`storeql.catalog.fixture-retired`). inventory-svc projects both into `shelf_targets` and answers `GET /admin/inventory/reports/shelf-gaps` from them; the version is the high-water mark that makes a re-delivered older layout a no-op.

---

### Food safety, origin and age-restricted sales

Four capabilities that are law rather than product strategy. The reads sit on `/catalog` deliberately — a shopper is entitled to allergen information before buying, and a till needs the age check — while every write is management-only under `/admin/`.

- `GET /catalog/allergens` — the fourteen allergens Regulation (EU) 1169/2011 Annex II names. Reference data, seeded, read-only: a tenant that could edit the list could quietly delete one.
- `PUT /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/allergens` — declare a variant's allergens. **Replaces** the whole declaration, so a mistake can be corrected; merging would make "we were wrong, it has no celery" unsayable. Each entry is `CONTAINS` or `MAY_CONTAIN`, and the distinction is legal rather than cosmetic — `MAY_CONTAIN` is a cross-contamination warning. Declaring the same allergen twice is refused (`PRODUCT_DUPLICATE_ALLERGEN`) rather than silently resolved to the stricter one.
- `GET /catalog/variants/{variantId}/allergens` — the declaration, open to shoppers. **Read the `status`, not the list length.** `UNDECLARED` with an empty list means nobody has checked; `DECLARED` with an empty list means the product has been checked and contains none of the fourteen. Sending an empty list to the PUT is how the second is said — a positive statement, not an omission. Treating the first as the second is how an allergic customer is told a product is safe when nobody knows.
- `GET /admin/products/by-allergen/{code}?presence=` — every product carrying one allergen; the query a recall runs. Both presences by default, because a withdrawal usually has to cover the may-contains too.
- `GET /admin/products/allergen-gaps` — food products nobody has declared yet, oldest first. The list an inspector asks for. An item counts as food once it is marked so: `"food": true` on the compliance PUT moves it from `NOT_APPLICABLE` to `UNDECLARED`, and it leaves this list when its allergens are declared. `"food": false` makes it `NOT_APPLICABLE` and removes any declaration; omitting the flag leaves the status alone, and re-marking a declared item as food never turns its declaration back into an unknown. (SJ-D42: until the flag existed nothing wrote `UNDECLARED` — the column defaults to `NOT_APPLICABLE`, whatever V17's comment says — so this list could never return a row.)
- `GET /admin/products/{id}/safety-information` · `PUT …` · `GET /catalog/products/{id}/safety-information` — **product safety information for online offers (01.12)**: what Regulation (EU) 2023/988 (GPSR) art.19 requires an online offer to show — the manufacturer's name, postal address and electronic address (`manufacturerContact`: an e-mail address or an `https://` URL) and country (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2); where the manufacturer is outside the EU, the same for the EU responsible person; and the `warnings`, or `noWarnings: true` stating none apply (not both, `SAFETY_WARNINGS_CONFLICT`). The PUT replaces the statement; blank fields are absent; `SAFETY_TEXT_INVALID` (length or control characters), `SAFETY_CONTACT_INVALID`, `SAFETY_COUNTRY_INVALID`. Reads return `recorded`, `required` (whether GPSR binds this business's online offers today) and `missing`. The public read is open to shoppers, shown with the offer. Whether the regulation binds a business, and whether a manufacturer's country is inside it, are asked of tenant-svc's jurisdiction rules (`GPSR_ONLINE_OFFER`) through `Jurisdictions` — never a list of EU members here — and `503` when they cannot be read. Where it binds, **a product is not offered online without it**: `POST /admin/products` accepts `safetyInformation` and refuses `sellableOnline` without enough (`400 PRODUCT_SAFETY_INFORMATION_REQUIRED`, the missing items in `details`); `PUT /admin/products/{id}` refuses to keep or put an active product online while its stored statement lacks something; the safety PUT refuses to thin the statement of a product that is online; a bulk import refuses rows that would create an online product (import them with `sellableOnline: false`, then state the information). Each check runs under the product's row lock, so a statement cleared while the product goes online cannot leave an online offer without one. A product sold only at the till needs none of it.
- `GET /admin/products/safety-information/missing` — active online products that GPSR requires safety information for and that lack some, with what each lacks (products listed before the rule was enforced); empty where it does not bind the business.
- `PUT /admin/products/variants/{variantId}/compliance` — country of origin (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, mandatory for unprocessed meat, fish, fruit, veg, honey, olive oil and wine under EU 1169/2011 art.26), ingredients, the age-restriction category, and the weighed-item fields: `soldBy` (EACH/WEIGHT/VOLUME/LENGTH), `netContent` + `netContentUom` for the unit price a shelf edge must display (Price Marking Order 2004), `tareWeight` a scale deducts, and `catchWeight` for items whose price is not knowable until they are weighed. An item not sold by the each with no unit named is refused — a shelf edge could not price it. **Unit pricing (03.13):** the price buys `netContent` of `netContentUom` (one of that unit when sold by weight, volume or length and no content is given); every save announces the resulting measure in standard units as `VariantMeasured` with the next `measure_version` (V21), in the same transaction, and `POST /admin/products/republish-catalogue` re-announces every variant's at its current version. Where `UNIT_PRICING` binds the business, a **food** item (marked food, or already food) must state a measure convertible to kg, litre, metre, m² or items — a single loose item states `1 EA` — or the save is refused `400 PRODUCT_UNIT_PRICE_MEASURE_REQUIRED`; a bag, a measure of nothing, or weight in litres gives no measure. Since 18.9 also `hsnCode` — the HSN code (or SAC code for a service), 4, 6 or 8 digits (`400 PRODUCT_INVALID_HSN_CODE`), which India's e-invoices name every line by; returned by `GET /admin/products/variants/resolve` for the invoice.
- **Drinks container (09.16):** the same route takes `depositMaterial` (`PET`, `ALUMINIUM`, `STEEL` or `GLASS`) with `depositVolumeMl` (1–10,000), both or neither: what the drink comes in, so the scheme where a store trades can price the deposit. `400 PRODUCT_DEPOSIT_CONTAINER_INCOMPLETE`, `PRODUCT_DEPOSIT_MATERIAL_UNKNOWN`, `PRODUCT_DEPOSIT_VOLUME_OUT_OF_RANGE`. `GET /catalog/variants/by-barcode/{code}` and `GET /admin/products/variants/resolve` carry both, so a till knows at the scan.
- **Item lifecycle:** a product's `status` is `NEW_LINE`, `ACTIVE`, `DISCONTINUED` or `DELISTED`. `POST /admin/products` takes `status: NEW_LINE` with an optional `launchOn` (ISO date) for a line listed before it goes on sale (`400 PRODUCT_STATUS_INVALID`, `PRODUCT_LAUNCH_ON_NEEDS_NEW_LINE`, `PRODUCT_LAUNCH_ON_INVALID`); `POST /admin/products/{id}/launch` (NEW_LINE → ACTIVE), `POST /admin/products/{id}/discontinue` (ACTIVE → DISCONTINUED: sold while stock lasts, never reordered), `POST /admin/products/{id}/reinstate` (DISCONTINUED → ACTIVE), `DELETE /admin/products/{id}` (any → DELISTED); a move a line cannot make from where it is is `409 PRODUCT_LIFECYCLE_INVALID`. `ProductResponse` carries `launchOn` and `discontinuedAt`. The public catalogue and search list what is on sale or being run down; `GET /catalog/variants/by-barcode/{code}` is `409 PRODUCT_NOT_ON_SALE_YET` for a new line (naming its launch day) and `404` for one delisted. Each move goes out as `ProductLaunched`, `ProductDiscontinued`, `ProductReinstated` (topic `storeql.catalog.product-lifecycle`) or `ProductDelisted`, with the `variantIds` it covers; inventory-svc takes a discontinued or delisted line's variants out of the low-stock report and the planning run.
- `GET /catalog/variants/{variantId}/compliance` — the same, for a shelf edge and a scale.
- `GET /catalog/variants/{variantId}/age-check?country=GB` — what the till must ask before taking payment. **Read `restricted`, never the absence of `minimumAge`**: a null field is omitted from the JSON entirely, so a client inferring "no age given, therefore sell it" cannot tell an unrestricted item from a field that went missing. `restricted` is always present. **A date of birth, not an age (10.8):** from the day a birth-date cut-off takes effect, `bornBefore` (yyyy-mm-dd) says to refuse anyone born on or after it, whatever their age — the UK's generational tobacco ban (Tobacco and Vapes Act 2026: nobody born on or after 1 Jan 2009) appears from 1 Jan 2027 — and `bornBeforeTenantOverride` is true when the date is the business's own earlier policy. The strictest cut-off wins; a tie is reported as the law's.
- `GET /admin/age-restriction-rules?country=` · `PUT /admin/age-restriction-rules` — the statutory defaults with this tenant's overrides shadowing them. A tenant rule may be **stricter than the statute and never laxer** (`PRODUCT_AGE_BELOW_STATUTORY`): a chain adopting Challenge-25 is making a policy decision, a chain setting alcohol to 16 in the UK is committing an offence, and a system that lets them configure it has helped. A rule may also carry `bornBefore`: a business may adopt the law's cut-off early or choose an earlier date, never a later one (`PRODUCT_BORN_BEFORE_LAXER`); a malformed, pre-1900 or future date is `PRODUCT_INVALID_BORN_BEFORE`. The listing shows the strictest cut-off per category, with `bornBeforeFrom` — the day a statutory one takes effect, null for a business's own, which applies at once. Setting a rule replaces it, so a rule set without `bornBefore` clears the business's date; the law's still applies.

**Why the age is not on the product.** The same bottle of wine is 18 in the UK, 18 in China, 20 in Japan and 21 in the US. The variant carries the *category* (`ALCOHOL`, `TOBACCO`, `KNIVES`, `SOLVENTS`, `FIREWORKS`, `LOTTERY`, `VIDEO_18`, `NICOTINE_VAPE`, `CORROSIVES`, `PETROL`) and the age is resolved per country at the till. India's drinking age varies by state, so the seeded default is the conservative 21 and a tenant trading where it differs overrides it. The endpoint takes the country rather than a store id on purpose: this is asked for every restricted line scanned, and a second network hop belongs nowhere near a queue.

A restricted item in a country with **no** rule for it returns `400 PRODUCT_NO_AGE_RULE` rather than "no restriction" — a missing rule is a gap in configuration, not a licence to sell.

## purchase-svc

### Suppliers & Purchase Orders
- `POST /suppliers`, `GET /suppliers`, `GET /suppliers/{id}`, `PUT /suppliers/{id}` — onboard/list/get/correct suppliers. `PUT` (management-only) replaces name, VAT details, country, currency and terms — SJ-D34 found none of them could be corrected after creation, which mattered once purchase orders inherited the supplier's currency. The currency can change only while no purchase order against the supplier is open (`409 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_CURRENCY_IN_USE`, with the count); orders already raised keep the currency they were raised in. A name already taken is `409 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_DUPLICATE`. `currency` is the currency **this supplier invoices in**, defaulting to the tenant's own declared currency; set it explicitly for an overseas supplier (a Japanese supplier billing a UK tenant in JPY). **Payment fields (17.10):** `remittanceEmail` (where remittance advice goes; on `PUT` an empty string clears it, and one that is not an email is `400 PURCHASE_REMITTANCE_EMAIL_INVALID`) and the bank details `bankAccountName`, `bankSortCode` + `bankAccountNumber` (UK, six and eight digits, dashes and spaces allowed) and/or `bankIban` (check digits verified) with an optional `bankBic`. They are validated as one set — half a UK account, a BIC without an IBAN or no holder name is `400 PURCHASE_BANK_DETAILS_INVALID` — and on `PUT` a set that is given replaces the old one whole, while `clearBankDetails: true` removes them. Setting, changing or clearing them needs `finance.payments` (`403 PERMISSION_DENIED`): a storekeeper may add a supplier, not the account its money goes to. Only an actual change moves `bankDetailsChangedAt`. Responses never carry the full account: `bankAccountNumberMasked` and `bankIbanMasked` show the last four characters, with `hasBankDetails` and `bankDetailsChangedAt`.
- `POST /purchase-orders` — create a purchase order for a supplier. **The order's currency comes from the supplier**, not the tenant and not a default — a purchase order is a commitment to pay whoever invoices (SJ-D24). Naming a currency that contradicts the supplier's is refused with `PURCHASE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` rather than silently overridden; a non-ISO-4217 code is `PURCHASE_INVALID_CURRENCY`.
- **Lead time.** An order records `submitted_at` when it goes to the supplier; a receipt against it measures the delivery (see *Supplier lead times and scorecards*).
- **Consignment (sale or return).** `POST /purchase-orders` takes `ownership`: `OWNED` (the default — the business owns the goods on arrival) or `CONSIGNMENT` (the supplier owns them until they sell; `400 PURCHASE_OWNERSHIP_INVALID` otherwise). A receipt against a consignment order tells inventory-svc whose the stock is (`GoodsReceived` carries `ownership` and `supplierId`) and **posts nothing** — no stock asset, no goods-received-not-invoiced; the debt arises as the stock sells. A consignment order is settled on its sales, never invoiced on receipt: `POST /supplier-invoices` against one is `409 PURCHASE_CONSIGNMENT_NOT_INVOICED`. Every order answer carries `ownership`.
- **Dropship.** An order raised from a confirmed sale for a supplier that ships to the customer has `source: DROPSHIP`, `salesOrderId` (the sale) and `shipTo` (the customer, as the order said). It is never received: `POST /goods-receipts` against it is `409 PURCHASE_DROPSHIP_NOT_RECEIVED`; `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/dropship-delivered` moves a SUBMITTED one to RECEIVED with no stock and posts **Dr 5020 Purchases - Dropship / Cr 2109 Goods Received Not Invoiced** for its net (`409 PURCHASE_PO_NOT_DELIVERABLE` otherwise). See *Dropship* below.
- `GET /purchase-orders`, `GET /purchase-orders/{id}` — list/get purchase orders.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/submit` — submit a draft PO. Lands in `SUBMITTED` when the order's **net** value is within the submitter's own spend authority, and in `PENDING_APPROVAL` when it is not; either way the submission is recorded in the order's append-only approval trail. Spend authority is configured per **currency and role** (`storeql.purchase.approval.limits`, e.g. `GBP:MANAGER:5000,JPY:MANAGER:800000,GBP:OWNER:UNLIMITED`) — there is no FX handling in StoreQL, so a ceiling in one currency cannot be compared against an order in another. **Unconfigured means off**: with no limits at all, any staff role may submit any amount, exactly as before the feature existed. Once anything is configured, a currency with no entry **fails closed** rather than granting unlimited authority. **Translated (03.x):** an order in a currency with no ceiling of its own is measured in the business's home currency at the exchange rate it keeps (`GET /admin/tenant/fx-rates`) — the decision and its reason name both figures and the rate (`USD 4000.00 translated to GBP 3160.00 at 0.79 GBP per USD`), and the order keeps `fxRate`, `totalNetHome` and `homeCurrency` from the moment it was measured, because a rate moves and the record must not. Without a rate an unconfigured currency still fails closed; a currency with a ceiling of its own is never translated.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/approve` — release a `PENDING_APPROVAL` order to the supplier. The approver's own authority is checked against the same figure by the same rule, so **separation of duties falls out of the limits**: an order is only pending because it exceeded the submitter's ceiling, so that same person is refused with `PURCHASE_APPROVAL_EXCEEDS_AUTHORITY`. There is deliberately no separate self-approval rule — it would be redundant, and would deadlock a single-owner shop.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/reject` — send it back to `DRAFT` with a **required** reason. Needs no spend authority: refusing to commit money is not itself a commitment, and requiring it would strand an order too large for anyone configured.
- `GET /purchase-orders/{id}/approvals` — the append-only approval trail, newest first. Each row carries the net value and the authority **as they stood at the time**, so it still answers "was that person allowed to commit that much?" after the configuration changes — and because a rejected order can be edited before resubmission, the figure a decision was made against is not necessarily the one the order carries now.
- `GET /purchase-orders/spend-authority?currency=GBP` — what the caller may commit, so a buyer learns their ceiling before building the order rather than after trying to submit it.
- `POST /supplier-invoices` — **capture a supplier's invoice and match it three ways.** Quantity is matched against what was **received**, not ordered — you pay for what turned up, and an order for 100 that delivered 60 and invoiced 60 is correct. Price is matched against the **order**, because a goods receipt records quantity only. Invoiced quantity is compared **cumulatively across every invoice on the order**, so a supplier who delivers and bills in two parts is not flagged on the second (the same shape partial receipt established for the received leg). The invoice is stored either way: `MATCHED` or `FLAGGED`. **Flagging never blocks capture** — an invoice that arrived is a fact, and refusing to record one that disagrees destroys the evidence of the disagreement. A duplicate invoice number for the same supplier is `409 PURCHASE_INVOICE_DUPLICATE`, matched case-insensitively because the reference is printed on paper and typed by a human. An invoice in a currency the order was not placed in is `400 PURCHASE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` — that is a different document, not a variance.
- `GET /supplier-invoices`, `GET /supplier-invoices/{id}` — list (optionally `?poId=` and `?status=MATCHED|FLAGGED|APPROVED|REJECTED`; `?status=FLAGGED` is the queue awaiting a decision, another value `400 PURCHASE_INVOICE_STATUS_UNKNOWN`) and get, each line carrying ordered / received / previously-invoiced / invoiced and both unit prices. Variances are the ones **stored at capture**, not recomputed: a purchase order can be amended after an invoice is flagged, and re-matching on read would silently erase the disagreement it was flagged for. Codes: `INVOICED_ABOVE_RECEIVED`, `NOT_RECEIVED`, `NOT_ON_ORDER`, `PRICE_ABOVE_ORDER`, `PRICE_BELOW_ORDER`; header-level `TOTAL_MISMATCH` in `headerVariances`. **Tolerance is SAP-shaped (07.7)**: percentage *and* absolute, with separate upper and lower bands on price, the stricter governing — `storeql.purchase.match.tolerance.price-percent` (upper), `.price-lower-percent` (defaults to the upper), `.price-absolute`, `.qty-percent`, `.qty-absolute`, `.total-absolute`. Every band defaults to exact, which surfaces every difference; widening one is a procurement policy made in configuration. The response also carries `dueDate` (invoice date + the supplier's `paymentTermsDays`), `statedGross`, `postedAt`, `payable` (MATCHED, or FLAGGED then APPROVED), the decision (`resolvedAt`, `resolvedBy`, `resolutionReason`), and whether a payment run has settled it (`paid`, `paidAt`, `paymentRunId`).
- **Capture posts to the nominal ledger** — `Dr 2109 Goods Received Not Invoiced` (net), `Dr 2201 VAT Input` (VAT), `Cr 2100 Trade Creditors` (gross), dated the invoice date, on the order's store — **whether or not the invoice matched**. This is SAP's model rather than "post on approval": the liability exists the moment the supplier has invoiced, whatever the buyer thinks of the figures; what a variance blocks is *payment* (`payable=false`). `statedGross`, when keyed, is the total printed on the document and is checked against the supplier's own lines plus VAT; a difference beyond `.total-absolute` flags `TOTAL_MISMATCH`, because an invoice that does not add up is wrong before any line is compared. An invoice dated in a month whose accounting period is closed is `409 PURCHASE_PERIOD_CLOSED`.
- `POST /supplier-invoices/{id}/resolve` `{action: APPROVE|REJECT, reason}` — **the decision on a flagged invoice (07.7).** Management only, reason required. `APPROVE` releases it for payment and leaves its posting as it is. `REJECT` reverses its posting line for line on a new journal dated today (`INVOICE_REVERSAL`), publishes `SupplierInvoiceRejected` so pricing-svc takes its input VAT back out of the return, and frees the quantities it billed so the supplier's corrected invoice matches cleanly. Only a `FLAGGED` invoice can be decided (`409 PURCHASE_INVOICE_NOT_FLAGGED`), and only once — the status condition is inside the `UPDATE`, so twenty managers deciding together produce one decision and nineteen `409 PURCHASE_INVOICE_ALREADY_RESOLVED`. An action that is neither is `400 PURCHASE_RESOLUTION_UNKNOWN`.
- `POST /purchase-orders/proposals/run` `{storeId, coverDays?}` — **the automatic order proposal (06.x).** Reads the store's reorder plans, stock on hand and forecast from inventory-svc, adds what is already on order here (open orders less receipts), and for every item at or below its reorder point raises a **DRAFT** purchase order on the supplier the business last bought it from (or one whose item code names it) — one order per supplier, due after the longest lead time among its lines. The quantity is the reorder-point rule: the EOQ when the plan has one, otherwise back to the reorder point plus the forecast over `coverDays` (28 by default; the plan's average daily demand stands in when there is no forecast, and the line says so), never less than restores the reorder point, then bent to the supplier's order modifiers (minimum, lot, maximum). Every line carries `proposalReason` — *on hand 3 + on order 10 = 13 ≤ reorder point 28; order EOQ 45* — and the order carries `source: PROPOSAL`; a person submits it like any draft. Returns the run: `considered`, `orders[{poId, supplierId, supplierName, currency, lines, totalNet}]`, `skipped[{variantId, reason}]` (no reorder point computed; no supplier). Refused while a proposed order for the store is still a draft (`409 PURCHASE_PROPOSAL_OPEN`), when inventory-svc cannot be read (`503 PURCHASE_PROPOSAL_STOCK_UNAVAILABLE` — nothing is proposed without the stock position), for a cover outside 1–365 (`400 PURCHASE_PROPOSAL_COVER_INVALID`); a store-scoped caller is held to their stores. `GET /purchase-orders/proposals?store=&limit=` — the store's runs, latest first, each with its orders as they stand now.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/lines`, `GET /purchase-orders/{id}/lines` — add/list PO line items (a line a proposal raised carries `proposalReason`; a purchase order carries `source`: `MANUAL` or `PROPOSAL`). **Adding a line restates the order's totals** in the same transaction (SJ-D22): `totalNet` from the lines, `totalVat` from each line's VAT code rated against pricing-svc's table for the tenant, `totalGross` from the two. Before this the three columns were inserted as zero and never written again, so every purchase order in the product reported a value of `0.00`. A VAT code with no configured rate contributes zero rather than refusing the line — VAT rates are tenant configuration nothing seeds, so a fresh tenant genuinely has none.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/cancel` — cancel a DRAFT or SUBMITTED order with a required reason. A RECEIVED one is refused: stock is booked against it (SJ-D3).
- `GET /purchase-orders/{id}/progress` — **ordered against received, line by line, with the balance still due.** This is what a `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` status does not tell you: a buyer chasing a supplier needs to know *what* is missing. Receipts are matched to order lines by variant rather than by line id, because a delivery note names products, not order rows.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/close` — short-close a `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` order with a required reason: the balance is never arriving and we have stopped waiting. Refused on any other status — nothing delivered is a cancellation, everything delivered is already RECEIVED.
- `POST /goods-receipts` — record goods received against a purchase order. Accepts partial deliveries. `GoodsReceived` carries each line's `costPrice` — the order's unit price — so the batch inventory-svc creates knows what it cost (07.x). **Posts `Dr Stock / Cr 2109 Goods Received Not Invoiced`** for the quantity received at the order's own prices, in the receipt's transaction (an idempotent replay posts nothing twice). The stock code is **the store's zone-to-GL mapping in inventory-svc** (17.3: the store-level row of `PUT /admin/inventory/zone-gl-mappings`, read service-to-service) or `1001 Stock` when the store has none; when inventory-svc cannot be reached the default is used, so a discovery outage changes which code a receipt posts to, never whether it posts. A receipt into a month whose accounting period is closed is `409 PURCHASE_PERIOD_CLOSED` (04.7), and the order is untouched.
- `GET /goods-receipts?poId=` — list goods receipts for a PO.
- `POST /vendor-returns` `{poId, reason, notes, lines:[{variantId, qty}]}` — **return to vendor and the debit note (07.8)**, the reverse SJ-D3 named. Goods go back against an order something was received on (`PARTIALLY_RECEIVED`, `RECEIVED` or `CLOSED`; otherwise `409 PURCHASE_RTV_NOTHING_RECEIVED`), from the order's store, at the order's own price for each variant with VAT at the order's VAT code — so the debit note and the invoice it offsets agree to the penny. No variant may go back in more than was received less what already went back (`422 PURCHASE_RTV_OVER_RETURN`, checked under the order's lock); a variant not on the order is `422 PURCHASE_RTV_NOT_ON_ORDER`; `reason` is one of `DAMAGED`, `WRONG_ITEM`, `OVER_DELIVERED`, `QUALITY`, `EXPIRED`, `RECALL`, `OTHER` (`400 PURCHASE_RTV_REASON_UNKNOWN`). The debit note takes the next number in the tenant's series (`DN-000012`, a counter row moved under its lock like a receipt number). `ReturnedToVendor` is written in the same transaction and inventory-svc deducts the stock FIFO from the store's available batches as `RTV` movements against the return; when inventory-svc can be reached, on-hand is checked first and a return of goods already sold is `422 PURCHASE_RTV_INSUFFICIENT_STOCK` here rather than a skipped line there. **The purchase order's status is untouched**: what was received was received, the three-way match still compares the invoice to it, and the debit note is the offset — which is how accounts payable expects it; `GET /purchase-orders/{id}/progress` now carries `qtyReturned` per line. `Idempotency-Key` makes a retried raise return the same document. Warehouse and management roles (`STOREKEEPER` and up), assigned to the order's store; a cashier is `403`.
- `GET /vendor-returns?poId=`, `GET /vendor-returns/{id}` — the returns, newest first, each with its lines: the debit note as a document. Any staff role; another tenant's is `404`.
- `POST /vendor-returns/{id}/credit` `{creditNoteNumber, creditNoteDate, amount?}` — record the supplier's credit note, closing the return (`RAISED` → `CREDITED`; `amount` defaults to the debit note's gross). **Posts `Dr 2100 Trade Creditors` for the credit, `Cr 2201 VAT Input` for its VAT share (the return's own VAT ratio applied to the amount, so a partial credit splits the way the debit note did) and `Cr Stock` for the rest**, dated the credit note, on the return's store; `409 PURCHASE_PERIOD_CLOSED` when that month is closed. Once only: a second is `409 PURCHASE_RTV_ALREADY_CREDITED` naming the first. Management-only — matching money received to money owed is a finance decision.
- `POST /landed-costs` `{grId, chargeType, basis, amount, currency?, reference?, chargedBy?, notes?}` — **landed cost apportionment (07.x)**: freight, duty, insurance, handling or another charge landed on a goods receipt after the goods. `chargeType` is `FREIGHT`, `DUTY`, `INSURANCE`, `HANDLING` or `OTHER`; `basis` is `BY_VALUE` (each line's value at the order's prices) or `BY_QUANTITY`; `chargedBy` names the carrier or broker on the supplier file, or is null for the goods supplier. The charge is spread over the receipt's lines **exactly** — each share to the currency's minor unit, the rounding remainder on the heaviest line — posted `Dr Stock / Cr 2110 Landed Costs Accrued`, and announced as `LandedCostApplied` so inventory-svc lifts the cost of the receipt's batches by each line's `perUnit`. The carrier's own bill is settled against 2110 through the ledger; it is not a supplier invoice against the order, which the three-way match would rightly refuse. Supports `Idempotency-Key`. Refused: an unknown type or basis or a zero amount (400 `PURCHASE_LANDED_INVALID`), a charge in another currency than the order's (400 `PURCHASE_LANDED_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` — convert it at the rate paid and keep the rate in the notes), a receipt or carrier that is not this business's (404), a closed period (409), a `BY_VALUE` charge over an order priced at zero (422 `PURCHASE_LANDED_NO_BASIS`, which says to use `BY_QUANTITY`). OWNER, MANAGER, STOREKEEPER.
- `GET /landed-costs?grId=|poId=`, `GET /landed-costs/{id}` — the charges on a receipt, on an order, or in the business, newest first; one charge with its lines (`qty`, `lineValue`, `amount`, `perUnit`).
- `POST /landed-costs/{id}/reversal` `{reason}` — reverse an applied charge: the mirror posted (`Dr 2110 / Cr Stock`), `LandedCostReversed` published so inventory takes the uplift back, the charge and its lines kept and marked. A charge is never edited — apply a new one for the corrected figure. Refused without a reason (400) and a second time (409 `PURCHASE_LANDED_REVERSED`).
- `POST /e-invoices` — **receive a supplier's e-invoice (07.13).** The body is the document itself: UBL 2.1 or CII D16B XML (`application/xml`, `text/xml`) or a Factur-X / ZUGFeRD PDF (`application/pdf`), up to 20 MB. It is read by `shared/einvoice` with DTDs refused and its size, depth and element counts capped (`400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_<code>` naming what was wrong — `NOT_AN_INVOICE`, `DTD_REFUSED`, `PDF_UNREADABLE`, `PDF_ENCRYPTED`… — `400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_EMPTY`, `413 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_TOO_LARGE`, `415 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_MEDIA_TYPE`), checked against EN 16931 and, when it says it is one, Peppol BIS Billing 3.0, and kept byte for byte: the same bytes again answer `200` with `alreadyReceived` and the first receipt. Then it is matched. **Supplier:** the electronic address it was sent from, else a seller VAT number exactly one supplier holds. **Order:** the id in its order reference (BT-13). **Lines:** the order line each references (id or position), then an item code a person matched before, then the buyer's own variant id on the order. Complete, it is captured through the three-way match exactly as `POST /supplier-invoices` captures a keyed one (`CAPTURED`, `supplierInvoiceId`), or its credit note closes the only open return on the order, or the one for its gross (`CREDITED`); a number the supplier already has captured is `DUPLICATE`. Otherwise it waits with `problem` in words: `NEEDS_SUPPLIER`, `NEEDS_ORDER`, `NEEDS_LINES`, `NEEDS_RETURN`, or `NEEDS_DECISION` when the capture itself was refused. A fatal rule makes it `NOT_COMPLIANT` with its `violations`; a buyer VAT number or electronic address that is not this business's makes it `MISDIRECTED`. The business's own are tenant-svc's `vatNumber`, `einvoiceScheme` and `einvoiceId` on `PUT /admin/tenant` (empty removes one; `400 TENANT_VAT_NUMBER_INVALID`, `400 TENANT_EINVOICE_ADDRESS_INVALID`); a supplier's are `einvoiceScheme` and `einvoiceId` on `POST`/`PUT /suppliers` (both or neither, a Peppol EAS code with its check digits: `400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_ADDRESS_INVALID`; one supplier per address: `409 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_EINVOICE_ADDRESS_TAKEN`). Any staff role.
- `GET /e-invoices?status=&limit=`, `GET /e-invoices/{id}`, `GET /e-invoices/{id}/document` — newest first (`limit` up to 100; a status that does not exist is `400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_STATUS_INVALID`); one with its lines, what each was matched by (`ORDER_LINE`, `ITEM_CODE`, `PERSON`) and the rules it broke; and the original as it arrived, with its media type, `Content-Disposition` and `nosniff`. Another tenant's is `404 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_NOT_FOUND`.
- `POST /e-invoices/{id}/match` `{supplierId, poId, returnId, lines:[{position, poLineId}], remember}` — **a person finishes a waiting e-invoice.** Anything left out is found again as on arrival; what is chosen is checked (`400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_ORDER_NOT_SUPPLIERS`, `PURCHASE_EINVOICE_LINE_UNKNOWN`, `PURCHASE_EINVOICE_LINE_NOT_ON_ORDER`, `PURCHASE_EINVOICE_LINE_CHOSEN_TWICE`, `PURCHASE_EINVOICE_RETURN_NOT_OPEN`). `remember` keeps the supplier's electronic address and which of its item codes are which variant, so its next invoice matches on its own. A settled, non-compliant or misdirected document is `409 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_SETTLED`, `…_NOT_COMPLIANT`, `…_MISDIRECTED`. Two people at once is `409 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_BUSY`: the first takes a claim that lapses after two minutes, so ten matches sent together make one invoice.
- `POST /e-invoices/{id}/refuse` `{reason}` — kept, not captured (`REFUSED`, with the reason and who decided). Needs `purchasing.invoices.decide`; a settled document is `409 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_SETTLED`. 
- `POST /e-invoices/inbound/{network}` — **a network delivers a supplier's e-invoice (the transport seam).** What a Peppol access point, France's approved platform or this platform's own simulated network calls, `network` being `peppol`, `fr_pdp` or `simulated` (`400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_NETWORK_UNKNOWN` for anything else: KSeF is pulled by the buyer and India's portal delivers nothing). Public at the gateway and in the service like a payment webhook — the provider holds no token and names no tenant — and authenticated instead by the deployment's delivery key, presented as `X-EInvoice-Key` and held in constant time against `storeql.einvoice.inbound.key` before a byte is read (`401 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_KEY_REFUSED`; `503 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_DELIVERY_OFF` on a deployment with no key, which takes no deliveries). The receiver is the business the document itself names as its buyer — by electronic address (BT-49), else by VAT identifier (BT-48) — found platform-wide through tenant-svc (`GET /platform/tenants/by-einvoice-address`), never by anything in the request: identity headers on a delivery choose nothing. `422 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_RECEIVER_UNNAMED` for a document naming neither; `404 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_RECEIVER_UNKNOWN` when no active business holds what it names; `409 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_RECEIVER_SHARED` when more than one does, since a delivery to a shared address lands nowhere. The network's own reference, `X-EInvoice-Reference` (up to 200 characters: `400 PURCHASE_EINVOICE_REFERENCE_TOO_LONG`), is kept as the document's `deliveryRef`. Then the document goes through the intake exactly as an upload does — the same media types, caps and refusals, the same matching and capture, written by nobody — with the network as its `channel`; and the network learns `{id, network, reference, alreadyReceived, receivedAt}` and nothing of the receiver's own: `201` when delivered, `200` when the same bytes were already there. Ten deliveries at once of one document make one row.

### Supplier payment runs (`/payment-runs`, 17.10, 17.12)
Every route needs a management role **and** `finance.payments`; a storekeeper or cashier is `403`, another tenant's run `404 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_NOT_FOUND`.
- `POST /payment-runs` `{payUpTo, paymentDate, currency?}` — **propose a run.** Collects every invoice that may be paid — `MATCHED`, or `FLAGGED` then `APPROVED`; posted; unpaid; in the run's currency (the tenant's own when omitted); due on or before `payUpTo`; not held by another open run — and each supplier's unallocated credit notes (vendor returns `CREDITED`), which are offset against what that supplier is paid. A supplier with no bank details, or whose credits cover its invoices, is not paid and is listed under `excluded` with `NO_BANK_DETAILS` or `NET_NOT_POSITIVE`; a supplier whose bank details changed in the last 14 days is paid but carries the warning `BANK_DETAILS_CHANGED_RECENTLY`, because a changed account on a supplier about to be paid is the pattern payment-diversion fraud leaves. The run gets a reference such as `PAY260913-3F9A1C` (at most 18 characters, for a BACS reference) and holds its documents: a partial unique index lets a document sit in one open run at a time, so a second proposal does not pick them up and ten racing proposals produce one run and nine `409`s (`PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_NOTHING_DUE` or `PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_CONFLICT`). `paymentDate` before today, or either date more than a year ahead, is `400 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_DATE_INVALID`. Nothing payable is `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_NOTHING_DUE` with each supplier left out, and why, in `details`. `201` with the run.
- `GET /payment-runs?status=&limit=`, `GET /payment-runs/{id}` — runs newest first, each with `suppliers` (per supplier: `net`, `warnings`, `remittanceEmailOnFile` and its `documents`, invoices paid and credit notes offset) and `excluded`. An open run is judged against the suppliers as they stand, so one that can no longer be paid moves to `excluded`; a paid or cancelled run is shown as it was. Bank details never appear. An unknown status is `400 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_STATUS_UNKNOWN`.
- `POST /payment-runs/{id}/approve` — `PROPOSED` → `APPROVED`, by someone other than the proposer (`403 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_SELF_APPROVAL`) unless the approver is the owner, because a one-person business still has to pay its suppliers. A supplier in the run that can no longer be paid is `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_STALE`; a run already approved `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_ALREADY_APPROVED`.
- `GET /payment-runs/{id}/bank-file?format=` — **the file for the bank**, named from the run reference, `Cache-Control: no-store`. Only for an `APPROVED` or `PAID` run (`409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_NOT_APPROVED`, `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_CANCELLED`), and refused with `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_BANK_DETAILS_CHANGED`, naming the suppliers, when any supplier's bank details changed after the run was approved. An unknown format is `400 PURCHASE_BANK_FILE_FORMAT_UNKNOWN`.
  - `CSV` (the default) — for a bank's bulk upload (`text/csv`): `payee_name,sort_code,account_number,iban,bic,amount,currency,reference`, one payment per supplier, CRLF line endings. Every text cell is guarded against formula injection (a payee named `=SUM(A1)` is written `'=SUM(A1)`).
  - `PAIN001` — an ISO 20022 `pain.001.001.09` SEPA credit transfer (`application/xml`) for a euro run: one payment block from the euro paying account (service level `SEPA`, charge bearer `SLEV`, batch booking, the payment date as the requested execution date) and one transaction per supplier, carrying the creditor's IBAN (and BIC when known), the run reference as message id and remittance, and an end-to-end id derived from the run and the supplier — the same in every file for the run, so the bank's report matches back to it. Names and references are written in the SEPA Latin character set and cut to the scheme's lengths. Refused with `409`: a run not in euro (`PURCHASE_BANK_FILE_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`), no euro paying account (`PURCHASE_PAYING_ACCOUNT_MISSING`), a supplier with no IBAN (`PURCHASE_BANK_FILE_PAYEE_UNSUPPORTED`, naming them).
  - `BACS18` — a Bacs Standard 18 file (`text/plain`) for a sterling run: `VOL1`, `HDR1`, `HDR2` and `UHL1` labels (the processing day one banking day before the payment date, on the three-day cycle), one 100-character standard record per supplier (transaction code `99`), the contra record (`17`) debiting the paying account, then `EOF1`, `EOF2` and `UTL1` with the totals. The file's serial and number are derived from the run, so a file sent twice carries the same serial and the bank refuses the second. Refused with `409`: a run not in sterling, no sterling paying account with a service user number, a payment date before the earliest a file made today can pay (`PURCHASE_BANK_FILE_TOO_LATE`, that date in `details`), a supplier with no sort code and account number (`PURCHASE_BANK_FILE_PAYEE_UNSUPPORTED`).
  - Both bank formats are refused with `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_PAYING_ACCOUNT_CHANGED` when the account the run pays from changed after approval: a run pays from the paying account in force when it was approved or, approved before any was set, the first one set after.
- `GET /payment-runs/paying-accounts`, `PUT /payment-runs/paying-accounts/{currency}` `{accountName, sortCode?, accountNumber?, iban?, bic?, serviceUserNumber?}` — **the accounts supplier payments are made from**, one per currency: the latest set, earlier ones kept. The holder's name with a UK sort code and account number or an IBAN (check digits verified), a BIC optional; a euro account needs its IBAN; a six-digit Bacs service user number goes with a UK account. Account numbers and IBANs come back masked. Anything else is `400 PURCHASE_PAYING_ACCOUNT_INVALID` naming what is wrong; a currency that is not ISO 4217 is `400 PURCHASE_INVALID_CURRENCY`.
- `POST /payment-runs/{id}/status-report` (the bank's ISO 20022 `pain.002` customer payment status report as `application/xml`) — **the bank's answer to a file**, for an `APPROVED` run (`409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_NOT_AWAITING_BANK` otherwise). Each payment is matched by its end-to-end id, and its status, reason code and the Verification of Payee result the bank returns (`MTCH`; `CMTC` with the name the bank holds; `NMTC`; `NOAP`) are recorded; a payment with no status of its own takes the report's group status. A rejected payment (`RJCT`), a payee the bank could not match (`NMTC`) or matched only closely (`CMTC`) is **held**, as each supplier's `bankCheck` in the run shows, and the run is not paid while it is. The latest report on a payment is the one in force; a report is recorded once by its message id, so uploading it again changes nothing. Refused: not well-formed, a DTD or entity, more than 2,000,000 characters or 10,000 payments, an unknown status (`400 PURCHASE_STATUS_REPORT_INVALID`); a report whose original message id is not the run reference (`409 PURCHASE_STATUS_REPORT_NOT_FOR_RUN`); a report naming a payment the run does not make (`409 PURCHASE_STATUS_REPORT_UNKNOWN_PAYMENT`).
- `POST /payment-runs/{id}/payments/{supplierId}/release` `{reason}` — **release a held close match** a manager has checked, saying what was checked. Once: ten concurrent releases release it once and the rest are `409 PURCHASE_PAYEE_ALREADY_RELEASED`. A payment the bank did not hold is `409 PURCHASE_PAYEE_NOT_HELD`; a rejected payment or a payee the bank could not match is `409 PURCHASE_PAYEE_NOT_RELEASABLE` — cancel the run, or upload the bank's later report once the supplier's details are right.
- `POST /payment-runs/{id}/pay` — **pay an approved run**, all in one transaction: the run `PAID`, every invoice it holds `paid` with the run's id, every credit note allocated, and `Dr 2100 Trade Creditors / Cr 1200 Bank` posted per supplier and store, dated the payment date, source `SUPPLIER_PAYMENT` with the run as `sourceRef` (a store whose credits exceed its invoices posts the other way round). `SupplierRemittanceIssued` is written per supplier with an event id derived from the run and the supplier. The state condition is inside the `UPDATE`, so twenty concurrent calls pay once and the rest are `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_ALREADY_PAID`; a document that changed under the run is `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_STALE` and nothing is written. Also refused: `PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_PAYEE_HELD` while the bank holds a payment no manager has released, naming each supplier and why; `PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_NOT_APPROVED`, `PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_CANCELLED`, `PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_BANK_DETAILS_CHANGED` and `PURCHASE_PERIOD_CLOSED` for a store whose period covering the payment date is closed.
- `POST /payment-runs/{id}/cancel` `{reason}` — a `PROPOSED` or `APPROVED` run to `CANCELLED` with a required reason, releasing its documents for the next run. A paid run is `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_ALREADY_PAID`, a cancelled one `409 PURCHASE_PAYMENT_RUN_CANCELLED`.

### Wave picking (`/admin/inventory/waves`) and directed putaway (`/admin/inventory/putaway`)

**The orders waiting at a store, gathered into one walk through the zones; stock arriving with no zone placed by rule.** inventory-svc keeps which online orders for `PICKUP` or `DELIVERY` are confirmed and not yet fulfilled at each store, from order-svc's `OrderConfirmed` (with their lines), cleared as `OrderFulfilled` and `OrderCancelled` arrive; a till sale is never waved. A wave gathers them (all waiting, or the ones named) into a pick list: one line per batch the resolved picking rule directs to (FEFO by default, FIFO, grade, zone priority), in the walk order the rule's zone priorities give (other zones by id, stock with no zone last), each naming the orders it serves in confirmation order. Picks are recorded per line, at most what was directed; completing the wave deducts exactly the picked batches as `SALE` movements against each order (a short pick shared to the earliest order first), reduces the orders' holds by as much (a hold emptied is consumed), records what each order still needs, and publishes **`WavePicked`** `{waveId, storeId, orders: [{orderId, lines: [{variantId, qty}]}]}` on the same transaction; order-svc fulfils each order it names for exactly those quantities (partially where short), once per event. The `OrderFulfilled` that follows finds the wave's pick for the line and records its revenue without deducting again; a line fulfilled by hand deducts as always. Cancelling a wave moves nothing: the orders wait for the next one. Building, picking and completing need `stock.transfer`; store scope applies as on every inventory route.

- `GET /waves/awaiting?storeId=` — the orders waiting, earliest first (by when order-svc confirmed them), with `qtyOutstanding` per line and the open `waveId` if any; with no store named, a keeper of one store reads that store and a keeper of several is asked which (`400 STORE_REQUIRED`); the list is bounded (500 earliest) rather than cursored, since a wave takes it whole.
- `POST /waves { storeId, orderIds? }` (`Idempotency-Key`; the same key returns the same wave) → `201` with `lines [{ id, walkOrder, zoneId, batchId, batchNo, variantId, directedQty, pickedQty, orders [{ orderId, qty, pickedQty }] }]`; `409 INVENTORY_WAVE_NOTHING_TO_PICK` (nothing waits, or nothing on the shelf for it), `INVENTORY_WAVE_ORDER_IN_ANOTHER_WAVE`. `GET /waves?storeId=&status=`, `GET /waves/{id}` (`404 INVENTORY_WAVE_NOT_FOUND`).
- `POST /waves/{id}/picks { lines: [{ lineId, pickedQty }] }` (`400 INVENTORY_WAVE_LINE_UNKNOWN`, `INVENTORY_WAVE_PICK_EXCEEDS_LINE`; `409 INVENTORY_WAVE_NOT_OPEN`); `POST /waves/{id}/complete` (`409 INVENTORY_WAVE_NOT_OPEN`; `422 INVENTORY_WAVE_STOCK_GONE` when a picked batch no longer holds what was picked, or is no longer available and duty-paid at the store) — each order's share is drawn only up to what it still waits for (an order cancelled or handed over by hand meanwhile is not drawn; the line's per-order `pickedQty` says what actually left), a consignment batch drawn announces `ConsignmentStockSold`, and `WavePicked` names the orders and quantities for order-svc to hand over once; `POST /waves/{id}/cancel`. A null pick line is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`; lists take `status` OPEN, COMPLETED or CANCELLED (`400 INVENTORY_WAVE_STATUS_INVALID`).
- **Putaway.** Every batch that arrives with no zone — a purchase receipt, a transfer received, a manual receipt that named none, a return — is placed inside the arrival's transaction by the store's rule for its product, else the store default; with neither it lands on the putaway list. `PUT /putaway/rules { storeId, variantId?, zoneId }` (OWNER, MANAGER; no variant is the store default; replaces an earlier rule for the same product), `GET /putaway/rules?storeId=`, `DELETE /putaway/rules/{id}` (`404 INVENTORY_PUTAWAY_RULE_NOT_FOUND`; the rule's own store is checked, so a manager of one store removes no other's). `GET /putaway/tasks?storeId=` — the batches waiting to be placed (scoped and bounded as the waiting list is), each with its batch's `batchNo` (read with the task, in the same tenant-scoped query; absent when the batch has none); `POST /putaway/tasks/{id}/place { zoneId? }` (`stock.transfer`) sets the batch's zone and answers the task, `batchNo` included (`400 INVENTORY_PUTAWAY_ZONE_REQUIRED` when no rule suggests one and none is named; `409 INVENTORY_PUTAWAY_TASK_PLACED`). A placement is not a stock movement.

### Depot / DC replenishment (`/admin/inventory/network`)

**A warehouse serves shops; a served shop is replenished by transfer, and the warehouse buys for it.** ([intent](../intent/depot-dc-replenishment.md)) Only a store of type `WAREHOUSE` serves, and only shops; tenant-svc refuses a store type other than `STORE` or `WAREHOUSE` (`400 TENANT_STORE_TYPE_INVALID`), and inventory-svc reads the types through `TenantProfiles`, never joined.

- `GET /network/serving` — each shop the caller may see, its warehouse, the days from it and the products it buys `direct`.
- `PUT /network/serving { storeId, warehouseId, leadTimeDays }` (OWNER, MANAGER) — set or replace a shop's warehouse (`400 INVENTORY_SERVING_SELF`, `INVENTORY_SERVING_STORE_UNKNOWN`, `INVENTORY_SERVING_NOT_A_WAREHOUSE`, `INVENTORY_SERVING_WAREHOUSE_TO_WAREHOUSE`, `INVENTORY_SERVING_LEAD_TIME_INVALID` outside 0–90); `DELETE /network/serving/{storeId}` (`204`; `404 INVENTORY_SERVING_NOT_FOUND`) — the shop buys everything direct again.
- `PUT` / `DELETE /network/serving/{storeId}/direct/{variantId}` (OWNER, MANAGER; `204`) — a product the shop buys direct from its supplier, not from its warehouse (`404 INVENTORY_SERVING_NOT_FOUND` for a shop nobody serves; `INVENTORY_SERVING_EXCEPTION_NOT_FOUND`).
- `GET /network/stock?variants=` (staff; at most 200 products, `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` beyond) — what each store can give of the products named (`levels[]`: `storeId`, `variantId`, `available`) and which of them a supplier ships per order (`dropship[]`). order-svc's read when it routes a delivery order across shops ([order orchestration](../intent/order-orchestration-and-split-fulfilment.md)); never shown to a shopper.
- `GET /network/sourcing?storeId=` — how a store is supplied: a shop's `servedBy`, `leadTimeDays` and `direct`; a warehouse's `shops` and `demand` per product (the shops' 28-day forecast summed, else their average daily demand × 28; their daily demand; what is `committed` to them in DRAFT and PENDING transfers). Read by purchase-svc's order proposal.
- `POST /network/proposals { warehouseId, coverDays? }` (`stock.transfer` at the warehouse; `Idempotency-Key`, looked up first) → `201` — per shop and product the warehouse serves (not bought direct): when on hand plus inbound (proposed, released or shipped transfers not yet received) is at or below the shop's reorder point, back to it plus the forecast (else its daily demand) over the lead time and `coverDays` (default 7, 1–60, `400 INVENTORY_PROPOSAL_COVER_INVALID`); when the warehouse holds less than they need (on hand less holds less what is committed), a fair share in proportion to need, rounded down, the rest to the least cover; one `DRAFT` `INTRANSIT` transfer per shop, a reason on every line. `400 INVENTORY_SERVING_NOT_A_WAREHOUSE`; `409 INVENTORY_PROPOSAL_NOTHING_SERVED`, `INVENTORY_PROPOSAL_OPEN` (a draft from the warehouse still waits). `GET /network/proposals?warehouseId=` — the last twenty runs.
- **Purchasing** (purchase-svc's `POST /purchase-orders/proposals/run`) reads the sourcing: a shop a warehouse serves proposes only what it buys direct; a warehouse proposes for its shops — the reorder point is their daily demand over the warehouse's lead time (its own plan's, else the supplier's quote; neither is skipped `no lead time for the warehouse`), the cover their forecast, the position its stock less what is committed to them, and the line's reason says `for the N shops it serves`.

### Cross-docking (`/purchase-orders/{id}/lines/{lineId}/allocations`, `/admin/inventory/network/crossdock`)

**A warehouse's delivery goes straight across the dock to the shops it was ordered for.** ([intent](../intent/cross-docking.md))

- purchase-svc: `PUT /purchase-orders/{id}/lines/{lineId}/allocations { allocations: [{ storeId, qty }] }` (OWNER, MANAGER, STOREKEEPER) — replace a line's allocations to the shops the warehouse serves while the order is a DRAFT; empty clears them. `400 PURCHASE_ALLOCATION_NOT_A_WAREHOUSE` (the order is not delivered to a warehouse), `PURCHASE_ALLOCATION_NOT_SERVED`, `PURCHASE_ALLOCATION_EXCEEDS_LINE`, `PURCHASE_ALLOCATION_QTY_INVALID`; `409 PURCHASE_ALLOCATION_ORDER_NOT_DRAFT`, `PURCHASE_ALLOCATION_STOCK_NOT_OWNED` (consignment or duty-suspended); `503 PURCHASE_NETWORK_UNAVAILABLE`. `POST …/allocations/fill` — allocate the whole line by the served shops' current needs (inventory-svc's `GET /admin/inventory/network/needs?warehouseId=&variantId=&qty=`: the replenishment rule and its fair share). `GET /purchase-orders/{id}/allocations`.
- Submitting the order publishes `CrossDockAllocationsSet` (a snapshot: `{poId, warehouseId, allocations: [{variantId, storeId, qty}]}`, keyed by the order); rejecting, cancelling or closing it short publishes an empty one. A warehouse's purchase proposal leaves what is allocated to shops out of its own on-order.
- inventory-svc keeps what each shop is owed (`crossdock_expected`, replaced whole per snapshot) and counts it as on its way to the shop. `GET /admin/inventory/network/crossdock?purchaseOrderId=` — what the order still owes each shop. At the warehouse's receipt, the lines the order still owes go across the dock on one transaction: per line, a batch for what crosses (never put away) and one for the rest (put away as any delivery); per shop, one `PENDING` `INTRANSIT` transfer (`source: CROSSDOCK`, `purchaseOrderId`, `goodsReceiptId`) whose lines ship from that batch first; a short line shared in proportion to what each shop was owed, the remainder to the least cover, the line saying so; what was owed drawn down.

### Fresh yield, preparation and butchery loss (`/admin/inventory/yield`)

**A primal is broken into cuts; what the cuts do not account for is the loss, and the cuts carry the primal's cost.** A yield template says what a primal (a side of beef, a whole salmon, a sack of potatoes) should break into: each cut's expected share of the input quantity, its share of the cost (by weight unless the business says otherwise — a fillet is worth more per kilo than the mince from the same side) and its own shelf life; what the shares leave is the expected loss. A breakdown at a store consumes the primal (a `YIELD` movement out of its batches, oldest first), makes each cut a batch of its own **under the primal's lot** (a `YIELD` movement in, a `TRANSFORM` link in the lot genealogy, dated by the cut's shelf life or the primal's date) at the primal's cost apportioned by share and spread over what came out, and records the loss against what was expected, at the primal's unit cost. The loss is not shrinkage and carries no cost of its own: it is absorbed by the cuts, which is why they cost more per unit than the primal did. Quantities are in the primal's own unit throughout. The stock projections elsewhere hear the primal go (`StockAdjusted`) and each cut arrive (`StockReceived`); the run itself is `YieldRecorded`.

- `POST /templates { name, inputVariantId, unit?, notes?, outputs: [{ variantId, expectedPct, costShare?, shelfLifeDays? }] }` (OWNER, MANAGER) → `201`; `400 INVENTORY_YIELD_OUTPUTS_REQUIRED`, `INVENTORY_YIELD_OUTPUT_IS_INPUT`, `INVENTORY_YIELD_OUTPUT_DUPLICATE`, `INVENTORY_YIELD_SHARES_INVALID` (the shares add up to more than 100). `GET /templates` (live first, with `expectedLossPct`), `GET /templates/{id}`, `POST /templates/{id}/end` (`404 INVENTORY_YIELD_TEMPLATE_NOT_FOUND`).
- `POST /runs { storeId, templateId, inputQty, outputs: [{ variantId, qty }], reference?, notes? }` (any staff with access to the store) → `201` with `inputCost`, `outputQty`, `lossQty`, `lossPct`, `expectedLossQty`, `lossVariance` (positive when more was lost than expected), `lossAtCost` and each cut's `expectedQty`, `unitCost` and `batchId`. A cut the template names but the run leaves out came to nothing. `400 INVENTORY_YIELD_OUTPUT_UNKNOWN`, `INVENTORY_YIELD_OUTPUT_EXCEEDS_INPUT` (more out than in); `409 INVENTORY_YIELD_TEMPLATE_ENDED`, `INVENTORY_YIELD_INPUT_NOT_OWNED` (the primal drawn is a supplier's consignment stock); `422 INVENTORY_YIELD_INSUFFICIENT_INPUT`. Nothing is drawn by a refusal.
- `GET /runs?storeId=&from=&to=` — the period's breakdowns, newest first, with `totals` (`runs`, `inputQty`, `outputQty`, `lossQty`, `expectedLossQty`, `lossAtCost`): the butchery-loss report (`400 INVENTORY_PERIOD_INVALID`).

### Bonded and duty-suspended stock (`/admin/inventory/bond`)

**A bonded warehouse holds excise goods with the duty suspended: on hand, never for sale, valued at cost without the duty; the duty crystallises when the goods are released to home use.** A store is approved by management with the revenue's approval number and regime; the platform derives no duty from strength or volume — management sets the duty one unit crystallises, in the home currency, with a note on how it was worked out. A storekeeper releases stock; the duty is quantity × rate rounded to the currency's minor units, the released units become a duty-paid batch of their own (same lot, cost and dates, with lot genealogy), and `DutyReleased` is published on the release's transaction for purchase-svc to owe.

- `PUT /approvals/{storeId} { approvalNumber, regime }` (OWNER, MANAGER) — approve, or re-approve, a store as a bonded warehouse; `regime` is `EXCISE` or `CUSTOMS` (`400 INVENTORY_BOND_REGIME_INVALID`). `POST /approvals/{storeId}/end` ends it (`404 INVENTORY_BOND_APPROVAL_NOT_FOUND`): what is already in bond stays in bond and can still be released, nothing more arrives suspended. `GET /approvals` — every approval, ended ones too.
- `PUT /duty-rates/{variantId} { dutyPerUnit, note? }` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the duty one unit crystallises, in the home currency; `GET /duty-rates`.
- `POST /releases { storeId, variantId, qty, reference? }` (any staff with access to the store) → `201 { qty, dutyPerUnit, dutyAmount, currency, reference, … }`. Draws duty-suspended batches oldest first as `BOND_RELEASE` movements (one out of the bonded batch, one into the new duty-paid one). `409 INVENTORY_STORE_NOT_BONDED` (never approved, or ended and nothing in bond), `409 INVENTORY_DUTY_RATE_MISSING`, `422 INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT_BONDED_STOCK`.
- `GET /releases?storeId=&from=&to=` — a period's releases and `totalDuty` (`400 INVENTORY_PERIOD_INVALID`); `GET /stock?storeId=` — what sits in bond per store and variant with `dutyPerUnit` and `dutyPotential`.

### RFQ and sourcing (`/rfqs`)

**Several suppliers asked for the same lines; the quotes read side by side in the business's own money; the award raises the orders.** A request is raised as `DRAFT` with its lines and the suppliers asked (`RFQ-000001`, the tenant's own series), issued when it goes out, and takes quotes only while `ISSUED`. The buyer records each supplier's quote — terms and a price per line in the supplier's currency; a line left out is a line they did not price — or their decline. The detail compares every price at home through the rates the business keeps (`FxRates`): the lowest per line is marked, each bid is totalled in its own money and at home, and bids that priced every line and can be read at home are ranked cheapest first; a price with no rate is shown but never lowest, a partial or untranslatable bid shown but never ranked. Each bid carries the supplier's scorecard grade (last 90 days), so price is read against the record. Awarding gives each line to a supplier who priced it and raises one `DRAFT` purchase order per awarded supplier (`source: RFQ`) in their currency at the quoted prices, expected on the day the goods are needed (or the quoted lead time from today), for a person to submit as any draft; a request is awarded once and not cancelled after. Buying roles (OWNER, MANAGER, STOREKEEPER) write; any staff read.

- `POST /rfqs { title, storeId, neededBy?, closesOn?, notes?, lines: [{ variantId, qty, notes? }], supplierIds }` → `201` (`400 PURCHASE_RFQ_LINES_REQUIRED`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_SUPPLIERS_REQUIRED`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_LINE_DUPLICATE`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_SUPPLIER_DUPLICATE`; `404 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND`). `GET /rfqs?status=&limit=` — newest first with `lines`, `suppliers` and `quotes` counts (`400 PURCHASE_RFQ_STATUS_INVALID`). `GET /rfqs/{id}` — the request with `lines`, `bids` (status, currency, terms, `grade`, `prices`), `comparison { homeCurrency, lines [{ variantId, qty, prices [{ supplierId, unitPrice, currency, homeUnitPrice, lineTotal, homeLineTotal, lowest }] }], bids [{ supplierId, status, complete, total, currency, homeTotal, rank }] }`, `awards` and `purchaseOrderIds` (`404 PURCHASE_RFQ_NOT_FOUND`).
- `POST /rfqs/{id}/issue` (`409 PURCHASE_RFQ_NOT_DRAFT`); `PUT /rfqs/{id}/quotes/{supplierId} { currency?, leadTimeDays?, validUntil?, notes?, lines: [{ variantId, unitPrice }] }` replaces the supplier's quote (`409 PURCHASE_RFQ_NOT_ISSUED`; `400 PURCHASE_RFQ_SUPPLIER_NOT_INVITED`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_LINE_UNKNOWN`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_QUOTE_EMPTY`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_LINE_DUPLICATE`); `POST /rfqs/{id}/quotes/{supplierId}/decline`.
- `POST /rfqs/{id}/award { awards: [{ variantId, supplierId }] }` (`409 PURCHASE_RFQ_NOT_ISSUED`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_NOT_QUOTED`; `400 PURCHASE_RFQ_AWARDS_REQUIRED`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_AWARD_DUPLICATE`, `PURCHASE_RFQ_LINE_UNKNOWN`). The orders are raised before the award is recorded, so an award that loses a race leaves drafts a person can cancel, never an award without its orders. `POST /rfqs/{id}/cancel { reason }` (`409 PURCHASE_RFQ_CLOSED` once awarded or cancelled).

### Supplier lead times and scorecards (`/suppliers/{id}/scorecard`, `/suppliers/scorecards`)

**Every goods receipt measures the delivery it books against the order's promise, and a period's facts are weighed into one card per supplier.** A supplier may quote a lead time (`leadTimeDays` on `POST`/`PUT /suppliers`, on every supplier answer); an order records when it went to the supplier (`submitted_at`, stamped when it becomes SUBMITTED, straight from DRAFT or through approval). On each receipt purchase-svc keeps a `supplier_deliveries` fact on the receipt's own transaction: when the order went out, the date it was due (the order's `expectedDelivery`, else the quote counted from the day it went out, else nothing), when the goods arrived, the whole days of lead time, the days late (negative when early, null when nothing was promised), whether the receipt completed the order, and the quantity. The card weighs what is known — deliveries on time 40, fill 30, quality 20 (the whole less the return rate), invoice accuracy 10 — and renormalises over the parts that exist, so a supplier nobody has invoiced yet is not marked down for it; a share of nothing is unknown, never zero. Grades: A from 90, B from 75, C from 60, D below. Management only (OWNER, MANAGER, PLATFORM_ADMIN); the period is `from`–`to` inclusive, the last 90 days when unsaid (`400 PURCHASE_PERIOD_INVALID` when it ends before it starts).

- `GET /suppliers/scorecards?from=&to=` — every supplier's card, the best first, those with nothing to judge last and unscored: `{ supplierId, supplierName, leadTimeDays, from, to, deliveries { count, avgLeadDays, medianLeadDays, maxLeadDays, promised, onTime, late, onTimePct, avgDaysLate, receivedQty }, fill { orders, orderedQty, receivedQty, fillRatePct, shortClosed }, quality { returns, returnedQty, returnRatePct }, invoices { invoices, flagged, accuracyPct }, score, grade }`. Fill is over the orders that reached their end in the period (received in full or closed short, by the day they went to the supplier); quality is what went back against what arrived in the period; invoice accuracy is the invoices that matched against all captured in it.
- `GET /suppliers/{id}/scorecard?from=&to=` — one supplier's card (`404 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND`).
- `GET /suppliers/{id}/deliveries?from=&to=` — the deliveries as measured, newest first (up to 200).

### Dropship (`/admin/dropship/arrangements`)

**Stock the business never holds.** An arrangement names the supplier that fulfils a variant per order and what it charges; one is live per variant. Making or ending one publishes **`VariantSourcingChanged`** `{variantId, fulfilment: DROPSHIP|STOCK, supplierId}`, which inventory-svc keeps so the variant is available with nothing on the shelf and a checkout hold on it draws nothing. A confirmed sale with such a line (`OrderConfirmed`, which now carries `fulfilmentType`, `deliveryAddress`, `deliveryRecipientName` and `deliveryRecipientPhone`) raises **one DRAFT purchase order per supplier** — `source: DROPSHIP`, `salesOrderId`, `shipTo` the customer, lines at the arrangement's cost — for a person to submit, as a proposal's draft is; once per event. Management only.

- `POST /admin/dropship/arrangements` `{variantId, supplierId, unitCost, vatCode?}` → `201` `{id, variantId, supplierId, unitCost, vatCode, active, createdAt, endedAt}`; `404 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND`; `409 PURCHASE_DROPSHIP_ARRANGEMENT_EXISTS` while the variant has a live one.
- `GET /admin/dropship/arrangements?limit=` — live ones first.
- `POST /admin/dropship/arrangements/{id}/end` — the variant is stocked again, and inventory-svc is told (`fulfilment: STOCK`); `409 PURCHASE_DROPSHIP_ARRANGEMENT_ENDED`, `404 PURCHASE_DROPSHIP_ARRANGEMENT_NOT_FOUND`.
- `POST /purchase-orders/{id}/dropship-delivered` — the supplier delivered to the customer: RECEIVED with no stock, Dr 5020 / Cr 2109 for the order's net; the supplier's invoice then clears GRNI as any other.
- **Not here (yet):** the supplier's shipment confirmation and tracking (delivery is marked by a person); a dropship order submitted automatically (a person submits, within spend authority, as for proposals); a dropship variant's price list cost fed from the arrangement.

### Excise duty (`/admin/duty`)

**The duty is owed the day the goods leave bond.** inventory-svc announces every release from a bonded warehouse (`DutyReleased`, with the quantity, the duty per unit and the amount in the home currency); purchase-svc records each once (`duty_releases`, unique on the event) and posts **Dr 5030 Excise Duty / Cr 2140 Excise Duty Payable** dated the day of release. A purchase order carries `dutyStatus` (`DUTY_PAID` or `DUTY_SUSPENDED`, `400 PURCHASE_DUTY_STATUS_INVALID`): a receipt against an order under bond tells inventory-svc the goods arrive with the duty suspended (`GoodsReceived.dutyStatus`) and posts the stock at cost without the duty, as any receipt does.

- `GET /admin/duty/releases?from=&to=` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the period's releases and `totalDuty` in the home currency: the figure an excise return is made from (`400 PURCHASE_DUTY_PERIOD_INVALID`).

### Consignment stock (`/admin/consignment`)

**Nothing is owed at the door; the sale is owed.** inventory-svc announces every sale drawn from a supplier's consignment batch (`ConsignmentStockSold`); purchase-svc records each once, at the order's price, and posts **Dr 5010 Purchases - Consignment / Cr 2100 Trade Creditors** the day it sold (source `CONSIGNMENT_SALE`) — the cost of goods that were never the business's stock, against the supplier it now owes. A sale for a supplier the business does not have is logged and dropped. Management only.

- `GET /admin/consignment/sales?supplierId=&settled=true|false&limit=` — each sale: `{id, supplierId, storeId, variantId, batchId, orderId, qty, unitCost, amount, currency, soldOn, settled, settlementId, recordedAt}`, newest first; `settled=false` is what no statement has taken yet. The currency is the supplier's invoicing currency.
- `POST /admin/consignment/settlements` `{supplierId, from, to}` → `201` — gathers every unsettled sale of the supplier sold within the period (in the supplier's currency) into one statement `{id, supplierId, reference "CS-…", periodFrom, periodTo, currency, total, salesCount, createdAt, sales}` the supplier invoices against. A sale is **settled once**: the settlement that took it is the one thing written to its row after it is recorded. `409 PURCHASE_CONSIGNMENT_NOTHING_TO_SETTLE` when nothing is left; `400 PURCHASE_CONSIGNMENT_PERIOD_INVALID`; `404 PURCHASE_SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND`.
- `GET /admin/consignment/settlements?supplierId=&limit=` · `GET /admin/consignment/settlements/{id}` (with its `sales`; `404 PURCHASE_CONSIGNMENT_SETTLEMENT_NOT_FOUND`).
- **Not here (yet):** the supplier's invoice matched against a statement (it is captured as a non-PO document, which this service does not model); a return of unsold consignment stock to the supplier as a distinct movement (it goes back as a return to vendor); dropship — see the readiness review.

### Intercompany & Ledger
- `POST /intercompany-invoices` — raise a matched AR/AP invoice pair for an inter-org inventory transfer.
- `GET /intercompany-invoices`, `GET /intercompany-invoices/{id}` — list/get intercompany invoices.
- `POST /intercompany-invoices/{id}/settle` — settle an intercompany invoice.
- `GET /nominal-ledger` — the double-entry nominal ledger line by line (filter by `?code=&from=&to=`, cursor-paged). Every line now carries `journalId` (the lines of one posting share it), `sourceType` (`GOODS_RECEIPT`, `SUPPLIER_INVOICE`, `INVOICE_REVERSAL`, `CREDIT_NOTE`, `SUPPLIER_PAYMENT`, `SALE`, `SALE_TENDER`, `SALE_REFUND`, `LOYALTY_DEFERRAL`, `LOYALTY_RELEASE`, `GIFT_CARD_LOAD`, `GIFT_CARD_BREAKAGE`, `INTERCOMPANY`, `SETTLEMENT`, `JOURNAL`; null for rows written before postings existed) and `storeId`. Readable by any member of staff, as before. **A journal's `description` names what it is about as people read it** — in the accounting package it is pushed to and on the Integrations screen — by `#` and the last eight characters of the id, the handle the screens show, **never by a whole id**: `Goods received against PO #0d72a43e`, `Sale #…`, `Tender (CARD) for sale #…`, `Refund for sale #…`, `Chargeback won on sale #…`, `Freight landed on receipt #… against PO #…` (its reversal `Freight on receipt #… against PO #… reversed: <reason>`), `Dropship PO #… for sale #… delivered to the customer`, `Loyalty points earned on sale #…`, `Gift card breakage on sale #…`, `Intercompany AR invoice #…`, `Consignment sale of 3 x variant #… (<supplier>)`. A handle is not a key — two ids can share one — so match on `sourceRef`, which still carries the id; journals written before this keep the whole id they were written with (the ledger is append-only).
- `POST /nominal-ledger/journals` `{entryDate, description, storeId?, lines:[{nominalCode, nominalName?, debit?, credit?}]}` — **a manual journal (17.1).** Management only. Two to fifty lines, each a debit or a credit and never both (`400 PURCHASE_JOURNAL_LINE_INVALID`), a nominal code of one to ten letters or digits, balancing to the penny (`422 PURCHASE_JOURNAL_UNBALANCED`). A store, when named, must be one the caller may operate in (`403 STORE_ACCESS_DENIED`) and its accounting period for the date must be open (`409 PURCHASE_PERIOD_CLOSED`); a tenant-level journal answers to no store's period. Returns the journal with its `journalId` and totals.
- `GET /nominal-ledger/journals/{journalId}` — one posting, whole. Management only; another tenant's is `404`.
- **Sales and tender posting (17.7).** The ledger now records what the business sells, from events rather than a route: each `PaymentCaptured` posts **Dr the tender's control account / Cr 1105 Sales Receipts Clearing** — `1210 Cash in Tills` for cash, `1250 Card and Wallet Clearing` for card, UPI and wallet, `2310 Gift Card and Voucher Liability`, `2320 Store Credit Liability`, and `1299 Unallocated Receipts` for a method nobody mapped — and the confirmed sale (`OrderConfirmed`, which now carries `taxAmount`) posts **Dr 1105 with the total / Cr 4010 Sales net / Cr 2200 VAT Output**. A sale paid in full therefore nets 1105 to zero for its order. A `PaymentRefunded` (now carrying each tender's share) credits the control account each share came from and debits 4010 and 2200 in the sale's own VAT ratio, or debits 1105 when the ledger never saw the sale confirmed. Every posting names the order as `sourceRef` and its store; each event, and each sale or tender announced twice, is posted once. Postings are dated the day received and are not refused in a closed period — the sale has happened; a late one is what the clearing report is for.
- `GET /nominal-ledger/sales-clearing?storeId=&limit=` — **the orders whose receipts clearing did not net to zero**: taken but never confirmed, confirmed for more than was taken, or refunded against a sale the ledger never saw, each with its balance (debit less credit: negative is money nobody's sale has claimed) and the first and last day posted, oldest first. Management only; a store the caller cannot operate in is `403`, a storeId that is not one `400`.
- `GET /nominal-ledger/trial-balance?from=&to=&storeId=` — **the trial balance (17.1):** every nominal code's debits, credits and balance (debit less credit) over the range, with `totalDebit`, `totalCredit` and `balanced`. Management only. `balanced` is the ledger's own invariant — every posting the service writes balances, so `false` is a fault to investigate, not a figure to report. A range that ends before it starts is `400 PURCHASE_INVALID_PERIOD`.
- `GET /nominal-ledger/deferred-revenue` — **deferred revenue for loyalty points and gift cards (17.11; FRS 102 s.23 as revised for periods from 1 Jan 2026, IFRS 15 B39–B47):** the estimates in force (`settings`: `pointValue`, `pointsBreakagePct`, `giftCardBreakagePct`, `reason`, `setBy`, `setAt`, `currency`; absent until first set) and their `history`; `pointsOutstanding`, `deferredIncome` (2330), `pointsUnmatched` (points spent before their earning reached the ledger) and `eventsAwaitingEstimates`; `giftCardsLoaded`, `giftCardsRedeemed`, `giftCardBreakage` (4031) and `giftCardLiability` since the ledger began to follow gift cards. Management only. **Points that expire (13.x):** a `LoyaltyExpired` event leaves the pool as a lapse the customer did not choose — no income is recognised for it — and once nothing is outstanding whatever deferred income remains is breakage (4030), which is what the breakage estimate was for.
- `PUT /nominal-ledger/deferred-revenue/settings` `{pointValue, pointsBreakagePct, giftCardBreakagePct, reason}` — **the tenant accountant's estimates.** Management only. A point's value above 0 and at most 1000 to four decimal places (`400 PURCHASE_POINT_VALUE_INVALID`), each breakage estimate 0 to 95 to two decimal places (`400 PURCHASE_BREAKAGE_OUT_OF_RANGE`), and a reason. Append-only; a change applies from then on. Loyalty events that arrived before any estimates are posted in the same transaction, oldest first. From customer-svc's `LoyaltyEarned/Redeemed/Adjusted` (by `eventId`), order-svc's `GiftCardLoaded` (by card transaction) and a `GIFT_CARD` tender (by payment), once each, the ledger posts: points earned with a sale `Dr 4010 / Cr 2330`, the points' share of the sale's net revenue by standalone selling price (points × value × (1 − breakage)); points given away `Dr 6410 / Cr 2330`; points spent `Dr 2330 / Cr 4020`, their share of what is deferred against the points expected to be spent, and the rest `Cr 4030` once no points remain; a gift card sold or reloaded `Dr` the tender's control account, or `6420` when given away, `/ Cr 2310`; a gift card spent `Dr 2310 / Cr 4031` breakage of amount × b / (1 − b), never more than b of what was loaded and reversed when spending overtakes the estimate.

### Accounting connectors (`/accounting`, 17.9)
- `GET /accounting/providers` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the packages that can be connected: `XERO`, `QUICKBOOKS`, `SAGE` and the platform's `SIMULATED` stand-in, each with the `settings` it needs (`tenantId`; `realmId` and optionally `environment`; `businessId`; none), the `optional` ones and where its `tokens` come from.
- `GET /accounting/connection` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the business's connection: provider, `status` (`ACTIVE`/`DISABLED`), `settings`, `syncFrom`, `hasRefreshToken`, `lastSyncAt`, `lastError`, and `counts` (`pending`, `delivered`, `failed`, `uncertain`, `skipped`). Never the tokens. `404 ACCOUNTING_NOT_CONNECTED`.
- `PUT /accounting/connection { provider, settings, credentials: { accessToken, refreshToken?, clientId?, clientSecret?, expiresAt? }, syncFrom }` (OWNER) — connect, replacing whatever was connected with its mapping and its log; the tokens are sealed under `storeql.accounting.secrets-key` and never shown again; journals dated from `syncFrom` are pushed. Refused: `400 ACCOUNTING_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN`, `ACCOUNTING_SETTINGS_INVALID` (a required setting missing or an unknown one given), `ACCOUNTING_CREDENTIALS_MISSING` (a real package with no access token), `ACCOUNTING_CREDENTIALS_INVALID`, `ACCOUNTING_SYNC_FROM_INVALID`; `503 ACCOUNTING_NOT_CONFIGURED` when the deployment has no sealing key. `DELETE /accounting/connection` (OWNER) — the tokens, the mapping and the log go; journals already in the package stay. `POST /accounting/connection/disable|enable` (OWNER).
