- `GET /accounting/connection/accounts` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the package's chart of accounts, read live (`id`: what a journal line names the account by — Xero's code, QuickBooks' and Sage's ids; `code`, `name`, `type`), which also proves the connection: `502 ACCOUNTING_PROVIDER_REFUSED` with the package's words, `503 ACCOUNTING_PROVIDER_UNREACHABLE`.
- `GET /accounting/connection/mappings`, `PUT /accounting/connection/mappings { mappings: [{ nominalCode, externalAccount, externalName? }] }` (management with `finance.journal`) — the whole mapping of the business's nominal codes onto the package's accounts; a code left unmapped is sent as itself; `400 ACCOUNTING_MAPPING_INVALID`.
- `POST /accounting/connection/sync` (management with `finance.journal`) → `{ queued, delivered, failed, uncertain }` — one pass now: every journal posted since the day chosen is queued and everything due is tried; the clock does the same every thirty seconds (`storeql.accounting.poll-seconds`). `409 ACCOUNTING_DISABLED`.
- `GET /accounting/syncs?status=&after=&limit=` (OWNER, MANAGER) — every journal's push, newest first, cursor on the id: `status` (`PENDING` with `nextAttemptAt`, `DELIVERED` with `externalId`, `FAILED`, `UNCERTAIN`, `SKIPPED`), `attempts`, `lastError`, and the journal's `entryDate`, `description`, `sourceType`, `total`. `GET /accounting/syncs/{id}` — whole, with `lines` and `attemptLog` (each try's `statusCode`, `error`, the package's answer and the duration). `POST /accounting/syncs/{id}/retry` — queued to go now, whatever it was waiting for; `POST /accounting/syncs/{id}/skip { reason }` — left out with the reason (`400 ACCOUNTING_REASON_REQUIRED`); both management with `finance.journal`, both `409 ACCOUNTING_SYNC_DELIVERED` for a journal already in the package. What each package receives, and what `UNCERTAIN` means: [docs/ACCOUNTING-CONNECTORS.md](ACCOUNTING-CONNECTORS.md).

**Business rules**
- Purchase orders run create (draft) → add lines → submit before goods can be received against them.
- **The status vocabulary is `DRAFT → SUBMITTED → PARTIALLY_RECEIVED → RECEIVED`**, with `CLOSED` (short-closed, balance abandoned) and `CANCELLED` (nothing ever received) as the two terminal exits. `CLOSED` is deliberately not `RECEIVED`: "we got it all" and "we gave up on the rest" are different facts, and a supplier scorecard that cannot tell them apart is worthless.
- **A partial delivery no longer closes the order.** A receipt is compared against what was ordered — under a `FOR UPDATE` lock on the order, so two deliveries arriving at once cannot both book the same balance — and the order lands in `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` or `RECEIVED` accordingly. Previously the status was set to `RECEIVED` with no reference to quantity, so 6 of 10 closed the order *and the second delivery of the remaining 4 was then refused*, stranding the balance with no PO to receive it against.
- **Over-receipt is refused** (`422 PURCHASE_OVER_RECEIPT`), cumulatively across deliveries rather than per delivery. Accepting more than was ordered would book stock nobody asked for against an order that cannot account for it, and a mistyped 60 for 6 would do it silently. Whether a tolerance band should be allowed is a per-tenant procurement policy, not something to invent.
- Goods receipt posting accepts an `Idempotency-Key`; a replayed receipt does not count its quantity twice.
- **The nominal ledger is written by the documents** (goods receipt, supplier invoice, rejection, credit note, intercompany invoice and settlement) **and by manual journals; every posting balances.** The balance is checked where lines are made — a `LedgerPosting` that does not balance does not build — rather than trusted to each caller, and the lines of one posting share a `journalId` so it can be read back whole and reversed line for line. Nothing is ever updated or deleted: a rejection is a reversal, not an erasure.
- **Period control (04.7)** reads the store's accounting periods from inventory-svc. A period is the calendar month of its `periodDate`; a month with no period is not controlled (periods are optional, and a business that has never opened one must not find its goods receipts refused); a month whose period is `CLOSED` refuses every posting dated in it. Unreachable is treated as open and logged: a discovery outage that refused every goods receipt would be a far larger outage than a posting landing in a month finance has closed.
- Invoices captured before the ledger was wired in have `postedAt` null and were not back-posted: a posting dated today for an invoice dated months ago would land in the wrong period, and inventing one is finance's decision, made with a journal.

**Events**
- Consumes: `OrderConfirmed`, `PaymentCaptured`, `PaymentRefunded` (17.7: posted to the nominal ledger as sales, tenders and refunds, deduped per event and per sale or tender). Also `PaymentDisputeOpened`, `PaymentDisputeFundsWithdrawn`, `PaymentDisputeClosed` (11.9): the disputed amount out of card clearing (1250) into card receipts in dispute (1255) with the acquirer's fee to 6511, back on a win, to chargeback losses (6510) otherwise; source type `CHARGEBACK`, once per event. And `SettlementReconciled` (11.10): a journal per store the payout covers — Dr 1200 bank for what the acquirer paid and Dr 6500 card processing fees / Cr 1250 card clearing for the payments covered and Cr 1299 unallocated receipts for money that answered to nothing; a negative figure changes sides; source type `CARD_SETTLEMENT`, dated the day it was reconciled and naming the payout and when it was paid; once per event.
- Publishes: `PurchaseOrderCreated`, `GoodsReceived` (with `dutyStatus`: a receipt against an order under bond arrives with the duty suspended), `IntercompanyInvoiceRaised`, `SupplierInvoiceCaptured` (SJ-D39: invoice id as event id, net/VAT/gross, invoice date as tax point — pricing-svc projects it into the VAT return's boxes 4 and 7), `SupplierInvoiceRejected` (07.7: the same figures on the same topic, projected as their negative so the rejected invoice leaves boxes 4 and 7), `ReturnedToVendor` (07.8: return id as event id, the store and the lines going back — inventory-svc deducts them as `RTV` movements, once per line), `SupplierRemittanceIssued` (17.10: one per supplier a payment run pays, on `storeql.purchase.supplier-remittance-issued` keyed by the run — the run reference, payment date, supplier, remittance email, currency, total and each document settled; notification-svc emails it as the remittance advice).

---

## reporting-svc

### Labour against sales (`/admin/reports/sales/labour`)

- `GET /admin/reports/sales/labour?from=&to=&storeId=` — **what each day took, and what the hours that earned it cost.** Neither number means much alone: takings without the cost of the hours is half a story, and a labour cost without takings is a number to worry about for no reason. Labour is projected from tenant-svc's clock, and **the event carries a store, a day and money and no person** — a labour figure is a fact about a shop's Saturday, and pay stays in the service that keeps it, which is what lets this projection exist at all. Keyed on the time entry, so the same announcement twice is one row and a **correction takes its predecessor's figure back out**: a report that counted a corrected day twice would look right and be wrong. A day with takings and no hours recorded is as real as a day with hours and no sales, and both appear. Where some of a day's hours had **no pay rate in force the cost is null rather than zero**, with `uncostedHours` beside it — a Saturday shown as free labour would be worse than one that says it does not know. `labourPercent` is labour as a share of net takings, the figure a shop is run on, and is null when nothing was taken: a percentage of nothing is no percentage, and reporting one would raise an alarm for a day the shop was closed. `salesPerHour` reads the same pair the other way.

### Inventory Analytics (`/admin/reports/inventory`)
- `GET /admin/reports/inventory/on-hand` — cross-store on-hand stock snapshot.
- `GET /admin/reports/inventory/supply-demand` — supply/demand netting (on-hand + open in-transit supply lines).
- `GET /admin/reports/inventory/movement-stats` — stock movement statistics bucketed by day/week/month.

### Sales Analytics (`/admin/reports/sales`)
- `GET /admin/reports/sales/summary?from=&to=&storeId=&channel=` — gross/refunded/net revenue and order count for a date range, grouped by currency. **A till sale voided after the fact is not takings**: order-svc's `OrderVoided` marks its fact (`sales_facts.voided_at`, the moment the void was heard) and the sale leaves the order count, the gross and — with it — any refund already made against it, so the net is never pushed below what was taken. The row and its lines are **kept, never deleted**, as the receipt keeps its number. Deduped per event id; the first void heard for an order stands and a redelivered `OrderConfirmed` does not bring the sale back; a void heard **before** its sale (a consumer catching up reads its topics in no particular order) is kept in `sales_voids` and marks the sale the moment it lands. Scoped by the event's tenant first, so another business's void naming the same order id changes nothing of ours.
- `GET /admin/reports/sales/by-day?from=&to=&storeId=&channel=` — daily revenue buckets per currency, newest day first; a voided sale is left out, as in the summary.
- `GET /admin/reports/sales/by-category?from=&to=&storeId=&channel=&level=leaf|top` — **what each category took (19.x)**, from the sale lines `OrderConfirmed` now carries and the catalogue's own word on where each variant sits (`ProductCategorised`, `VariantCreated`, projected into `catalogue_products`/`catalogue_variants`): one row per category and currency, largest first, with `orders`, `units`, `gross` (before refunds — a refund is known by order, not by line) and `share` of the currency's total. `level=top` rolls each category up to its top-level ancestor. A row with no `categoryId` is the lines the report cannot place — a product with no category, or a variant the catalogue has not announced (`POST /admin/products/republish-catalogue` on product-svc fills that) — shown rather than dropped. Names are the catalogue's: the report answers in ids and the app reads names from `GET /admin/categories`. `level` outside leaf/top is `400 REPORT_LEVEL_INVALID`.

**Business rules**
- Entirely read-only: every endpoint serves from projections built by consuming events, never a live call into order/payment/inventory-svc.
- Sales figures are computed net of refunds, and leave out every till sale voided after the fact — the summary, the days, the categories (a voided sale's lines) and labour against takings alike.

**Events**
- Consumes: `OrderConfirmed`, `PaymentRefunded`, `OrderVoided` (sales projection), `StockReceived`, `StockDeducted`, `StockAdjusted`, `TransferOrderShipped`, `TransferOrderReceived` (stock movement projection).
- Publishes: none.

---

## tenant-svc

### The roster and the time clock (`/workforce/clock` staff · `/admin/workforce` management)

A shop's biggest controllable cost is its hours, and the platform kept no record of them: staff were assigned to stores with roles, and nothing said who was meant to be in on Tuesday or who actually was. **This is not a till session** — iam-svc's POS session is about the drawer, and a cashier may open three tills in one shift while a storekeeper opens none, so paying from till sessions would pay a cashier for the gaps and a storekeeper nothing.

**Clocking is the person's own act**, so those routes sit outside `/admin/`, which is gated to management platform-wide; a clock only a manager could press would be a clock nobody used. The user id comes from the token and is never taken from the request.

- `POST /workforce/clock/in` `{storeId, shiftId?}` · `POST /workforce/clock/out` · `GET /workforce/clock/open` · `POST /workforce/clock/breaks/start` `{kind, paid}` · `POST /workforce/clock/breaks/end` · `GET /workforce/clock/shifts` — the caller's own hours and their own roster. **One open entry per person** and **one open break per entry** are partial unique indexes, so two taps on a slow terminal are one entry rather than two afternoons' pay (`409 WORKFORCE_ALREADY_CLOCKED_IN`, `WORKFORCE_BREAK_OPEN`). **Clocking out closes a break somebody forgot to end**, at the same instant: nobody is kept at a terminal arguing with a validation message at the end of a shift. Whether a break is paid is the employer's arrangement, kept rather than decided — unpaid breaks come off the hours and paid ones do not. An **open entry has no hours yet and says so**, because a zero looks like a day nobody worked. Refused for somebody not assigned to that store (`409 WORKFORCE_NOT_ASSIGNED`) or a shift rostered for somebody else (`400 WORKFORCE_SHIFT_NOT_THEIRS`).
- `POST /admin/workforce/shifts` `{storeId, userId, startsAt, endsAt, duty?, note?}` · `GET /admin/workforce/shifts?store=&user=&from=&to=` · `POST …/{id}/publish` · `POST …/{id}/cancel` `{reason}` — the rota, planned first and published when it is settled so a half-written week is not something staff can rely on. The read carries **concerns about each person's own shifts**: eleven hours' daily rest, a break expected after six, and shifts that overlap — **flagged, never refused**, because the Working Time Directive is implemented member state by member state with its own derogations and an employer who has one is entitled to roster against it; what the platform must not do is stay quiet, since nobody spots eleven hours by eye. Concerns are per person, not per store: two people's shifts back to back is a shop staying open, not somebody going without sleep. Calling a shift off needs a reason and keeps it.
- `GET /admin/workforce/time-entries?store=&user=&from=&to=` · `POST /admin/workforce/time-entries?user=` · `POST /admin/workforce/time-entries/{id}/adjust` `{clockedInAt?, clockedOutAt?, reason}` — the hours, and the two management acts on them. A **correction is a new entry that supersedes** the one it replaces, with the reason, and both stay: hours that can be quietly rewritten are hours nobody can be held to. Its breaks come with it, or the correction would pay for the lunch hour. The read returns only the entries that stand, because counting a corrected entry and its correction would double a day. A manager clocking somebody in by hand is recorded as `MANAGER` rather than `CLOCK`, so an audit of hours can tell who pressed what.
- `POST /admin/workforce/pay-rates` `{userId, effectiveFrom?, hourlyRate, currency?, note?}` · `GET /admin/workforce/pay-rates?user=` — **what an hour of somebody's time costs**, dated and append-only: a rise must not re-cost the past, or last quarter's labour figure would disagree with itself the day somebody got a pay rise. This is a rate and nothing else — no salary, no deductions, no payroll — because the question a shop asks is what a Saturday cost against what it took. Zero is meaningful (an unpaid trial, a proprietor drawing no wage); less than zero is not. `409 WORKFORCE_RATE_EXISTS` for two rates starting the same day, since that is an undecidable cost.
- `GET /admin/workforce/attendance?store=&user=&from=&to=` — planned against worked, day by day and person by person, built from the same objects payroll would read so the two cannot disagree about a day. Rostered with **nothing clocked at all** is an absence; clocked with nothing rostered is as much a management fact as an absence; somebody still on the clock is neither. Turning up is counted in **entries, not minutes** — somebody who clocked in and straight back out worked nothing but did turn up, and calling that an absence would put a disciplinary question where a mis-tap is. `lateByMinutes` compares the first clock-in with the rostered start, negative for early.

### What a sale earns the person who made it (`/admin/workforce/commission` · `/admin/commission`)

**Commission and sales attribution.** The platform already knew who *rang a sale up* — `pos_log_entries.cashier_id`, the POS journal — and that is a different question: on a counter an assistant sells and a supervisor takes the money, and a shop that pays commission pays the seller. Two services are involved on purpose. The **arrangement** is a term of employment, so it sits in tenant-svc beside the pay rates; the **sales** are order-svc's. Figures go over and money comes back, so no order or return ever leaves the service that owns it and the commission rule exists in exactly one place — a second implementation would eventually disagree with the one the business agreed to, and somebody would be paid on the wrong one.

- `POST`/`GET /admin/workforce/commission/schemes` · `GET`/`DELETE …/schemes/{id}` · `POST …/schemes/{id}/corrections` — the arrangement: a basis (`PERCENT_OF_NET`, or `PER_UNIT` with the currency its amount is in) and its **marginal rate bands**, only the part of a period's sales inside a band earning that band's rate. Marginal because re-rating a whole period once a threshold is crossed makes a figure that changes after the fact, and nobody can be paid on one of those; the threshold belongs to the band above it, so crossing it never re-rates what came before. A scheme is **superseded, never edited** (`POST …/corrections` writes a new version and moves whoever is on the old one across from `effectiveFrom`, leaving somebody who already has an arrangement written for that very day alone), so commission earned at 2% cannot become 3% because somebody corrected the scheme in April. `400 COMMISSION_SCHEME_INVALID` for an unknown basis, a per-unit scheme with no currency, a percentage with one, no bands, a first band above zero (which would leave the first sales of every period earning nothing, silently) or two bands at one figure; `409 COMMISSION_SCHEME_SUPERSEDED`, `COMMISSION_SCHEME_IN_USE` (withdrawal is refused while anybody is still on it — a shop that believed commission had stopped and found it had not is why).
- `PUT`/`GET /admin/workforce/commission/staff/{userId}` — who is on which arrangement, **dated exactly as a pay rate is**: the one in force for a sale is the latest effective on or before the day it was sold, so moving somebody in April does not re-earn January. A request with no `schemeId` **ends** the arrangement from that day, because an arrangement that stopped is not the absence of one. `409 COMMISSION_NOT_STAFF`, `COMMISSION_SCHEME_NOT_CURRENT`, `COMMISSION_ARRANGEMENT_EXISTS` (the unique index decides, and the answer names the day).
- `POST /admin/workforce/commission/rate` `{from, to, sellers:[{userId, days:[{day, net, units}]}]}` — **what these sales earn**, for the service that holds the sales. Days are cut into segments wherever the arrangement changes, because a new arrangement starts its own band progression: somebody moved onto a tiered scheme mid-month has not already sold their way up its first band. A person on no arrangement comes back with their sales and no commission rather than being left out, since sales that earned nothing are what a manager needs to see. `400 COMMISSION_PERIOD_INVALID`, `COMMISSION_PERIOD_TOO_LARGE`.
- `POST`/`GET /admin/commission/statements` · `GET`/`DELETE …/{id}` · `POST …/{id}/approval` (order-svc) — the statement. A sale counts on the day it was **supplied** (its first move to FULFILLED in the append-only history) and a refund subtracts on the day it was **refunded** — the same definitions the tax reporting uses, so two reports over the same trade cannot disagree about which month it happened in; a refund therefore reduces the period it was refunded in, never the one already stated and paid. The period must have **finished** (`400 COMMISSION_PERIOD_INVALID`): one produced mid-month would be approved, paid, and then contradicted. Approval **freezes** it, and only one statement stands per store-or-business, period and currency (a partial unique index; `409 COMMISSION_STATEMENT_STANDS`), so a period that has to change is restated by a new statement naming `supersedes` — and the old one stands until the restatement is approved, so a month is never left with nothing. A draft is a working document and can be discarded; an approved statement is a record and cannot (`409 COMMISSION_STATEMENT_NOT_DRAFT`). One line per person per stretch under one arrangement per rate band, with the sales that earned nothing carried as lines with no scheme, so a statement's sales add up to the period's takings. When the arrangements cannot be read the statement is **refused** (`503 COMMISSION_RATES_UNAVAILABLE`) rather than produced as zeros: a zero somebody signs off is worse than an error somebody retries.
- `PUT`/`GET /admin/commission/sales/{orderId}/seller` (order-svc) — who a sale is credited to, with a **required reason**, kept append-only because commission follows attribution. `POST /orders` takes `sellerUserId` on the **POS channel only** (`400 ORDER_SELLER_POS_ONLY`), defaulting to whoever is operating the till; an online sale is credited to nobody, since a website does the selling. An approved statement does not move when attribution changes — it is frozen — so a period paid on the old attribution has to be restated for the change to reach anybody's pay, which is a decision somebody takes deliberately.

### The work a shop does every day (`/workforce/tasks` staff · `/admin/workforce/tasks` management)

**Store task lists and opening/closing checklists.** The roster says who is in and the clock says they turned up; neither says what they were meant to *do*. This is the list a shop runs on — open up, count the float, check the bins, lock the back door — and the record that it was done. **One mechanism, not two:** a list may carry lines; without them it is a single task, with them it *is* a checklist, finished when every required line is ticked. It is not the food-safety check (inventory-svc's `fs_*`, statutory HACCP records at monitoring points): a shop deciding to sweep the yard on Fridays is not making a due-diligence record.

- `POST`/`GET /admin/workforce/tasks/lists` · `GET`/`DELETE …/lists/{id}` — the list: a kind (`OPENING`, `CLOSING`, `DAILY`, `WEEKLY`, `AD_HOC`), the days it falls due (ISO numbers; empty is every day), a `dueTime` read **on the store's own clock** (08:00 in Mumbai is 02:30Z; a list read in UTC falls due before the staff arrive or after they leave), `graceMinutes` before it counts as missed (a closing check at 22:00 is not missed at 22:01), a `role`, whether it is `required`, and optional `lines`. `storeId` narrows it to one shop; omitted, it is every store's. Withdrawing a list generates no more days for it and leaves the days already generated alone. `400 TASK_LIST_INVALID` (an unknown kind, a day outside 1–7 — refused rather than ignored, because a list quietly scheduled for nothing reads as scheduled and never falls due — a weekly list with no day, grace outside 0–1440), `TASK_TIME_INVALID`, `TASK_LINE_BLANK`; `409 TASK_LIST_WITHDRAWN`.
- `POST /admin/workforce/tasks/days` `{storeId, businessDate?}` · `POST …/raise` `{listId, storeId, businessDate?}` — a store's day, and a job put on it by hand. **A day is generated before it is worked**, because a task nobody did has to exist in order to be missed: a list that appeared only when somebody opened it could never report the morning nobody opened it. The sweeper does this hourly for every store on its own clock (`storeql.tasks.sweeper.*`), and a manager who has just written the opening list need not wait for it. Idempotent under a unique constraint per list, store and business date, so a day is never generated twice however many ask; each occurrence carries the list's title and lines **copied**, so editing the list tomorrow does not rewrite what was done today. `409 TASK_ALREADY_RAISED`, `TASK_LIST_OTHER_STORE`.
- `GET /admin/workforce/tasks/days?storeId=&from=&to=` · `GET …/summary?…` · `GET …/{id}` — the work and what each day came to: done, **late** (done after it fell due, which is not missed), skipped, missed, open, and whether the day is `settled` (nothing required still open or missed; explained counts as settled). At most 62 days (`400 TASK_RANGE_INVALID`).
- `GET /workforce/tasks?storeId=&date?` · `GET /workforce/tasks/{id}` · `POST …/{id}/lines/{position}/tick` · `POST …/{id}/complete` `{note?}` · `POST …/{id}/skip` `{reason}` — the staff half, **outside `/admin/`** as the clock is, because a checklist only a manager could tick is a checklist nobody keeps. Who ticked what comes from the token, and every act is refused for somebody not assigned to that store (`409 WORKFORCE_NOT_ASSIGNED`). A checklist is refused until every required line is ticked (`409 TASK_LINES_OUTSTANDING`) — a closing list signed off with the safe still open is what the required flag exists for; a line ticked twice is `409 TASK_LINE_TICKED`; skipping needs a reason (`400 TASK_REASON_REQUIRED`), since a skipped closing check with none is what an auditor asks about; a settled task is `409 TASK_NOT_OPEN`.
- The sweep marks an open occurrence **missed** once its grace has passed and announces each one on the outbox (`StoreTaskMissed`, `storeql.tenant.store-task-missed`) in the same transaction — a mark without the announcement would be the manager's alert lost, an announcement without the mark would nag every hour. notification-svc tells the store's devices (`STORE_TASK_MISSED`), once per event.

### What management tells the shop floor (`/workforce/broadcasts` staff · `/admin/workforce/broadcasts` management)

**Store communications and broadcast.** A shop runs on notices as much as on lists — the price change on Monday, the recall on the counter, the new closing procedure. Until now the only way to tell every store was a message outside the platform, and the only way to know who had read it was to ask. A notice is **never edited**: what staff acknowledged is the text they saw, and a notice that changed under its acknowledgements would make every one of them meaningless. A correction withdraws and publishes again, and both stay.

- `POST /admin/workforce/broadcasts` `{title, body, priority, storeId?, role?, requiresAck?, expiresAt?}` · `GET …?all=&limit=` · `GET …/{id}` · `POST …/{id}/withdrawal` `{reason}` — publish to one store or every open store, to everybody there or one role (matched against the assignment's role **or its tier**, so a custom role is reached by a notice to its tier); `INFO` is read when convenient, `IMPORTANT` today, `URGENT` now. Announced to each store's devices **in the same transaction it is published in**, one event per store reached, so notification-svc never has to know which stores a business has — and **only an urgent notice wakes the devices**, because a device that buzzes for every price change is muted before the recall comes. `expiresAt` is when it stops being current (a notice about Thursday's drill has nothing to say on Friday); omitted, it is pinned until withdrawn. Withdrawing needs a reason and leaves the acknowledgements standing. `400 BROADCAST_INVALID` (no title, nothing to say, over 4,000 characters — a longer thing is a document — an unknown priority, an expiry before publication), `BROADCAST_EXPIRY_INVALID`, `BROADCAST_REASON_REQUIRED`; `409 BROADCAST_WITHDRAWN`.
- `GET /admin/workforce/broadcasts/{id}/reach` — how far it reached, **per store**: how many it is addressed to, how many acknowledged, and who has not — **named**, because "three of nine" is a number and a manager needs a name. `complete` when nobody is outstanding.
- `GET /workforce/broadcasts?storeId=` · `POST /workforce/broadcasts/{id}/acknowledgement` `{storeId}` — the staff half, **outside `/admin/`** as the clock and the task list are, because a notice only management could read reaches nobody. The notices current for the caller at that store, newest first, each with when they acknowledged it; who acknowledged what comes from the token. One acknowledgement per person per notice is a unique constraint (`409 BROADCAST_ALREADY_ACKNOWLEDGED` — a second tap is not a second reading); `409 BROADCAST_NOT_CURRENT` for one withdrawn or expired, `BROADCAST_NOT_ADDRESSED` for one not meant for this person here, `WORKFORCE_NOT_ASSIGNED` for somebody not on the store's staff.
- notification-svc consumes `StoreBroadcastPublished` (`storeql.tenant.store-broadcast-published`) and, when the event says `wake`, tells the store's devices once (`STORE_BROADCAST`).

### Onboarding (`/onboarding`)
- `POST /onboarding` — single-call signup: create a tenant and its first store atomically. `storeTimezone` is required (an IANA zone such as `Europe/London`) and is checked before the tenant is created, so a refused zone leaves no business without a store.
- `GET /admin/tenant/obligations?country=&on=` — **which laws bind this business**: the legal obligations that reach a country, directly or through a regime it belongs to (the EU), each with a stable `code` services check (such as `PRICE_REDUCTION_PRIOR_PRICE`), its `scope` (`EU` or the country), `citation`, `summary`, `effectiveFrom`, `effectiveTo` and `status` — `IN_FORCE` on the day asked about, then `UPCOMING`; anything that had ended is left out. A regime's obligation is narrowed to the country's membership, so a British business is not handed EU law made after 31 Jan 2020, and `?on=2019-06-01` shows the GDPR window ending that day. `country` defaults to the business's own (`409 TENANT_COUNTRY_MISSING` if it never declared one), `on` to today; `400 COUNTRY_INVALID` / `400 OBLIGATION_DATE_INVALID` otherwise. Reference data in tenant-svc's V9 — regimes, EU membership with its dates, and the obligations with their citations (V9; V12 the EU's unit pricing; V13 Germany's take-up of art.6a's options, `PRICE_REDUCTION_PROGRESSIVE` and `PRICE_REDUCTION_PERISHABLE_EXEMPT`) — read by any staff role (the till obeys them) and never written through the API: the law changes by migration. Services ask it through `common-service`'s `Jurisdictions` rather than keeping a list of countries. **Cash limits (09.17):** the sheet also carries `cashLimits` — every cash payment limit that reaches the country, its own and its regimes', as `{scope, currency, fromAmount, effectiveFrom, effectiveTo, citation, summary, status}`: the amount at and above which cash is refused, in the currency the law names (the Union's EUR 10,000 from 10 July 2027 under Regulation (EU) 2024/1624 art.80; France's EUR 1,000, Belgium's and Portugal's 3,000, Italy's 5,000, Spain's 1,000, Greece's 500; India's two lakh rupees under s.269ST), `IN_FORCE` or `UPCOMING` on the day asked about. Reference data (`cash_limits`), never a literal in code. A country whose currency is not the law's (a Polish business under the EU cap) meets no limit until the register carries a row in its currency: the equivalent is a matter of rate, not of law.
- `GET /admin/tenant/statutory-returns?asOf=` — **what this business owes each authority, and when (07.14)**. The obligations sheet above says what the law *asks*; this says what this business *owes and when*, period by period, and holds the evidence that it went. Returns `{asOf, obligations, outstanding}`: each obligation is one period of one return — `returnCode`, `name`, `scopeKind`/`scope`, `frequency`, `periodStart`, `periodEnd` (exclusive), `dueOn`, `state`, `citation`, `exportService`/`exportPath` and the `filing` that stands. `state` is `NOT_DUE` (the period is still running), `DUE` (ended, unfiled, on or before its date), `OVERDUE` (past its date) or `FILED`; **`outstanding`** is the same list filtered to `DUE`/`OVERDUE`, oldest first. **Nothing is stored** — the date and the state are derived from the return's frequency and its statutory offset on every read, because a deadline in a column goes stale the first time a rule changes. The calendar reaches 24 periods back. Which returns reach a business is asked of `jurisdiction_members` **of the period**, not of today: a Portuguese business owes the EU's recapitulative statement, a British one owes it for no period after 31 January 2020, and a leaving member still owes the quarters it was a member for. Reference data in tenant-svc's V25 (`statutory_returns`) with the instrument named: Portugal's monthly SAF-T (PT) by the 5th of the following month (`P4D`), the UK VAT return under MTD by the 7th of the second month after the quarter (`P1M6D`, which is what HMRC publishes — a fixed day count cannot produce the 7th for all four quarters), the EU statement by the 20th (`P19D`), and Germany's DSFinV-K as a **monthly checkpoint and not a filing**, because KassenSichV sets no periodic date and an inspector can arrive any morning. `exportService`/`exportPath` are a **link, never a proxy**: the SAF-T and DSFinV-K are order-svc's (`/admin/fiscal-receipts/export?format=…`) and the VAT return is pricing-svc's (`/admin/vat-return`), and serving their bytes from tenant-svc would be one service reading another's data. A return the platform cannot produce carries neither, which the console shows as a gap to close with an accountant rather than an empty export. Management-only (`OWNER`/`MANAGER`): a statutory filing is a statement to a tax authority, and the person who makes it is the person answerable for it. `400 STATUTORY_DATE_INVALID` for an `asOf` that is not a date.
- `GET /admin/tenant/statutory-returns/filings` — everything this business has filed, newest period first, **superseded rows included**: a correction replaces a filing and both stay on the record, for the same reason an invoice is never edited. Each carries `stands` (false once a correction replaced it) and `supersedes`.
- `POST /admin/tenant/statutory-returns/{code}/filings` `{periodStart, provider, reference?, payloadDigest?, supersedes?, note?}` — **records that a return went.** The platform does not file on a business's behalf: nothing here is authorised to speak to a tax authority unprompted, so this records what a person did — the authority's `reference` where it gives one, and `payloadDigest` (SHA-256, base64) so the filing can be proved against an export produced later. `provider` is `HMRC_MTD`, `MANUAL` or `SIMULATED` (`SIMULATED` says on the record that nothing left the building). The period is **named, not inferred**, so filing for the wrong month is a mistake somebody makes on purpose. Refusals: `404 STATUTORY_RETURN_UNKNOWN` (no such return in force for a period beginning then), `400 STATUTORY_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN`, `400 STATUTORY_PERIOD_NOT_A_PERIOD` (a date that does not begin one of that return's own periods — a filing against an invented period would sit on the calendar for ever looking unfiled), `409 STATUTORY_PERIOD_NOT_ENDED` (the figures do not exist yet, so a filing recorded against them would be evidence of something that had not happened), `409 STATUTORY_FILING_EXISTS` (already filed; a correction must name what it replaces) and `409 STATUTORY_FILING_NOT_STANDING` (`supersedes` is not the filing that currently stands — which is also what stops one business naming another's filing id, because the lookup is under the caller's own tenant). A correction marks its predecessor and inserts itself in one transaction; `uq_statutory_filing_period` (partial, `WHERE superseded_by IS NULL`) is what makes "exactly one filing stands per period" a database fact. Returns the period, re-derived, with its new `state` and the filing that now stands.
- **Deposit return schemes (09.16):** the same sheet carries `depositSchemes[]` — for each scheme that reaches the country: `scope`, `currency`, `depositEach`, `materials`, `minVolumeMl`/`maxVolumeMl`, `vatTreatment` (`OUTSIDE_SCOPE` or `STANDARD`), `effectiveFrom`/`effectiveTo`, `citation`, `summary`, `status`. Seeded: Germany's Pfand (EUR 0.25, PET, aluminium, steel and glass 100 ml–3 l, since 2003, taxed as the drink, VerpackG §31) and the UK scheme (GBP 0.20, PET, aluminium and steel 150 ml–3 l, from 1 October 2027, outside the scope of VAT, SI 2025/67 and VATA 1994 ss.55B–55D). `Jurisdictions.depositScheme(tenant, store, currency, day)` in common-service is how order-svc asks for the scheme in force where a store trades.

**Data retention (21.16)** — UK GDPR art.5(1)(e) storage limitation and art.30's record of the periods applied, with the statutory floors that stop a period being set too short.
- `GET /admin/tenant/retention` — the schedule: `country`, `countries` (the business's own and every store's), and every class of data — `TRANSACTIONS` (orders, receipts, returns, payments: `KEEP`, never purged by the platform), `ORDER_PERSONAL_DATA` (the delivery name, address, phone and notes on a settled order: `ANONYMISE`, by order-svc), `CUSTOMER_RECORDS` (a record nothing has happened on: `ANONYMISE`, by customer-svc, through the ordinary erasure), `NOTIFICATION_LOG` (messages sent: `DELETE`, by notification-svc) — each with `floorDays`, `floorScope`, `floorCitation` and `floorSummary`, the highest floor among the countries traded in (V14: GB six years, VATA 1994 Sch.11 para.6(3); DE eight, AO §147(3); FR ten, C.com L123-22; IN eight, Companies Act 2013 s.128(5)), and the `periodDays` the business set, absent until it decides; plus the `holds` in force. Readable by every staff role, because the purgers read it under a staff identity.
- `PUT /admin/tenant/retention/{dataClass}` — `{ periodDays }` (0–36500; 0 means as soon as settled). A longer period than the floor is allowed; a shorter one is `400 RETENTION_BELOW_LEGAL_MINIMUM` with the floor and its instrument in `error.details`. `400 RETENTION_CLASS_UNKNOWN` / `RETENTION_PERIOD_INVALID`. Every decision is kept (`retention_schedules`, append-only); the latest is in force. Management only.
- `GET /admin/tenant/retention/holds?active=` and `POST /admin/tenant/retention/holds` — `{ dataClass?, subjectKind: ALL|CUSTOMER|ORDER, subjectId?, reason }`: a matter that stops a purge of one customer, one order or everything, on one class or every class (`400 RETENTION_HOLD_SUBJECT` when the subject and kind disagree). `POST /admin/tenant/retention/holds/{id}/release` — `{ reason }`, once (`409 RETENTION_HOLD_RELEASED`, `404 RETENTION_HOLD_NOT_FOUND`). Management only.
- `GET /admin/tenant/retention/runs?after=&limit=` — the register: every purge a service ran for this business, newest first, with `service`, `dataClass`, `cutoff`, `rowsAffected` and `heldSkipped`. Recorded from `RetentionRunCompleted` events (tenant-svc's first consumer; topics `storeql.{order,customer,notification}.retention-run-completed`), once per event id. Management only.
- The purgers share `common-service`'s `Retention` (the schedule cached ten minutes; a purge whose schedule cannot be read does not run, `503 RETENTION_UNAVAILABLE`) and `RetentionSweeperBase` (daily, `storeql.retention-sweeper.enabled` / `interval-seconds`, first run one interval after start). Each also exposes `POST …/retention/sweep` to run now for the caller's business (management only; `409 RETENTION_PERIOD_NOT_SET` when no period is set), returning `{ dataClass, cutoff, rowsAffected, heldSkipped, startedAt, finishedAt }`: `POST /admin/orders/retention/sweep` (order-svc — settled orders older than the cutoff by `updated_at`; also the address a receipt was emailed to and the number a recall notice held), `POST /admin/customers/retention/sweep` (customer-svc — no profile change, loyalty movement or consent change since the cutoff; each record erased as the customer could have asked, `CustomerErased` and all), `POST /admin/notifications/retention/sweep` (notification-svc — log rows older than the cutoff; the service gained an outbox, V5, for the announcement). The rows, the held count and the announcement commit in one transaction; concurrent sweeps lock the same rows and never count one twice.
- `GET /admin/tenant/security-notices` · `POST /admin/tenant/security-notices/{id}/acknowledge` — **security notices** the platform has sent this business about an incident that affects it (21.15), newest first, with `acknowledged` and `acknowledgedAt`. Management only (the default tier for `/admin`): the owner or a manager answers for the business as controller. Acknowledging again returns the first acknowledgement; another business's notice is `404 SECURITY_NOTICE_NOT_FOUND`. **The business's own duties on a breach (13.12):** a notice of a `PERSONAL_DATA_BREACH` carries `regime` — `DPDP` where the register puts the business's country under India's Act, `binding` or not yet with `bindsFrom`; `GDPR` elsewhere — and `duties`, reference data with their citations (`breach_duties`): under the DPDP Rules r.7, `PRINCIPALS_TOLD` and `BOARD_INTIMATED` without delay and `BOARD_REPORTED` within 72 hours of the notice; under GDPR arts.33–34, `AUTHORITY_NOTIFIED` within 72 hours and `SUBJECTS_TOLD` without undue delay; each `DUE`, `OVERDUE`, `WAITING` (no fixed time) or `DONE`. `POST /admin/tenant/security-notices/{id}/reports` `{duty, doneAt, reference, note}` → `201` records a duty done, once (`409 SECURITY_NOTICE_DUTY_DONE`; `400 SECURITY_NOTICE_DUTY_UNKNOWN` for a duty the regime does not put on the business, `SECURITY_NOTICE_NO_DUTIES` for a notice of anything but a breach; owners and managers). Telling the customers is done through customer-svc's breach intimation, whose id is the reference.
- `POST /onboarding/tenants` — create just the tenant (binds it to the caller as owner). Takes an optional `planId` from the public price list (21.13): the business starts on that plan and, when the plan has `trialDays`, on its trial (`TRIALING`, `trialEnd` set, nothing invoiced until then); without one it starts on the platform's default plan. A plan not on sale is `409 PLAN_NOT_SOLD`, one sold by hand `409 PLAN_NOT_PUBLIC`, both refused before any business exists. A login owns one business: a second signup on the same login is `409 TENANT_ALREADY_OWNED`, a second trial as much as a second shop.
- `POST /onboarding/stores` — create the tenant's (first/default) store. `timezone` is required, as below.
- `GET /onboarding/status` — onboarding checklist/completion status.

### Tenant, Store, Zone & Staff Administration (`/admin`)
- `GET/PUT /admin/tenant` — get/update the tenant's own profile. The profile carries `mode` (`LIVE`, or `SANDBOX` for a business's test double) and `sandboxOf` (the live business a sandbox stands in for).
- **The sandbox (22.8)** — `GET /admin/tenant/sandbox` (OWNER, MANAGER): the business's active sandbox, or from inside a sandbox the sandbox itself (`404 SANDBOX_NOT_FOUND`); `POST /admin/tenant/sandbox` (OWNER, from the live business) → `201` a tenant of its own — `mode: SANDBOX`, `sandboxOf` the live business, the same owner, country and currency, the name with ` (sandbox)`, on the `SANDBOX` plan, the live default store copied in, no subscription; `409 SANDBOX_EXISTS` (one at a time), `409 SANDBOX_NESTED` (from inside), `503 SANDBOX_PLAN_MISSING`; `DELETE /admin/tenant/sandbox` (OWNER) → the sandbox switched off with `deactivatedReason: SANDBOX_DELETED`, its stores suspended, `TenantStatusChanged` and `TenantDataErasureDue` published so its keys stop within seconds (the gateway asks iam-svc), a token already issued for it lasts out its fifteen minutes and is neither renewed nor issued again, and every service erases what it held; another can be made at once. Every service reads `mode` from the profile: no message leaves a sandbox (logged `SUPPRESSED`), no money moves (the MANUAL provider), nothing is billed. Details: [API-VERSIONING.md](API-VERSIONING.md).
- `POST/GET /admin/stores`, `GET/PUT /admin/stores/{storeId}`, `PATCH /admin/stores/{storeId}/status` — create/list/get/update/activate-suspend a store. `tillPhone` says whether the store's till asks for the customer's phone ([a phone at the till](../intent/phone-at-the-till.md)): `REQUIRED`, `OPTIONAL` (the default, existing stores included) or `OFF`, read case-insensitively, anything else `400 STORE_TILL_PHONE_INVALID`, left out of an update kept; owners and managers only, like the rest of a store. `type` is `STORE`, `WAREHOUSE` or `DARK_STORE` (`400 TENANT_STORE_TYPE_INVALID` otherwise): a dark store holds stock, is replenished, delivers and is routed to like any shop, sells delivery-only and has no till. `StoreStatusChanged` carries the `type` beside the status, so iam-svc knows where no till opens. **A store's `timezone` is required on create and never defaulted (SJ-D54)** — `400 STORE_TIMEZONE_REQUIRED` when absent, `400 STORE_TIMEZONE_INVALID` for anything that is not an IANA zone (`UTC+01:00` is an offset, not a zone). No country has one right answer, and the platform used to fill a missing zone with `UTC` in the column and in the service, which put a London store's tills on the wrong clock for half the year. An update that leaves `timezone` out keeps the store's zone; it used to reset it to `UTC`.
- `POST/GET /admin/stores/{storeId}/zones`, `GET/PUT /admin/stores/{storeId}/zones/{zoneId}`, `PATCH .../status` — create/list/get/update/activate-suspend a zone.
- `POST /admin/staff` `{userId, storeId, role}` — assign a staff member to a store with a role: a built-in one (`OWNER`, `MANAGER`, `STOREKEEPER`, `CASHIER`) or **the code of one of the tenant's own roles** (20.10; `400 STAFF_ROLE_UNKNOWN` for anything else). `StaffAssigned` carries the tier iam-svc binds and, for a custom role, `roleCode` and the `permissions` it holds now. Needs `staff.manage`.
- `GET /admin/staff` — list staff assignments; each carries `role` as assigned and `baseTier`. A caller held to stores sees the assignments at those stores and the business-wide ones only; the owner and a business-wide manager see all.
- `DELETE /admin/staff/{userId}?store=` — remove a staff member's role at a specific store (role assignment is per-store, not tenant-wide). Publishes `StaffRemoved` per tier taken away, and iam-svc unbinds it (SJ-D51). Needs `staff.manage`.
- `GET /admin/roles` — **the tenant's roles (20.10)**: the four built-in tiers first, each holding its default permissions (`custom: false`), then the tenant's own by code.
- `GET /admin/roles/permissions` — the catalogue: every permission with the sentence a screen shows beside it and the tiers that hold it by default.
- `POST /admin/roles` `{code, name, baseTier, permissions[], description?}` — **define a role.** `code` is upper snake case, 2–32 characters (`400 ROLE_CODE_INVALID`) and not a built-in role's name (`400 ROLE_CODE_RESERVED`); `baseTier` is `MANAGER`, `STOREKEEPER` or `CASHIER` (`400 ROLE_TIER_INVALID`); every permission must exist (`400 ROLE_PERMISSION_UNKNOWN`) **and be held by the tier** (`400 ROLE_PERMISSION_OUTSIDE_TIER` — a cashier who can void sales is a manager, not a cashier); the same code twice is `409 ROLE_ALREADY_EXISTS`, decided by the unique index so twenty at once produce one. Publishes `RoleDefined`. Needs `staff.manage`.
- `GET /admin/roles/{code}`, `PUT /admin/roles/{code}` `{name, permissions[], description?}` — read, and rename or change what it holds (the tier cannot change: an assignment's tier is bound on the login). Publishes `RoleDefined`; holders carry the new set from their next sign-in.
- `DELETE /admin/roles/{code}` — delete a role nobody holds; `409 ROLE_IN_USE` while an assignment still names it, decided under the role's row lock.

### Inventory Configuration (`/admin/inventory-config`)
- `PUT /admin/inventory-config`, `GET /admin/inventory-config` — upsert/get tenant-wide inventory control parameters (lot, serial, grade, costing, UOM policy).

### Platform Administration (`/platform`, `PLATFORM_ADMIN` only)
- `GET /platform/tenants` — list every tenant on the platform, sandboxes included and marked (`mode: SANDBOX`, `sandboxOf`).
- `PATCH /platform/tenants/{tenantId}/status` — activate/suspend a tenant platform-wide.
- `GET /platform/tenants/by-einvoice-address?scheme=&id=` or `?vatNumber=` (`PLATFORM_ADMIN`) — **which business a network's delivery lands with (07.13, the transport seam).** By electronic address when one is named (both halves: `400 TENANT_EINVOICE_ADDRESS_INVALID`), else by VAT identifier (neither: `400 TENANT_RECEIVER_UNNAMED`), case and spaces aside. The one `ACTIVE` business holding it, as `GET /platform/tenants` lists it; `404 TENANT_NOT_FOUND` when none does, a suspended business included; `409 TENANT_EINVOICE_ADDRESS_SHARED` when more than one does — the platform cannot tell which the document is for, so purchase-svc places it nowhere. Asked by purchase-svc as the platform itself before a delivered e-invoice is read into anyone's inbox.

### Plans and packaging (`/platform/plans`, `PLATFORM_ADMIN` only; 21.8)

The `SANDBOX` plan (22.8) is seeded by migration and kept for sandboxes: sold (`ACTIVE`) so it can be held, off the public price list, never the default (`409 PLAN_SANDBOX_ONLY` on `POST /{id}/default`), never given to a live business nor taken from a sandbox (`PUT /platform/tenants/{id}/plan` → `409 PLAN_SANDBOX_ONLY`); its allowances may be changed like any plan's.
The platform's price list. A plan is written as a draft, priced, given its allowances, and only then sold; one plan on sale is the one a business signing up starts on. A plan is never deleted — retiring it takes it off sale and leaves its subscribers where they are.
- `GET /platform/plans` — every plan, drafts and retired ones included, with its prices and what it includes.
- `POST /platform/plans` `{code, name, description?, billingInterval, trialDays?, isPublic?, sortOrder?}` → `201`, always `DRAFT`. `400 PLAN_CODE_INVALID` (2–40 of A–Z, 0–9, `-`, `_`), `PLAN_INTERVAL_UNKNOWN` (`MONTH` or `YEAR`); `409 PLAN_CODE_TAKEN` (case-insensitive).
- `GET /platform/plans/{id}`, `PUT /platform/plans/{id}` — read and change name, description, interval, trial, visibility and order. `409 PLAN_INTERVAL_IN_USE`: how often a plan bills cannot change while businesses are on it.
- `POST /platform/plans/{id}/prices` `{currency, amount, effectiveFrom?}` — the price in one currency from a date (today when absent). An earlier price is kept, never edited. `400 CURRENCY_INVALID`, `PLAN_PRICE_DATE_INVALID`.
- `PUT /platform/plans/{id}/includes` `{grants:[{key, limitValue?, enabled?}]}` — replaced whole: a key left out is one the plan no longer names. A limit carries `limitValue` (absent means unlimited), a feature carries `enabled`. `400 PLAN_ENTITLEMENT_UNKNOWN` for a key the platform does not enforce, `PLAN_ENTITLEMENT_SHAPE` for a limit written as a yes or no, or the reverse.
- `POST /platform/plans/{id}/activate` — put it on sale. `409 PLAN_HAS_NO_PRICE`, `PLAN_ALREADY_SOLD`.
- `POST /platform/plans/{id}/retire` — take it off sale; its subscribers keep it. `409 PLAN_NOT_SOLD`.
- `POST /platform/plans/{id}/default` — the plan a business signing up starts on, at most one. `409 PLAN_NOT_SOLD`.
- `GET /plans` — **public, no identity** (21.13): the plans on sale to the public, for a prospect deciding before signing up — code, name, description, prices, what each includes, `trialDays`, and which is the default. A draft, a retired plan and one sold by hand (`isPublic` false) are not on it; the platform's own list, drafts and all, stays under `/platform`.
- `GET /platform/plans/entitlement-keys` — the keys a plan may carry, each with who enforces it. A console offers these rather than inventing one. Seven today: `stores.max` (tenant-svc), `staff.max` (iam-svc), `products.max` (product-svc), `feature.storefront` (the storefront gate), and since 21.11 `requests.per-minute` (the gateway's `TenantRateLimitFilter`: API requests a minute across every login and the online shop, counted once the request is known to be the business's; over it is `429 PLAN_RATE_LIMIT_REACHED` with `Retry-After` the rest of the minute; the platform's administrator is nobody's tenant and is not counted), `images.mb.max` (product-svc: what the business's product images weigh, measured as they would stand with the upload in, so a replacement is not charged twice; over it is `409 PLAN_LIMIT_REACHED` naming the plan's figure and what it would make, before a byte is stored) and `documents.mb.max` (purchase-svc: the same for supplier e-invoice documents, asked after the duplicate check and before the document is read). A plan that names none of them holds nothing back, and so does an allowance that cannot be read: limits fail open.
- `PUT /platform/plans/{id}/meters` `{meters:[{meter, included?, hard?}]}` (21.10) — what the plan includes of each metered thing **each billing period**, replaced whole: a meter left out is one the plan does not name, which leaves it unlimited and uncharged; `included` absent is unlimited. `hard` refuses use beyond the allowance rather than charging for it, and only a meter that may be refused can be hard. `400 PLAN_METER_UNKNOWN` (the platform counts `ORDERS` and `SMS`), `PLAN_METER_NOT_REFUSABLE` (**an order is never refused** — a till that stops mid-sale loses the sale and the customer; price the overage instead), `PLAN_METER_HARD_UNLIMITED`, `PLAN_METER_INCLUDED_INVALID`, `PLAN_METER_TWICE`.
- `POST /platform/plans/{id}/meter-prices` `{meter, currency, unitAmount, effectiveFrom?}` — what each unit beyond the allowance costs, per currency, from a date, to four places (a text part at `0.0350` is a real price). Never edited in place; **a period is charged at the price in force the day it began**. `GET /platform/plans/meter-keys` — what the platform counts, who counts it, and which of it may be refused. A plan's `meters` and `meterPrices` come back with it.
- `PUT /platform/tenants/{tenantId}/plan` `{planId, reason?}` — put a business on a plan. `409 PLAN_LIMIT_EXCEEDED_NOW`, naming each limit it is already over, because moving somebody onto a plan they do not fit would either take their stores away or leave them quietly past a limit they are now told they have.

### What a business is allowed (`/admin/tenant/plan`; 21.8)
- `GET /admin/tenant/plan` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the plan it is on, with each limit against what it is using. `used` is absent where the owning service holds the count, and is shown as unknown rather than guessed at. A business on no plan carries no `plan` and a `note` saying so; it is unrestricted.
- `GET /admin/tenant/plan/available` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the plans on sale. Moving between them is the platform's to do.
- `GET /admin/tenant/plan/limits` (any staff role) — the allowances alone, no prices: what another service reads through `Entitlements` to enforce a limit it owns. Any staff role, because that is the identity a service-to-service read carries; the leaf is carved out of the management tier in `AdminAuthorizationFilter`, and without that line the read answers 403 and every limit outside tenant-svc silently enforces nothing (SJ-D65).
- Limits are refused with `409 PLAN_LIMIT_REACHED`, naming the ceiling and the count: stores and staff by tenant-svc, products by product-svc. A limit fails **open** — a business on no plan, or one whose allowances cannot be read this moment, is unrestricted — because a blip should not stop a shop taking on staff; that is the opposite of spend authority (11.x), which fails closed because it guards money leaving.

### Exchange rates (`/admin/tenant/fx-rates`; 03.x)
A business keeps one home currency and, from here, a table of rates into it — **home units per one unit of the other currency** (`0.79` for USD under a GBP home: a dollar is seventy-nine pence). Rates are the business's own, dated and append-only; the platform fetches none. Read by pricing-svc (prices shown in another currency) and purchase-svc (a foreign order's spend authority) through common-service's cached `FxRates` reader, under a staff identity.
- `GET /admin/tenant/fx-rates` — the sheet: `home` and, per other currency, the rate in force today (`{currency, rate, effectiveFrom, reason, setBy, setAt}`; the latest effective date that has arrived, the latest set when a day was set twice). **Staff-readable** — the leaf common-web lists for one service's read of another; the rest is management's.
- `PUT /admin/tenant/fx-rates/{currency}` `{rate, effectiveFrom?, reason}` — set a rate from a day (today when absent; at most 31 days ahead, never before 2000) with a reason; a new row every time, never an edit. Management only. `400 FX_CURRENCY_INVALID` (not ISO 4217, or the home currency itself), `400 FX_RATE_INVALID` (absent, not positive, more than ten decimals, implausibly large), `400 FX_DATE_INVALID`, `400 FX_REASON_REQUIRED`.
- `GET /admin/tenant/fx-rates/{currency}/history` — every rate ever set for the currency, newest first. Management only.

### Usage metering and quotas (`/admin/tenant/usage`; 21.10)

A plan's limits count what a business **has**; a meter counts what it **does** each billing period — `ORDERS` (every order placed, online or at the till, counted from order-svc's `OrderPlaced` by its order id, so the till's offline replay of the same sale is the same order) and `SMS` (every text sent, counted from notification-svc's `SmsSent` in the **parts the carrier bills**: 160 GSM-7 characters or 70 UCS-2 in one part, 153 or 67 in each of several). tenant-svc keeps the count beside the plan (`usage_records`, append-only, unique per business, meter and source), so a meter is read against its allowance without calling anybody; one business's meter is counted one record at a time, so a threshold is raised by exactly the record that reaches it.

- `GET /admin/tenant/usage` (OWNER, MANAGER) — this billing period (the subscription's own; the calendar month for a business with none), each meter against what the plan includes, what is `over`, what that comes to so far (`estimate`, nothing in a trial), the thresholds reached (`alerts`: 80%, then 100%, each once per period), and every closed period as it was billed (`history`, with the invoice that carried it, or `notCharged`: `TRIAL`, `NOT_PRICED`).
- `GET /admin/tenant/usage/allowance?meter=&quantity=` (any staff role, the identity a service-to-service read carries; the admin filter admits the leaf) — whether `quantity` more may be done now. **Only a hard ceiling answers no.** `400 USAGE_METER_UNKNOWN`, `USAGE_QUANTITY_INVALID`. notification-svc asks it, through common-service's `Quotas`, before every **marketing** text, and refuses one past the ceiling with `409 USAGE_QUOTA_REACHED` — **a text a customer must be sent (an order ready, a recall notice) is never refused**, only counted. `Quotas` fails **open** like `Entitlements`, and is **not cached**: usage moves with every text.
- **Billed in arrears**, on the renewal that bills the next period in advance, one `USAGE` line per meter over its allowance (`quantity` × `unitAmount`, to four places; the invoice's total is what is rounded). What each period billed is snapshot in `usage_periods` **in the invoice's own transaction**, and a period's usage is everything recorded since the subscription started and before the period ended, less what earlier periods billed — so a record that commits a moment after its period was billed (a sale replayed from a till that was offline) is billed with the next one: late, never lost, never twice. A subscription that ends at its period end has its last period billed as an `ADJUSTMENT`; with nothing over, the period is written down with no invoice. A trial is free whatever it used.
- `GET /platform/billing/tenants/{tenantId}/usage` · `GET /platform/billing/usage-alerts?limit=` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — one business's reading, and every threshold reached across businesses, newest first: who is about to outgrow a plan, before the invoice says so.

### Subscription billing and invoicing (21.9)

Billed **in advance, on the subscription's own anniversary** — not a calendar batch, which would make
every first period a stub and every proration a special case. The price is **locked when the business
is sold the plan**: a change to the price list reaches new subscribers only.

- `GET /platform/billing/profile` · `PUT /platform/billing/profile` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — who the platform invoices as. **Nothing is billed until this is set:** an invoice with no seller is not an invoice anywhere the platform trades (`409 BILLING_PROFILE_NOT_SET`).
- `GET /platform/billing/vat-rates` · `PUT /platform/billing/vat-rates` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — the rates it charges where it has no choice. A country with no rate **refuses the invoice** (`409 BILLING_RATE_NOT_SET`) rather than guessing: a guessed rate is VAT charged wrongly in somebody else's country.
- `POST /platform/billing/tenants/{tenantId}/vat-check` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — records that a buyer's VAT number has been checked, and by what (`VIES` | `MANUAL` | `SIMULATED`). The reverse charge rests on the number being real, so **who checked it and when** is kept; an audit asks for the date.
- `POST /platform/billing/run?asOf=` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — bills everything whose period has run out, catching up on every missed period in one call (bounded at 24). A business the platform **cannot** bill (no rate for its country, no price in its currency) is **passed over and named in `skipped`**, not allowed to stop everybody else's invoices — while that same business's own upgrade refuses loudly with the same code, because there somebody is waiting on the answer. An unbilled business is money not asked for, so it is named rather than silently dropped. Safe to run twice: a period is invoiced once by a unique index on (subscription, period start), not by a check somebody remembered. `asOf` is a **test facility** gated on `storeql.billing.test-clock.enabled` — on in compose, off in k8s, `403 BILLING_TEST_CLOCK_DISABLED` otherwise.
- `GET /platform/billing/receivables` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — what is still owed, most overdue first. **Every row names its business** (`tenantId`), so the platform console can say whose each invoice is; any other role, of any business, is `403`.
- `GET /platform/billing/invoices/{id}` · `POST .../payments` · `POST .../void` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — one invoice with its lines, payments and both snapshots, and the business it was sent to (`tenantId`); money recorded against it (append-only, settling it when what has been paid reaches the total); and withdrawal. **An invoice is never edited.** A withdrawn one keeps its number — a gap is what an auditor asks about — and one that has taken money is corrected with a credit note, not voided (`409 INVOICE_NOT_VOIDABLE`).
- `GET /admin/tenant/billing` (OWNER, MANAGER) — the subscription this business is on and everything that has happened to it.
- `PUT /admin/tenant/billing/details` (OWNER, MANAGER) — where it is established, which is **not** the same question as where its shops are: it decides the VAT treatment. **Changing the VAT number discards the check against the old one**, or the reverse charge could be obtained by being checked on one number and quietly substituting another. `billingEmail` is where the platform's invoices and late-payment notices go: the owner's sign-up address until the business names another; an update that omits it leaves it standing (SJ-D72: it used to be dropped).
- `GET /admin/tenant/billing/invoices` · `GET .../invoices/{id}` (OWNER, MANAGER) — its own, and another business's answers `404` rather than `403`, because a 403 confirms to a guesser that the id is real. An invoice here has the same shape as the platform's, `tenantId` included — which is only ever the business's own id.
- `POST /admin/tenant/billing/plan` (OWNER, MANAGER) — `when: NOW` bills the days left on the new price against what was billed for them on the old one, as **two lines** (a credit and a charge) because a net figure cannot be checked by the person paying it; `when: PERIOD_END` waits for the period already paid for. A subscription in `PAST_DUE` **prorates nothing** — the change takes effect, the credit does not, or a business that has not paid would be credited for time it never paid for.
- `POST /admin/tenant/billing/cancel` · `/resume` · `/plan/cancel-pending` (OWNER, MANAGER) — ends at the period end, never now: it has paid to the end of the period.

Four tax treatments, and the one that is easy to get wrong is the third: `DOMESTIC` (the platform's own country, its own rate) · `REVERSE_CHARGE` (another EU state with a **checked** number: no VAT, and the invoice carries both numbers and the words required by Directive 2006/112/EC art. 226(11a)) · `DESTINATION` (another EU state with **no checked** number: that country's rate — treating this as a reverse charge issues an invoice with no VAT that should have carried some, and the platform owes the difference) · `OUT_OF_SCOPE` (outside the EU). Membership is asked of the **invoice's date**, not of a list, from `jurisdiction_members` — the United Kingdom was a member until 31 January 2020.

### Dunning and suspend-for-non-payment (21.12)

Chase, then interrupt the service, then give up on the debt — **in that order**, and the order is part of the policy: suspending before the last reminder takes the platform away from a business that has not finished being told it is late.

- `GET /platform/billing/dunning/policy` · `PUT …/policy` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — a platform that has set no policy still chases, on the published defaults (reminders on days **1, 3, 5, 7** after the due date, suspend at **14**, uncollectible at **30**), and says `set: false` rather than inventing somebody who chose them. Stages out of order are refused with `400 DUNNING_POLICY_INVALID`.
- `POST /platform/billing/dunning/run?asOf=` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — **every notice a business missed is sent, once.** A run that has not run for a week owes each reminder it did not get; skipping to the latest step would suspend a business the platform never finished telling. Safe to run twice by `UNIQUE (invoice_id, step)`. A business it cannot act on is named in `skipped`, not allowed to stop the rest. Same test-clock gate as the billing run. Each reminder, and the suspension, is a notice: a `DunningNoticeIssued` event claimed with the step and a fresh pay link in one transaction (SJ-D68 closed), which notification-svc emails to the subscription's billing address; a business with no billing address is named in `skipped` (`DUNNING_NO_BILLING_EMAIL`) and nothing is claimed, so nothing is taken away from a business nobody could tell.
- `GET /platform/billing/dunning/overdue` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — what is owed, most overdue first, with the stage reached and **what it earns next**, so an operator sees a suspension coming rather than hearing about it from the customer.
- `GET /platform/billing/dunning/invoices/{id}/events` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — what has been done about one invoice, append-only.
- `GET /platform/billing/dunning/invoices/{id}/pay-link` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — mints the link a notice carries. Returned once, because only its hash is kept; asking again replaces it.
- `PUT /platform/billing/invoices/{id}/due-date` (PLATFORM_ADMIN) — a promise to pay. **Outwards only, and only on an open invoice** — a date that could move inwards would shorten the time a business has to pay after the fact. Recorded, so an extension is on the file rather than a due date that quietly moved.
- `POST /billing/pay/{token}` — **unauthenticated, deliberately.** A suspended business cannot sign in, so a pay link that demands a session is a dead end; the same reasoning as the PECR opt-out link. The token is the whole capability: 256 bits, stored only as a hash, naming one invoice, able to do one thing to it. A token for an invoice no longer open answers `404 PAY_LINK_INVALID` — the same answer as a token nobody issued, because telling them apart tells a guesser which half they got right. Reachable only because **both** the gateway's public-path list and `AdminAuthorizationFilter` carve it out by shape; each comment points at the other.

**Paying up lifts only what the platform imposed.** `tenants.deactivated_reason` is `NON_PAYMENT` or `ADMINISTRATOR`, and reactivation-on-payment moves only the first. Before V24 the column did not exist and the two were indistinguishable, so a payment would have quietly overruled a decision somebody took. A CHECK pairs the reason to the status, so a stale reason cannot make the next payment lift a suspension nobody asked it to lift.

### Tenant data export and leaving (21.14)

A business may leave on notice and take its data with it, or have it erased (EU Data Act (EU) 2023/2854 art.25). Its data is exported through every service's `/admin/tenant-data` (see *Platform-wide conventions*); tenant-svc keeps the notice, starts the erasure and records what each service erased. The owner alone acts (anyone else `403`).

- `GET /admin/tenant/switching` — the latest notice with its dates (`noticeEndsOn`, `transitionEndsOn`, `retrievalEndsOn`, `erasureDueOn`, `extendedAt`, `cancelledAt`, `cancelReason`, `erasureStartedAt`), its `stage` (`NOTICE`, `TRANSITION`, `RETRIEVAL`, `ERASURE_DUE`, `ERASING`, `ERASED`, `CANCELLED`), and once erasure has started the `evidence` from each service (`service`, `rowsErased`, `tables`) and the services still `awaiting`. `404 SWITCHING_NO_NOTICE`.
- `POST /admin/tenant/switching` `{intent, noticeEndsOn}` → `201`. `intent` is `SWITCH`, to take the data elsewhere: notice of at most two months (art.25(2)(a)), then a transitional period of 30 calendar days while the service carries on, then at least 30 days to retrieve the data (art.25(2)(g)), and erasure the day after (art.25(2)(h)). `ERASE`, to have it erased: erasure when notice ends, which may be today (art.25(3)). One notice stands at a time. `400 SWITCHING_INTENT_UNKNOWN`, `SWITCHING_NOTICE_INVALID` (before today, not a date), `SWITCHING_NOTICE_TOO_LONG`; `409 SWITCHING_NOTICE_ALREADY_GIVEN`.
- `POST /admin/tenant/switching/extend` `{transitionEndsOn}` — extends a switch's transitional period once (art.25(4)), while it runs, to at most seven months after notice ends; the retrieval period and erasure move with it. `400 SWITCHING_EXTENSION_INVALID`, `SWITCHING_EXTENSION_TOO_LONG`; `409 SWITCHING_ALREADY_EXTENDED`, `SWITCHING_NOT_EXTENDABLE` (an erasure), `SWITCHING_TRANSITION_OVER`, `SWITCHING_CLOSED`.
- `POST /admin/tenant/switching/cancel` `{reason}` — withdraws the notice while it runs. `409 SWITCHING_TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL` once notice has ended, `SWITCHING_CLOSED`.
- `GET /platform/tenants/switching/{tenantId}`, `POST /platform/tenants/switching/sweep` (`PLATFORM_ADMIN`) — a business's notice, and the sweep that starts every erasure due: the business is made `INACTIVE` (`TenantStatusChanged`) and `TenantDataErasureDue` published, in one transaction per business, once however many sweeps run. The sweep also runs hourly (`storeql.switching.sweeper.interval-seconds`). `{started}` is how many it started.
- Each service's `TenantDataErased` is recorded once as evidence; the business's record, its notice and the evidence are kept at erasure as the platform's record that it left. Neither a notice nor the evidence is ever imported into another business.

### Security incidents (`/platform/security-incidents`, `PLATFORM_ADMIN` only)

The platform's register of what the Cyber Resilience Act (Regulation (EU) 2024/2847 art.14, reporting applies from 11 Sep 2026) and GDPR art.33(2) require of it as the manufacturer of the software and the processor of its businesses' customer data. The statutory stages and their clocks are reference data with citations (V11 `incident_reporting_stages`), not constants: an `EXPLOITED_VULNERABILITY` or `SEVERE_INCIDENT` owes an early warning within 24 hours of awareness and a notification within 72; a vulnerability's final report is due 14 days after a corrective or mitigating measure is available, a severe incident's one calendar month after its notification; a `PERSONAL_DATA_BREACH` owes each business affected notice without undue delay, since each is the controller with its own 72 hours. Every read computes each stage's `dueAt` and `state` — `DONE`, `DUE`, `OVERDUE`, `WAITING` (its anchor has not happened) or `NO_DEADLINE` — from the timeline as it stands. The register does not file with ENISA or a supervisory authority: the reports are made on their own platforms, and recorded here with their references.

- `POST /platform/security-incidents` `{kind, title, summary, awareAt, tenantIds?}` → `201` with its stages. `tenantIds` names the businesses affected (at most 500); none means every business. `400 INCIDENT_KIND_UNKNOWN`, `INCIDENT_TITLE_INVALID` (1–200), `INCIDENT_SUMMARY_INVALID` (1–4000), `INCIDENT_AWARE_IN_FUTURE`, `INCIDENT_TOO_MANY_TENANTS`, `INCIDENT_TENANT_UNKNOWN`, `INVALID_DATE`, `INVALID_UUID`.
- `GET /platform/security-incidents?status=OPEN|CLOSED&limit=` — newest first, each with `nextStage`, `nextDueAt` and `overdue`; `400 INCIDENT_STATUS_UNKNOWN`.
- `GET /platform/security-incidents/{id}` — the incident with its `stages`, append-only `events`, `noticesIssued` and `noticesAcknowledged`; `404 INCIDENT_NOT_FOUND`.
- `POST /platform/security-incidents/{id}/events` `{kind, occurredAt?, reference?, note?}` → `201` — record `EARLY_WARNING_SENT`, `NOTIFICATION_SENT`, `MITIGATION_AVAILABLE`, `FINAL_REPORT_SENT`, `NOTE` or `CLOSED`, with the authority's reference (≤120) and a note (≤2000). Decided under the incident's row lock and backed by a unique index, so twenty people recording one report at once record it once. `400 INCIDENT_EVENT_UNKNOWN`, `INCIDENT_EVENT_NOT_APPLICABLE` (not a stage of this kind; `TENANTS_NOTIFIED` is written only by issuing notices), `INCIDENT_EVENT_BEFORE_AWARE`, `INCIDENT_EVENT_IN_FUTURE` (five minutes' clock skew allowed), `INCIDENT_REFERENCE_INVALID`, `INCIDENT_NOTE_INVALID`, `INCIDENT_NOTE_REQUIRED`; `409 INCIDENT_STAGE_ALREADY_RECORDED`, `INCIDENT_FINAL_REPORT_TOO_EARLY` (before the notification, and for a vulnerability before a measure), `INCIDENT_STAGES_OUTSTANDING` (closing with a stage not done), `INCIDENT_CLOSED`. Nothing edits or deletes an incident or its timeline.
- `POST /platform/security-incidents/{id}/notices` `{message}` → `{issued, total, acknowledged}` — issues a notice to each business affected that has none, once per business, and records `TENANTS_NOTIFIED` the first time; `400 NOTICE_MESSAGE_INVALID`, `409 INCIDENT_CLOSED`.

### Public Storefront Config (`/storefront`)
- `GET /storefront/config?store=` — per-store storefront display config (e.g. whether to show prices). Carries `depositScheme` — the deposit return scheme in force where the store trades today, or absent (09.16) — so a shop and a till can say what the deposit is before the sale — and `businessName`, as `GET /storefront/stores` does.
- `GET /storefront/active` — whether the tenant may currently transact (the gateway's storefront suspension gate).
- `GET /storefront/stores` — active stores for the tenant (storefront's store switcher). Each carries `type` (`STORE`, `WAREHOUSE`, `DARK_STORE`) and `pickupOffered` — false at a **dark store**, a shop with no shop floor that fills online orders for delivery only ([ship-from-store and dark-store picking](../intent/ship-from-store-and-dark-store-picking.md)): the storefront lists it with the shops, offers no collection there, and the till never offers it. Each also carries `businessName` — the business the store belongs to, the tenant's legal name, else its name — which the storefront's accessibility statement names as the service provider (the European Accessibility Act's), never a store. It is always the business whose storefront is asked (the tenant the gateway resolved from `X-Storefront-Tenant`): another business's store id is `404 STORE_NOT_FOUND` on `config`, never its name. `GET /storefront/config` says the same three things for one store. Both carry `tillPhone` — what the store's till asks for the customer's phone ([a phone at the till](../intent/phone-at-the-till.md)); the till reads it here because a cashier may not read `/admin/stores`, and it names nobody.

**Business rules**
- Onboarding is designed as a single atomic call (tenant + first store) so a new signup needs no JWT refresh mid-flow.
- Platform-wide tenant visibility/suspension is restricted to `PLATFORM_ADMIN`.
- tenant-svc is the system of record for tenant/store/zone/staff state — it's a pure event publisher, never a consumer.

**Events**
- Consumes: none.
### Weighing instruments (`/admin/stores/{storeId}/weighing-instruments`)

The register of every instrument a store weighs for trade on (Weights and Measures Act 1985 s.11: using an instrument not passed as fit for use for trade, or one whose stamp a repair has broken, is an offence), and an append-only history of every verification, inspection and repair. Standing is derived on every read, never stored: **certified** means in service, latest history entry a pass, and not yet due again; otherwise `NEVER_VERIFIED`, `FAILED`, `REPAIRED_SINCE`, `OVERDUE`, `OUT_OF_SERVICE` or `RETIRED`. Reads need any staff role at the store (the till asks `?certified=true` before it sells by weight); writes are management-only.

- `GET /admin/stores/{storeId}/weighing-instruments?certified=` · `POST …` · `GET/PUT …/{id}` · `PATCH …/{id}/status` (`IN_SERVICE`, `OUT_OF_SERVICE`, `RETIRED`; retirement is final — a replaced or repaired-beyond-recognition scale is a new instrument). An instrument has an identifier unique in the store and a serial number unique in the tenant, a kind (`COUNTER`, `LABELLING`, `PLATFORM`, `HANGING`), capacity and scale interval as marked, the type-approval reference, and for a labelling scale the `labelScheme` JSON the till reads its barcodes with (`{prefixes, itemDigits, valueKind: PRICE|WEIGHT, valueDecimals, priceCheckDigit?}`), validated here once rather than trusted on every scan.
- `GET/POST …/{id}/verifications` — the history: `INITIAL`, `RE_VERIFICATION`, `INSPECTION` or `REPAIR`, with who did it, the certificate reference, whether it passed and when it is due again. A `REPAIR` is never a pass; a due date before the work, a date in the future, or an entry on a retired instrument is refused.

- Publishes: `TenantCreated`, `StoreCreated`, `ZoneCreated`, `StaffAssigned`, `TenantStatusChanged`, `StoreStatusChanged`, `UserRoleGranted`, `DunningNoticeIssued` (21.12: one per reminder and one for the suspension, on `storeql.tenant.dunning-notice-issued`, written with the step in one transaction — the invoice, what is left on it, the due date, the step, the day the service goes unless paid, the billing address and the pay link; notification-svc writes and sends it). Also `TrialNoticeIssued` (21.13: `ENDING` some days before a trial ends — the day, the plan and its price — and `ENDED` the day it ends, with the first invoice and the pay link; on `storeql.tenant.trial-notice-issued`, claimed once per stage in `trial_notices` with the outbox row).
